# Postmortem — 2026-11

> Entries from November 2026, newest date first. The index of month files is [docs/POSTMORTEM.md](../POSTMORTEM.md).

> Canvas paths in historical entries retain `design/`; the current canvas directories are `.doan/canvases/` (since 2026-10-28) and `.doan/ds/` for design-system canvases. File moves preserve canvas/frame/element IDs.

> Historical `app/e2e/` paths refer to the suite retired on 2026-10-27. Current runtime safety helpers live in `app/runtime/`; use focused probes under `.scratch/`, as specified in AGENTS.md.
> Some entries name code removed later: the app's comment UI (`useComments.tsx`, `CommentsSession.ts`, `CommentsPanel.tsx`, `CommentPins.ts` and most of `app/src/comments/` and `app/src/main/comments/`, with their tests) on 2026-11-01; the rest of comments on 2026-11-08 (`doan comments`, `doan comment resolve`, `cli/internal/comments/`, `cli/internal/repo/orphans.go`, `cli/internal/formats/fold*.go`, `app/src/comments/`, `app/src/formats/fold*.ts` and `schema/comment-event.v1.schema.json`; `app/src/main/comments/document.ts` and `identity.ts` now live in `app/src/main/document/` as `bindings.ts` and `identity.ts`); the separate Settings window (`settingsWindow.tsx`) and the `Sidebar.tsx` component on 2026-10-30; Settings > Connections (`ConnectionRows.tsx`, `connectionRowView.ts`, `setupBroker.ts`) on 2026-10-31. Other entries name files or symbols removed or renamed since (for example `ProjectSettingsDialog.tsx` and its test, which became the Project Settings page `ProjectSettingsPage.tsx` on 2026-11-03); `git log --diff-filter=D --oneline -1 -- <path>` finds when a path was removed. Their search commands and test pointers apply only to code that still exists.

### 2026-11-26 — A runner refused before spawning kept its session reserved, and a closing Codex server was reused

- **Area**: `app/src/main/conversation/service.ts` (`start`, `submit`, `openRunner`, `stopRuns`), `service.test.ts`.
- **Symptom**: a send or start whose runner refused before spawning (a Doan Claude connection with no pinned workspace answers `not_logged_in`; a Codex profile server without the app socket answers `unknown`) left the session reserved with no process whose exit would release it. Every later send of that conversation waited 10 s and was refused `busy`, and a resume start answered `session_in_use`, until the app restarted. Separately, a Codex server closed after a send that opened it failed (a recheck after the open), or closed by a sign-out (`stopRuns`), stayed reusable, so a quick resend went to the closing server and failed with `process_exited` instead of waiting for it.
- **Root cause**: the catch released the reservation only when the runner had not started opening, though `openRunner` knew the open failed before its spawn callback (`registerStop`) ran. The two close paths closed the runner without setting `live.closed`, which `previousExit` and `submit` read as "reusable" for a Codex server; `switchCodexServer` and `closeRemovalRunner` already set it.
- **Net**: caught by the 2026-11-26 audit (lifecycle reviewer). The service tests pinned only the spawned case ("held until exit"); every fake opener that threw did so without the spawn callback, and no test resent right after a failed open or a sign-out.
- **Prevention**: a failure path that ends a runner decides from spawn evidence (`registerStop`, kept as `live.cleanup`): no spawn releases the reservation at once with `not_spawned`; a spawned process keeps it until exit. Every path that closes a live runner without replacing the entry sets `live.closed` first. Regression tests: `service.test.ts` 'a runner refused before it spawned' and 'a Codex server closed between turns'. Search: `rg -n 'runner\??\.close\(\)' app/src/main/conversation/service.ts` (2026-11-26: the remaining unmarked closes are the unaccepted start, failed persistence, which `previousExit` checks separately, and quit).

### 2026-11-26 — Codex permission request ids repeated within one conversation across app-server processes

- **Area**: `app/src/main/conversation/transport.ts` (`codexPermission`), `app/src/main/conversation/summary.ts` (`pendingRequest`), `app/src/renderer/threadItems.ts` (permission card key); tests `dispatcher.test.ts`, `summary.test.ts`, `threadItems.test.ts` ("share an id").
- **Symptom**: after a new Codex app-server took a conversation (an app restart, any new server), its first approval was stored as `requestId: "number:0"` like an earlier turn's. The list's `waitingSince` took the earlier, answered request's time, the thread drew two cards with one React key, and a jump to the waiting request scrolled to the answered card.
- **Root cause**: the Codex `requestId` was built from the JSON-RPC wire id alone, and app-server numbers its server requests from 0 in every process; every history reader keyed requests by that id alone. Claude and ACP ids are random UUIDs, so only Codex repeated.
- **Net**: found by a static audit (2026-11-26 round 1); the recorded fixtures both start at `id: 0`, but no test had two turns.
- **Prevention**: an id a provider numbers per process or per connection is not unique across a conversation's history; qualify it with something that is (the turn) before storing it, and key history readers by turn as well, since stored histories keep the old ids. Search: `rg -n "requestId: \`|p\\$\\{event" app/src/main/conversation app/src/renderer/threadItems.ts`.
- **Fix**: d4b8ac0a3 — new Codex ids are `<native turn id>:<type>:<wire id>`; the summary reads only the folded turn's pending request and the card key carries the turn id.

### 2026-11-26 — A tool input holding U+0000 could fail the turn's whole history write

- **Area**: `app/src/main/conversation/transport.ts` (`activity`, `storableText`), `app/src/main/conversation/acp.ts` (`toolPath`, `acpToolSummary`); test `activityStore.test.ts`.
- **Symptom**: a Claude, Codex or ACP tool call whose input put U+0000 into the activity summary or path (a `Grep` pattern, a file path, an ACP title or location) produced an activity event the store refuses, and one refused event fails the turn's `appendAll`.
- **Root cause**: `callIdOf` already dropped a call id holding NUL for this reason, but the summary and path next to it were stored raw; permission text was safe because it goes through `clip`/`visibleText`.
- **Net**: found by a static audit (2026-11-26 round 1) and confirmed with a parser + store test; no provider run has shown a literal NUL yet.
- **Prevention**: every agent-written string that reaches a stored event goes through the same store bound as its neighbours: escape what the store refuses (`storableText`) or drop it, never store it raw, and escape no more than that, since activity lines are shown as written. Search: `rg -n 'storableText|includes\("\\\\0"\)' app/src/main/conversation/transport.ts app/src/main/conversation/acp.ts`.
- **Fix**: d983bce32, c224b671b — activity summaries escape U+0000 (`storableText`), and a path holding it is dropped.

### 2026-11-26 — ACP failures read as different codes while opening and while prompting

- **Area**: `app/src/main/conversation/acp.ts` (`acpFailureCode`), `app/src/main/conversation/acpRunner.ts` (open-phase refusal); test `acp.test.ts` ("ACP failure codes").
- **Symptom**: the same provider text could get a different failure code depending on the phase: "sign in" read as `not_logged_in` while opening but `unknown` during a prompt, so the thread's recovery copy depended on timing.
- **Root cause**: the open phase had its own regex set (`transportCode`) beside the shared `transportError` the prompt phase used, and they drifted (POSTMORTEM 2026-11-22's prevention was applied to one of them only).
- **Net**: found by a static audit (2026-11-26 round 1); no test compared the two phases.
- **Prevention**: one classifier per provider family, and a test runs every pinned message through both phases. A merge must not give a phase a new code for words no recorded provider message backs: merging the open phase into `transportError` first read an unrecorded "Too many requests" as `rate_limited`, which would have refused a Cursor conversation as limit-reached although only `limitReached` may (ARCHITECTURE §7 'Turn-end reads'). Search: `rg -n "acpFailureCode|transportError\(" app/src/main/conversation`.
- **Fix**: 65a5cf1cc, 6b9d90bf0 — `acpFailureCode` serves both phases for recorded and Doan's own text; for the rest, opening keeps its earlier reading (`session_unavailable`, `process_exited` for a timeout) and a prompt reads it through `transportError`.

### 2026-11-26 — An existing Claude session read as missing when a plain file sat in the profile's projects/

- **Area**: `app/src/main/conversation/prepare.ts` (`claudeSessionExists`), `io.ts` (`claudeTranscript`, now both through `claudeProjectFind`); tests `prepare.test.ts` 'claudeSessionExists', `io.test.ts` 'finds a Claude transcript past plain files'.
- **Symptom**: a `.DS_Store` (or any plain file) in `<profile>/projects/` listed before the session's project directory made the session lookup fail, so sends, resumes and transcript reads of an existing session failed `session_unavailable` or found no transcript, depending only on the directory listing order.
- **Root cause**: both loops refused a non-canonical entry with `canonicalDirectory(dir) !== dir` and ended the whole lookup on it. That check is meant to refuse a linked project directory, but `canonicalDirectory` also throws on any non-directory, so a harmless file failed the lookup the same way a link does.
- **Net**: caught by the 2026-11-26 audit (conversation core review, marked suspected) and confirmed by a red test with plain files beside the session's directory. Missed by the 2026-11-18 fix of the same lookup, whose tests built `projects/` with only directories.
- **Prevention**: a scan of an agent-owned directory separates "not what I look for" (skip) from "refused" (fail): `lstat` the entry and skip plain non-directories and entries gone since the listing before the link check. One walker (`claudeProjectFind`) serves every lookup of the same directory, so the rule cannot drift between copies. Tests of such a scan put a plain file and a link beside the target. Search: `rg -n "canonicalDirectory\(dir\) !== dir" app/src/main -g '!*.test.ts'` and confirm each loop skips plain files first.
- **Related**: entry 2026-11-18 'An existing Claude session read as missing when its transcript was large or held one bad byte'.

### 2026-11-26 — A returned draft could drop an attachment its conversation's history keeps

- **Area**: `app/src/main/conversation/drafts.ts` (the removed `anchored` and `attachment`), `store.ts` (`validAttachment`); test `drafts.test.ts` 'reads back an attachment whose name is longer than a message's text'.
- **Symptom**: an attachment whose name passed the history's check but was longer than a message's text limit made the drafts reader drop that queued item or returned draft (no realistic file name is that long, so no user report).
- **Root cause**: `drafts.ts` kept its own copies of `prompt.ts` `anchorTokens` and the store's attachment check, and its attachment copy bounded the name by `LIMITS.text` while the store allows any non-empty label. ARCHITECTURE §7 'drafts.json' requires reader and writer to share bounds; two copies drifted.
- **Net**: caught by the 2026-11-26 audit (maintainability review). Missed because each copy had its own tests and nothing compared them.
- **Prevention**: a record the history and the drafts file both store is checked by one exported validator (`validAttachment`, `anchorTokens`); do not copy a validator into another reader. Search: `rg -n "function attachment|function anchored|\\{\\{\\(\\[\\^\\{\\}\\]\\*\\)\\}\\}" app/src/main -g '!*.test.ts'`.

### 2026-11-25 — In forced colours the thread's sent bubble read as plain text and Jump to latest had no edge

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/styles.css` (`.conversation-thread .bubble`, `.jump-latest`, `.md-code`, `.md blockquote`, `.md-table`, `.msg-files .attach-chip`)
- **Symptom**: with `forced-colors: active` (light and dark), the sent-message bubble lost its fill and showed as right-aligned text, and Jump to latest sat on the reply text with no outline; code blocks, quotes, the table's outer edge and a sent file chip lost their shape the same way.
- **Root cause**: these shapes are drawn only by a background or a box shadow. Forced colours paint every author background as Canvas and drop box shadows, so nothing is left to draw the edge; no rule or test flags a fill-only or shadow-only shape that lacks a forced-colours rule, and DESIGN.md leaves forced colours unchecked by default.
- **Net**: caught by the lead's real-app check with CDP-emulated forced colours after thread motion merged; missed by unit tests (jsdom has no forced colours) and by every `/design-sync` and `/runtime-test` that left forced colours out.
- **Prevention**: a shape drawn only by `background` or `box-shadow` (above all one that floats over text) gets a `@media (forced-colors: active)` edge (`1px solid CanvasText`, `ButtonText` on a button) and a CSS-string test; check it with emulated forced colours. Search floating shadow-only shapes and compare with the forced-colours rules: `rg -n 'position: absolute[^}]*box-shadow: var\(--shadow' app/src/renderer -g '*.css'` and `rg -n 'forced-colors' app/src/renderer -g '*.css'`. Not yet covered on 2026-11-25: the canvas size bubble `.dims` (accent fill) and `.chip-note`.

### 2026-11-25 — A canvas card's meta line wrapped, so the width and the minute's new time changed the card's height

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/styles.css` (`.conversation-thread .card-head .cc-meta` and its items), `app/src/renderer/ConversationThread.test.tsx`.
- **Symptom**: at a 347 panel a card whose meta read ".doan/ds · 1 frame · Edited just now" was 211 high with the time on a second line and dropped to 195 once it read "Edited 2m ago"; a wrapped line could end with a "·" separator. PRODUCT §5 'Canvas cards' says neither meta nor the image changes a card's height.
- **Root cause**: the line was `flex-wrap: wrap` with `nowrap` items, a rule written to keep each item whole. A row of text that changes on its own (a relative time) under a wrapping container makes the height depend on the text and the width, and the design-sync measurement recorded the three-line 280 case as expected instead of holding it against the height rule.
- **Net**: caught by the person seeing a card jump in the thread. Missed by the static markup tests (no widths) and by the measurement, which read one moment of the time and never across a minute.
- **Prevention**: a line inside a fixed-height component whose text changes while shown (times, counts, statuses) stays on one line (`flex-wrap: nowrap`, `min-width: 0` items cut with an ellipsis, a stated order of what cuts first) and is pinned by a CSS rule test like `ConversationThread.test.tsx` 'the meta line never wraps'; a runtime probe of it measures the height before and after the text changes, at the narrowest panel width. Search: `rg -n "flex-wrap: wrap" app/src/renderer --glob '*.css'` and check whether any hit holds text that changes while shown.

### 2026-11-25 — Chromium scroll anchoring moved the followed thread and read as the person's scroll

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/ConversationThread.tsx` (the follow loop's `onScroll` and its own-scroll check), `app/src/renderer/styles.css` (`.conversation-thread .thread-scroll`).
- **Symptom**: in the isolated real app, when a permission card arrived under a reply that was still revealing, the reply was shown whole in the same render and the thread stopped following: Jump to latest appeared although the person never scrolled. Unit tests of the follow state passed.
- **Root cause**: Chromium's scroll anchoring (`overflow-anchor: auto`, the default) adjusts `scrollTop` itself when content above the anchor node changes height, so the scroller gets a scroll event the follow loop did not write. The loop treats any scroll it did not write as the person's and unpins. jsdom has no layout and no scroll anchoring, so no unit test can see it.
- **Net**: caught by the runtime probe of a permission card mid-reveal (panel and prototype window). Missed by every unit test, because the browser writes the scroll.
- **Prevention**: any scroller whose `scrollTop` the app drives and that tells its own writes from the person's sets `overflow-anchor: none` (grep `scrollTop = ` in `app/src/renderer` for such scrollers); `ConversationThread.motion.test.tsx` pins the rule on `.thread-scroll`; check a programmatically followed scroller in the real app with content growing above the anchor.

### 2026-11-24 — A chosen Cursor model made the fenced conversation fail to open

- **Area**: `app/src/main/conversation/acpRunner.ts` (the model choice after `session/new` and `session/load`), `app/src/main/agentAuth/fence.ts` (`AGENT_WRITES.cursor`).
- **Symptom**: a Cursor conversation with a chosen model (anything but the session's current one) never opened: cursor-agent answered `session/set_config_option` for the model with an internal error (`EPERM` on a `cli-config.json` temporary file), and the start failed. `acp --model` failed the same way at `session/new`. Auto, which sends no model request, worked, and mode requests succeed in the fence.
- **Root cause**: cursor-agent (2026.10.22 and 2026.11.22) saves a model choice in `cli-config.json`, the file the fence keeps closed because it holds the permission allow list a later conversation runs under. The fence's comment said Cursor runs without writing it, which was true of the probed run (Auto) but not of a run with a model choice.
- **Net**: caught by an isolated fenced CLI probe that set a model, while checking the worker folder fix. Missed by the fence probe (Auto only), by `acpRunner.test.ts` (the fake server accepts every `session/set_config_option`), and by earlier real-app Cursor runs, which used the default model.
- **Prevention**: a fence probe runs every `session/set_config_option` Doan can send (each `configId` and a value other than the current one) inside the fence, not only a default turn; Doan now writes the model to `acp-config.json` from outside, and `acpRunner.test.ts` 'gives Cursor the chosen model through acp-config.json' pins that no model request is sent. `rg -n "set_config_option" app/src/main --glob '!*.test.ts'`.

### 2026-11-24 — Cursor conversations ended at once on a profile with a short HOME path, as a bare process_exited

- **Area**: `app/src/main/agentAuth/fence.ts` (`cursorWorkerDir`), `app/src/main/conversation/prepare.ts` (creating the folder), `app/src/main/conversation/acpRunner.ts` (`stderrTail`, the early-exit log line).
- **Symptom**: every Cursor conversation start and model list read on a profile copied under `/private/tmp` ended about 0.5 s in with `process_exited`, before `initialize` answered, while `cursor-agent status` and `--version` in the same fence succeeded. The app logged nothing; only a spawn hook showed stderr: `EPERM: operation not permitted, mkdir '<profile>/.cursor/projects/<name>-<hash>'`.
- **Root cause**: cursor-agent (2026.10.22 and 2026.11.22) creates its local worker's socket folder when `acp` starts and exits when it cannot. Its path depends on the length of HOME: `.cursor/projects/<slug>`, else `.cursor/<slug>` once that is over 83 characters, else `$TMPDIR/.cursor`, cut and hashed past 91. Only a short HOME puts it inside `.cursor`, which the fence closes; in the installed app that is a user name of 7 characters or fewer. The fence probes and the kept dev profile used long paths, where the folder lands outside `.cursor`, so the allowlist looked complete. `acpRunner.ts` read stderr only for Antigravity sign-in links, so the cause never reached a log.
- **Net**: caught by a PoC spawn hook that recorded stderr, on a temporary profile with a short path. Missed by the fence probe and every real-app check on the long kept-profile path, and by `acpRunner.test.ts`, whose fake server never writes stderr before exiting.
- **Prevention**: a fence probe for a provider runs with a short profile path as well as a long one (an installed app's `/Users/<short name>/Library/Application Support/doan/...` length and a short temporary path); an ACP server that exits before it is ready now logs a bounded, redacted stderr tail (`stderrTail`), pinned by 'logs a redacted stderr tail when an ACP server exits before it is ready'. Other fenced processes may still lose their stderr on an early exit (Codex's app server, Claude's run, usage reads, MCP connector commands); check them before relying on their logs. `rg -n "fenceArgs\(|profileFencedCommand\(" app/src/main --glob '!*.test.ts'`.

### 2026-11-23 — An Overview row waiting for a permission answer scrolled to the request but left focus on the row

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/ProjectOverview.tsx` (the entry's scroll effect); `App.tsx` `openOverviewRow` and `askConversation`, and the Overview's waiting-conversation toast, which all enter through `OverviewRun.request(..., "permission")`.
- **Symptom**: pressing a Conversations row (or a toast's Open) for a project conversation waiting on a permission request scrolled the thread to the card, but focus stayed on the row; a waiting question's entry focused its first option as it should.
- **Root cause**: the entry-focus rule is implemented once per surface: the canvas panel's arrival effect and the Overview's scroll effect. When questions came in, PRODUCT gave both surfaces one rule (a waiting entry focuses the newest request's first choice), but the Overview effect gained focus only for a question and kept a comment saying a permission entry "only scrolls here", which read as an intended rule. The canvas panel already focused both kinds, so nothing looked missing there.
- **Net**: a post-merge real-app check with main's list reply patched to a waiting status caught it. Unit tests missed it: the Overview tests render with `renderToStaticMarkup`, which runs no effects, so no test drove the Overview's entry effect for either kind.
- **Prevention**: an entry that scrolls to a waiting request is tested per surface for both a permission request and a question, with effects running (`ProjectOverview.entryFocus.test.tsx` runs hooks as plain state). A comment that restricts a PRODUCT rule to one kind names the PRODUCT section that says so. Search: `rg -n "scrollToItem\(|focusItem\(" app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'`; the canvas panel's scroll-request effect scrolls without focus on purpose, since its arrival effect focuses.

### 2026-11-23 — Shift+Enter in a composer at its maximum height left the new line out of view

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/chipField.ts` (`revealCaret`, `caretRect`); every composer (Home, the Overview's docked composer, the canvas conversation panel, the prototype window's panel).
- **Symptom**: with the field at its eight-row cap, Shift+Enter added a line but the field did not scroll, so the caret sat below the visible rows; typing scrolled as expected.
- **Root cause**: Chromium scrolls a contenteditable to the caret only after its own edits. Shift+Enter, paste, chips and undo are the field's edits: it redraws (`replaceChildren`) and places the caret with `setBaseAndExtent`, and neither scrolls. The first fix measured the caret with a collapsed `Range.getClientRects()`, which is empty on an empty line (measured in Electron 45), so a Shift+Enter at the end of a line with lines below (a new empty line) still did not scroll, and with a trailing line break the `<br>` fallback scrolled to the end instead.
- **Net**: reported by the person; the field's Electron DOM tests had no field with a height cap. The first regression test only pressed Shift+Enter at the text's end, so the empty-line case was caught by code review, not by the test.
- **Prevention**: an edit that redraws a scrollable editable and places the caret by code must scroll to it itself, measured on a caret that may sit on an empty line (use the line break after it, or the drawn `<br>`); test it at the cap both at the end and mid-text (`chipField.dom.test.ts` 'maximum height'). Find other programmatic caret placements: `rg -n "setBaseAndExtent|selectAllChildren|collapseToEnd" app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'`. Not verified: `focusFieldEnd` (a field focused at its end by selector) and `ChipField.focus(true)` place the caret without scrolling.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-23, `focusFieldEnd` and `ChipField.focus(true)` on the ProseMirror field)**: a long draft at the eight-row cap restored by a conversation switch or a preset pick showed the caret below the visible rows, because the field already had focus and a caret code placed in a focused field moved no scroll (focusing an unfocused field was fine: Chromium shows the selection of a field it focuses, so a test that blurs first passes on the old field too); the caret placed by code now scrolls into view, also after an unfocused field takes focus (it shows its previous caret first, so the reveal comes last); `chipField.dom.test.ts` 'a long draft focused at its end' starts from a focused field and from an unfocused one with the caret at the start, at scrollTop 0, and fails on the old field; `rg -n "focusFieldEnd|composer-input.*\.focus\(|focus\(\"end\"\)" app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'`.

### 2026-11-23 — A rounded playing frame showed the canvas body's colour in its corners

- **Area**: `app/src/main/prototypeScript.ts` and `cli/internal/export/viewer/isolate.js` (`isolate`, its adopted sheet), the generated `viewerApp.js`; the prototype window, the exported prototype file and the canvas file's player.
- **Symptom**: a frame with `border-radius` and its own background, on a dark `body`, showed dark corners when played in Fit (and in the exported files); on the board the same corners looked right, because the board shows the body around every frame anyway.
- **Root cause**: isolating in place keeps the whole document and the sheet only undid the ancestors' *layout* (margins, positioning, size limits, `filter`); their *paint* (background, box-shadow, outline) stayed, and the root background propagates to the iframe's whole viewport. Isolation was framed as "make the frame fill the viewport", so nothing compared what the frame covers with what the ancestors paint behind it.
- **Net**: a user screenshot caught it. Missed by the fake-DOM tests (they read attributes and sheet text, never paint) and by the real-DOM tests, which checked layout and links only. New real-DOM tests read the computed paint of the root, the body and a wrapper for opaque, transparent, half-transparent and image-only frames in both isolation copies, and the player's double run. A probe that samples the corner pixel in the app must keep the iframe away from the content panel's own ~20 px corner (enlarge the window, or use a radius well above 20 px): with the window fitted to the mark the panel clips the very pixels being measured.
- **Prevention**: any in-place isolation (a frame shown alone inside its document) decides ancestor paint as well as ancestor layout, and a real-DOM test reads `backgroundColor`/`backgroundImage`/`boxShadow` of `html`, `body` and a wrapper after isolation. Isolation copies are found with `rg -n "data-doan-proto-path" app/src cli/internal --glob '!*.test.ts'`; the board and thumbnail scripts are not isolation and keep ancestor paint by design.

### 2026-11-23 — Codex's Default probe started every configured MCP server once more

- **Area**: `app/src/main/conversation/runners.ts` (`openCodex`, the ephemeral `thread/start` that reads Ask first's policies), `app/src/main/conversation/permissionMode.ts`
- **Symptom**: each doan-owned Codex server opened a second, throwaway thread only to read Ask first's approval and sandbox, and that thread started every MCP server in the profile and the project layers, so each open paid for an extra connection to every server.
- **Root cause**: an ephemeral `thread/start` builds a full session, MCP servers included; "ephemeral" only means no rollout. A per-thread `config` override is merged into the config layers, so the obvious `config: { mcp_servers: {} }` disables nothing, and an override for a name the config lacks fails `thread/start` with "invalid transport" (codex-cli 0.171.0, PoC with an isolated `CODEX_HOME`). Only `mcp_servers.<name>.enabled = false` for each name `config/read` reports turns them off, without changing the reported policies.
- **Net**: the startup wait filtered statuses by thread, so the extra starts were invisible to users and to tests; the fake app-server never started servers. It was caught as a BACKLOG known defect, and only a fake MCP server that logs its launches (taking the log path as an argument, since Codex does not pass the parent environment to MCP servers) showed which override worked.
- **Prevention**: when a Codex config override is meant to clear or turn off a table, prove it with a launch- or effect-logging fake against the real binary, not by the reply alone; a table override merges. Search for Codex overrides with `rg -n '"-c"|config: \{' app/src/main --glob '!*.test.ts'`. `runners.test.ts` pins the probe's `config` against the names `config/read` returns.
- **Fix**: 891c2a021 — `config/read` runs first and the probe turns off each server it names.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-23, the lead's check of that fix)**: a plugin's MCP servers (its `.mcp.json`) are not in `config/read`'s `mcp_servers`, so the probe still started them, and naming one under `mcp_servers` fails `thread/start` ("invalid transport"); the probe now also sets `plugins."<plugin@marketplace>".enabled = false` for every key `config/read` reports under `plugins`, and a fake MCP server is checked once from config and once from a locally installed plugin, since config/read lists only the first; `rg -n 'mcp_servers|config\.plugins|"plugins"' app/src/main --glob '!*.test.ts' --glob '!**/fixtures/**'`.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-23, the lead's signed-in check of the plugin fix)**: a signed-in account's probe thread still started the built-in `codex_apps` connection (ChatGPT apps), a remote MCP that `config/read` names under neither `mcp_servers` nor `plugins`, so the isolated, signed-out PoCs never saw it; the probe now always sends `features.apps = false`, and a probe change is checked once in a temporary `CODEX_HOME` holding a copy of a real sign-in, reading the probe thread's `mcpServer/startupStatus/updated` notices, not only child processes; `rg -n 'features|codex_apps' app/src/main --glob '!*.test.ts' --glob '!**/fixtures/**'`.

### 2026-11-23 — An export viewer text drag drew no range when the canvas focus reached the page after the moves

- **Area**: the real-DOM fixture in `app/src/main/exportViewer.test.ts` ("a text range's rects lie on the canvas text they cover" and "shell %s releases the tool keys during text drag"); the page's gate in `cli/internal/export/viewer/inspectMessage.js` (`inspect-text`).
- **Symptom**: on a develop push run (CI shard 1/3) the test pressed in the canvas, moved across the span in six steps and timed out after 30 s waiting for `.text-rect` with the button held. The same commit passed on the pull request run and on rerun; 90 local runs of the test alone (30 idle, 30 under CPU load, 30 under load with hardware acceleration off) and 15 runs of its whole block under load never hit it.
- **Root cause**: the page accepts an `inspect-text` with `dragging: true` only while `document.activeElement` is the canvas iframe, so a canvas cannot hold the page in a drag on its own. The press moves focus into the canvas across processes, and the canvas's messages arrive on another channel: when the focus lands after the last move, every dragging message is dropped, and with no later move nothing redraws the range until release. The tool-key drag tests had the same gap the other way round: their expected keys (`true,true,true`, the page handles them) are also what a page that never entered the drag gives, so with a late focus they passed without testing a drag. The text-range test also sent a raw `mouse.move`/`mouse.down` right after ⇧0 without `pointAt`, which the 2026-11-21 entry 'Export viewer tests clicked the canvas while the stage was still moving' guards against; its search matched only `click(` and `hover(` on canvas locators.
- **Net**: CI caught it once. Missed by the original PR's runs and by local loops: the focus normally reaches the page within the first move. Holding the page's view of the canvas focus back by 500 ms after the press reproduced the timeout on 10 of 10 runs, and the fixed steps passed 10 of 10. With the same delay the `pointerup` and `blur` tool-key tests passed while the page had never held the keys; after the fix the page held them before each end event, `focus` included. The tool-key tests now also assert the held keys (`false,false,false`) before the end event, and with the late focus and no wait all three fail on that assertion.
- **Prevention**: a test that holds the button in the canvas and waits for drag state the page draws (a range, `blocks()`) waits for `document.activeElement` in the page to be the canvas before the moves, and presses after a stage move through `pointAt`. When the expected end state is also what the page shows without a drag, the test asserts the drag's own state before the end as well. `rg -n 'mouse\.down\(\)' app/src/main/exportViewer.test.ts`, then check each held press that is followed by `mouse.move`.
- **Fix**: 912264517, 7df0437d3 — `pointAt` before the press and a wait for the canvas focus before the moves.

### 2026-11-23 — The app sometimes never quit after a canvas write, its reload and a canvas switch

- **Area**: `app/src/main/document/bindings.ts` (`clear`), `windowDebugger.ts` (`detach`, `connect`), `readState.ts` (`tryMark`).
- **Symptom**: an isolated app did not quit within 5 s, and stayed up past 30 s, after a canvas write, the reload it caused and a canvas switch. The main window's `close` fired, but `destroyed`, `will-quit` and `quit` never came and the main process spun at full CPU.
- **Root cause**: while the main window closes, the read-state proof reads the main frame on every document change, and `tryMark` runs one more pass for a change during a pass. The real debugger detach (`target closed`) cleared the bindings and woke a pass; that pass called `connect()`, whose `attach` to the closing contents succeeded and detached again synchronously; that detach cleared the already empty bindings and notified anyway, which set `markAgain`; the command then failed and the pass ended, and the next pass did the same. Every step was a microtask, so the event loop never ran the native close. Whether it hung depended on whether a pass was still in flight at the first detach (a pass started fresh reads the stale main frame id and stops), which made it look intermittent and tied to one flow.
- **Net**: a main-process inspector `Debugger.pause` during the hang showed `tryMark → shown → mainFrame → connect` in every sample, and temporary bundle logs showed the attach/detach/`documentsChanged` cycle. The 2026-11-11 build hung 12/17 runs of the original probe flow with either Electron; current develop did not hang in 36 runs before the fix, by timing alone. Unit tests missed it because no fake debugger detached from inside `attach`. `windowDebugger.test.ts` now does, and fails without the fix. With the fix the same old build quit 10/10 and the current build 15/15.
- **Prevention**: a listener that triggers work which can emit the same event must not see an event for a change that did not happen; notify only on a real change. Look for other "one more pass" loops whose pass can wake itself: `rg -n 'Again = true|while \(\w*[aA]gain\)' app/src --glob '!*.test.*'`. Also check every debugger attach for a synchronous `detach` from a closing target: `rg -n 'on\("detach"|\.attach\(' app/src/main --glob '!*.test.*'`. `prototypeWindow.ts` and `opaqueFallback.ts` attach but subscribe to no `detach`, so they cannot loop this way. A quit probe asserts `will-quit` and exit, not only that `close` fired.
- **Fix**: f891cb1f3 — `DocumentBindings.clear()` notifies only when it removed something.

### 2026-11-23 — An ACP thread row ran with a completed command's words until the indicator settled on the next tool

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/turnStatus.ts` (`activityRun`, `settle`), `ConversationThread.tsx` (the thread's last row from `settled.shown` and the latest status).
- **Symptom**: when Antigravity issued a command and a file read back to back, the command's completion was followed at once by the read's stage, and the thread's last row spun with "Running a command" for the indicator's 300 ms settle, although that command had already completed and its step read "Ran a command".
- **Root cause**: an ACP agent writes a tool's activity only once the tool completed, so the row's words come from the indicator's shown status, which settle holds on the old stage for 300 ms. The earlier guard for a completed tool asked only whether both the shown and the latest status were a stage's tool. That covers a completion followed by the general stage, but a completion followed by another tool's stage still passes: both are stage tools, only different ones.
- **Net**: caught by a person in the real app with Antigravity. The unit tests had the single-tool case (completion, then the general stage) and Copilot's parallel commands (the same words before and after), but no case where the next stage names a different tool. A real-app probe reproduced it on the pre-fix build in 3 of 3 turns only when the prompt asked for both tool calls in one response; with a model step between the tools, the general stage came first and the defect did not show.
- **Prevention**: a rule that combines a settled (delayed) status with the latest one compares the two by identity (`statusKey`), not by kind. A test for such a rule covers the latest status changing to another value of the same kind while the shown one is held. Grep: `grep -rn "settled\.shown\|held\.shown\|fromStage(" app/src/renderer`.
- **Fix**: 813f09778 — an ACP row runs only while the shown and the latest status are the same stage's tool.

### 2026-11-23 — The inspector failed on every read, Retry included, after the canvas agent notice appeared

- **Area**: `app/src/main/windowDebugger.ts` (`Target.attachedToTarget` setup), `windowDebugger.test.ts`.
- **Symptom**: the inspector showed its failure line and Retry kept failing until the canvas was reloaded. Every `inspect:get` answered `unavailable` because the shown canvas had no proof; read-state's seen proof was lost the same way.
- **Root cause**: the window debugger's auto-attach reports every target of the main window, not only canvas iframes. When the canvas agent notice mounted, its dot field started a dedicated Worker; the debugger sent it `Network.enable` and `Page.enable`, Chromium answered "'Page.enable' wasn't found" (a worker has no Page domain), and the catch, meant for a frame whose events might be missed, cleared every document binding. A cleared proof comes back only with that frame's next commit.
- **Net**: caught by an isolated built-app probe that started the built dot field worker and recorded `inspect:get` replies. Missed by `windowDebugger.test.ts`, whose fake targets were all iframes and whose fake debugger failed a method for every session, and by every real-app inspector probe, which never had an agent notice on screen.
- **Prevention**: send setup commands only to the target types that need them (`targetInfo.type`), and never let one target's failure clear state other targets own; test an auto-attach handler with a non-frame target whose commands fail. `prototypeWindow.ts` already sets up only iframes and swallows a failure. `rg -n 'attachedToTarget|setAutoAttach' app/src/main -g '!*.test.*'`.

### 2026-11-23 — Send now on a Codex server that had just died said the message stays queued for a turn that was ending

- **Area**: `app/src/main/conversation/runners.ts` (`openCodex` `steer`, `transportDied`), `process.ts` (`ownProtocolProcess`), `service.ts` (`sendNow`, `dropSteers`), the refusal notice in `DockedComposer.tsx` (`QueueRows`, `turnRuns`); test in `runners.test.ts`.
- **Symptom**: a Send now pressed after the Codex server exited but before its turn's `turn.failed` was refused while the turn still read as running, so the queue row showed "Couldn’t send now. It stays queued." until the end arrived. BACKLOG had suspected a steer already in flight at the exit; that one was ordered correctly.
- **Root cause**: after the leader exits, `ownProtocolProcess` marks the process `ending` at once but lets `dispatcher.end()` emit the turn's end only after the output drain (up to 500 ms; see the 2026-11-16 entry "Process runners settled on the exit event and dropped output still in the pipe"). The runner's `steer` refused on `ending()` alone, inside that window. The renderer shows the refusal only for a running turn and relies on main publishing a turn's end before answering its refusals; that held for an in-flight steer (`end()` rejects pending requests and emits `turn.failed` in one call, and the rejection handlers run after) but not for a refusal the runner made itself during the drain.
- **Net**: found by reading the code for the BACKLOG item; a fake Codex that exits on the steer while a descendant that ignores SIGTERM holds its stdout reproduced it (the first fake let the stop kill the descendant before its handler was installed, which closed the pipe at once and passed). Missed by the steer tests, whose fakes ended turns with protocol frames and never left a gap between the exit and the end.
- **Prevention**: a runner call that refuses because its process is going waits for the turn's end (or emits it) before refusing, when the refusal's wording depends on whether the turn runs. Drive such calls in a fake whose leader exits while its output stays open. Search: `rg -n "ending\(\)" app/src/main/conversation -g '!*.test.ts'` — the permission and question answers that return false or `stale` on `ending()` (Codex `answer`, ACP `answer`/`answerQuestion`) refuse in the same window and are not checked.
- **Fix**: 34fb35dc1 — the Codex `steer` waits for `transportDied` before refusing on a dying server.

### 2026-11-23 — A frame the board offered for Play was reported missing by the run

- **Area**: `app/src/main/prototypeScript.ts` (injected run script), `cli/internal/export/viewer/isolate.js`; tests in `app/src/main/prototypeScript.test.ts` and `app/src/main/exportViewer.test.ts`.
- **Symptom**: a canvas that made its frames a little after `load` (after a `fetch`, or in a timer) showed them on the board with Play on, yet the prototype window said "Frame unavailable" and the export player said the frame was not in the file.
- **Root cause**: two judges of the same question ran at different times. The board's scripts (`canvasScript.ts`, `measure.js`) follow the document as it changes and re-send the layout, while the run scripts looked once, after the `load` handlers, and never again. Nothing compared when each judge looks, only what it accepts (the mark grammar and the ID rules are pinned between them, the moment was not).
- **Net**: known from use and listed in BACKLOG; no test drove a document that changes after `load`, because the fake documents in both test files flushed every timer at once and had no MutationObserver. Fixed by a bounded wait (2 s, the same literal in both scripts, pinned by a test) and by fakes whose timers keep their delay and whose observer delivers recorded changes.
- **Prevention**: when the board offers an action from the live layout, every script that acts on it must accept the same document states, or bound the gap and document it (ARCHITECTURE §1 convention 14). Drive such scripts in tests with a document that changes after `load`. Search: `rg -n 'addEventListener\("load"' app/src/main/*Script.ts cli/internal/export/viewer/*.js` and check that each hit either keeps watching the document or has a stated bound.

### 2026-11-23 — Send now came back on a flushed queue's rows for about 10 ms as the next turn started

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/conversationStore.ts` (`event`, `syncQueues`, `steerPhaseOf`), the queue rows in `ProjectOverview.tsx` and `CanvasConversationPanel.tsx`
- **Symptom**: in a project conversation, a queue going out after its turn's judgement hid Send now on its row (holding), then showed it again for about 10 ms when the flushed turn started, before the row left. A real Codex probe saw it in the DOM in every cycle and once in a painted frame.
- **Root cause**: the row and its button read two sources that update at different times. The rows drop only in `syncQueues`, which the runs call after a list read; the button reads `steerPhaseOf` on every render, and the flushed turn's `turn.started` event reaches the store before the list read, so the phase turned running while the rule that drops the rows had not run yet. The comment on `syncQueues` said the rows stay "until the next turn opens", which was true only once a list read followed.
- **Net**: caught by a real-app probe sampling the DOM on every mutation (rAF samples missed two of three cycles). Missed by the store tests, which always applied a list read right after the events, so the state between an event and the next read was never asserted.
- **Prevention**: a rule that hides or drops something on state from both the list and the thread runs on the event that changes the thread side too, not only on a list read; test the state after the event and before the read. Search: `rg -n "steerPhaseOf\(|syncQueues\(\)" app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'` — the queue rows' Send now in the Overview and the canvas panel read the store's rows, so `event`'s sync on `turn.started` covers both; a thread read (`readThread`) that brings the start does not sync and waits for the list read.
- **Fix**: e2443f6bd — the store runs `syncQueues` when a `turn.started` event merges.

### 2026-11-23 — The export viewer cut the selection line of a frame as wide as the canvas

- **Area**: `cli/internal/export/viewer/shell.css` (`.vw-overlay`, `.vw-overlay .mark`, `#inspect-range`); test in `app/src/main/exportViewer.test.ts`.
- **Symptom**: in an exported file, selecting a frame that fills the canvas's width drew only part of its 1px outline, and which part depended on zoom, display density and engine: Playwright WebKit at 68% drew only the top and bottom lines, Chromium drew one side at some zooms and neither at others, and a frame at the canvas top lost its top line too. The computed border was the same in every case. The app's board drew all four lines.
- **Root cause**: the mark's line lies outside the element (content-box with a negative margin of the line's width), but the overlay that holds the marks clipped to the canvas box (`inset: 0; overflow: clip`). For an element on the canvas edge the whole line falls outside the clip box. Subpixel rounding of the scaled 1/scale line then let a sliver through on some edges, so the defect looked like an engine rendering bug rather than a clip. The app does not clip its overlay to the canvas, so its marks never showed it.
- **Net**: caught by the user in real Safari and Chromium. Missed by every viewer test and probe, whose selected frames sat inside the canvas with margin, and by computed-style checks, which read the border, not the clip. Now a real-DOM test checks that the mark of a canvas-wide frame lies inside the overlay's box, and another that text range rects stay on the canvas text after the overlay origin moved.
- **Prevention**: a line drawn outside a box (an outline, an outer border by negative margin, an outer shadow) needs its clip container to extend past the content by at least the line; check every `overflow: clip|hidden` ancestor of a mark layer with an element at the content's edge, not only an element in the middle. WebKit has no `overflow-clip-margin`, so grow the clip box instead. `grep -n "overflow: \(clip\|hidden\)" cli/internal/export/viewer/*.css app/src/renderer/styles.css` lists the clip containers; the prototype window's `.pw-stage` holds no marks.
- **Fix**: c408c7abd, the overlay's clip box grows by the mark line on every side.

### 2026-11-22 — An agent's render copies in the canvas folder blocked its own conversation again and again

- **Area**: `cli/internal/cli/skill.md` (canvas convention 8); what it guards: `app/src/renderer/App.tsx` (`duplicated`, `DUPLICATE_CANVAS_TEXT`), `app/src/main/designChange.ts` (`classifyDesignChange`), `app/src/formats/canvasPath.ts` (`canvasKind`).
- **Symptom**: in an app conversation on a canvas, the composer showed "Another canvas has the same ID, so this conversation can't continue..." and cleared, over and over. The agent was checking an animation: 21 times it ran `sed "s#</style>#<css></style>#" .doan/canvases/landing.html > .doan/canvases/zz-motion-test.html`, took a headless Chrome screenshot of the copy over `file://`, then removed it.
- **Root cause**: Doan has no render or screenshot command, so agents improvise one, and the guide said only "keep ids stable and unique". A copy keeps the original's `doan:canvas` meta and, written into a canvas directory, is listed the moment it lands, so two rows share the id until it is removed: the composer blocks and a playing prototype's target is lost. The copy most likely sat beside the original because the canvas links `../assets/*.css` relatively and a copy elsewhere renders unstyled.
- **Net**: caught by the person watching the composer flicker; the session log showed the copies. Missed by `doan check`, which the agent ran only after deleting the copies, and by every eval scenario: none asks an agent to look at its own render. A dot-prefixed copy would not have been listed, but every write of a non-canvas name in a canvas directory bumps the folder's served revision and reloads every open canvas of the project.
- **Prevention**: guide rule 8 puts a temporary copy outside `.doan/` with `<base href="file://<absolute canvas folder>/">` first in `<head>` (a `<base>` after the links leaves them resolving from the copy's folder), pinned by `TestSkillGuideKeepsCanvasCopiesOutOfCanvasFolders`; eval scenario W13 asks for a check of a rendered animation and fails when a canvas other than the target is listed in a canvas folder at any moment, even one removed again (the runner watches the folders); in-place edit temp names pass it, and its judge scores a dot-prefixed copy or a copy elsewhere in `.doan/` as partial. When guide or preset text asks an agent to see its result (the `animate` preset's "verified only by watching the motion run" is one), it relies on rule 8 for where copies go. Search: `rg -n -i 'watching|screenshot|render(ed)? |verified only' cli/internal/designskill/presets cli/internal/cli/skill.md`.

### 2026-11-22 — Export refused custom property url()s whose other user was a rule for an element the canvas never had

- **Area**: `cli/internal/canvas/custom.go` (`CustomUsers`), `css.go` (`CustomUses`, `selectorNeeds`), `canvas.go` (`Elements`, `elementsOf`), `cli/internal/check/check.go` (`ambiguousURLs`), `cli/internal/export/customurl.go`.
- **Symptom**: `doan check` warned `ambiguous_custom_url` and `doan export` refused a canvas the app showed correctly, because a shared stylesheet in another folder used the property in a rule (`.btn{mask:var(--i)}`) for an element the canvas does not contain. Follows the 2026-11-03 entry "Export refused icons the app showed, set as url() in a custom property".
- **Root cause**: users were recorded by property name only, so every `var()` in every reached stylesheet counted, whether or not any element could match its rule. The first fix (count a rule only when its rightmost compound's class, id and type names exist in the canvas) then had three soundness gaps, each of which let export embed one file where the app shows another: the lexer drops comments and its token positions read `:/**/root` and `./**/card` as a descendant combinator plus a type selector; a `contenteditable` element or a `-webkit-user-modify` declaration lets a person type a new `<div>` with no script; and an escaped property name (`-webkit-user-modif\79`) is split by the lexer so the user-modify check never saw it.
- **Net**: caught by the BACKLOG known-defect entry; the three gaps were caught by an independent Codex review that reproduced each in Chromium 156 with real keyboard input. Missed by the first test table, which listed selector shapes and script markers but no tokenizer artifacts (comments, escapes) and no script-free way to create elements.
- **Prevention**: any static "this rule never applies" judgment must (1) give up on selectors whose text holds a comment or an escape instead of trusting token adjacency, and (2) treat every script-free DOM change as dynamic: `<script>`, `on*` attributes, `javascript:` URLs, SMIL `set`/`animate` of `class` or `id`, `contenteditable`, and `user-modify` (escaped names included); add a test case per path before trusting a new narrowing, and have it reviewed by a second model family with a real browser. Search: `rg -n 'Elements\.Match|elementsOf\(|selectorNeeds\(|\.applies\(' cli/internal --glob '!*_test.go'`.

### 2026-11-22 — Sent anchor chips inserted line breaks into copied text

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/styles.css`, `ConversationThread.copy.test.tsx`.
- **Symptom**: selecting and copying `Align {{1}} - stays` from a sent bubble put the chip label on its own line; list markers and depth spaces survived.
- **Root cause**: the chip was inline-flex, but its label became a flex item, which Chromium serialized with block boundaries. The existing markup-based copy test modeled tags rather than computed display and therefore treated the label as inline.
- **Net**: the BACKLOG defect reproduced in the app's Electron with the actual component markup and CSS for both button and span chips. The regression failed before the CSS change; a focused real-app copy into a textarea then matched plain text and nested-list text, including depth spaces and blank lines.
- **Prevention**: test browser selection with the shipped CSS when selectable text contains flex or grid children, and verify the clipboard by pasting it. Composer chips have their own copy serializer; reply-chip selection is outside this fix. Search: `rg -n 'inline-flex|inline-grid|onCopy' app/src/renderer/styles.css app/src/renderer/chipField.ts`.

### 2026-11-22 — Nested frame commit ids accumulated until the child session detached

- **Area**: `app/src/main/windowDebugger.ts`, `windowDebugger.test.ts`.
- **Symptom**: creating and removing nested srcdoc iframes kept growing the child session's committed-frame set while its canvas stayed open.
- **Root cause**: the set distinguished nested commits from the session root, but only target-session teardown cleared it. Frame removal invalidated document evidence without retiring that auxiliary id. Unlike the OOPIF ownership pitfall recorded on 2026-10-23, the live owner stayed correct while removed nested ids accumulated.
- **Net**: the BACKLOG defect reproduced with 100 commit/remove cycles; the existing ownership and late-event tests did not inspect retained nested ids. The new regression observes the set without exposing a production diagnostics API and covers explicit and unspecified non-swap removals, retained swaps and the root.
- **Prevention**: test the retained state after repeated child-resource creation/removal while its parent stays alive. Keep the root until session teardown and preserve the former-session event fence when retiring nested ids. Search: `rg -n 'sessionCommits|frameDetached|sessionFrames|frameOwners' app/src/main/windowDebugger.ts`.

### 2026-11-22 — A Cursor plan limit read as a completed turn, then as session_unavailable

- **Area**: `app/src/main/conversation/acpRunner.ts` (the open phase's refusal code), `app/src/main/agentAuth/usageService.ts` `withTurnUsage`, `app/src/main/agentAuth/usageRead.ts`.
- **Symptom**: a Cursor conversation whose first turn met the free plan's limit showed a done turn with the reply "Upgrade your plan to continue", and the next send was refused as `session_unavailable` ("Couldn't send your message. Try again."), which retrying never fixed.
- **Root cause**: cursor-agent (2026.10.22-27dcf5f) answers a turn its plan limit stops with `stopReason: "end_turn"` and the limit as ordinary reply text, and saves no session for it, so the next `session/load` answers "Session not found". ACP has no field for a usage limit, so every Doan signal it gives (a done turn, then a missing session) names the wrong cause; only the account's usage (`/api/usage-summary` `autoPercentUsed: 100` at the limit) tells it.
- **Net**: caught by the person in the real app and diagnosed with an ACP probe (store.db missing for the limited turn); the fake ACP server and the recorded Cursor fixture only had model-run turns, so no test held a limit. A first fix attempt was nearly dropped because the account had already reset when probed; burning a free account to its limit reproduced it.
- **Prevention**: when an ACP provider refuses or loses a session, ask what account state could cause it before mapping it to `session_unavailable`, and pin each case with a recorded provider answer in the fake server. Probing a limit needs the account at its limit at probe time (a free account can be used up on purpose); record the usage reading at that moment. Copilot and Antigravity limits over ACP are not checked. Search: `rg -n "not found|transportCode\(|limitReached" app/src/main/conversation/acpRunner.ts app/src/main/conversation/acp.ts`.
- **Fix**: 56754437c — a Doan-account Cursor run reads its usage when `session/load` answers "not found" and refuses as `rate_limited` when a window is at 100%.

### 2026-11-22 — An Antigravity tool command outlived Stop in a process group of its own

- **Area**: `app/src/main/conversation/process.ts` (`OwnedGroup`, `ownProtocolProcess`) and `acpRunner.ts`, which stops one ACP server per turn.
- **Symptom**: after Stop of an Antigravity turn (server 1.3.0) whose `sleep 120` the agent ran as a background command, the `sleep` kept running with parent 1, inside the fence it started in; Doan showed the turn interrupted and nothing said a process was left.
- **Root cause**: Antigravity runs each tool command in a process group of its own and ends neither a background command on `session/cancel` nor any command on SIGTERM. `OwnedGroup` signals only the group Doan spawned, and once the server exits its children belong to launchd, so nothing tied the command to Doan any more. Copilot's tool commands, also in groups of their own, end with Copilot, which hid the gap behind one provider.
- **Net**: a provider-profile PoC that sent `kill(-pgid)` straight to the server saw it; the real-app Stop of a foreground command did not, because `session/cancel` makes Antigravity end that one itself, so only a background command (the prompt stays open until it ends) reached the group stop after `CANCEL_WAIT_MS`. Unit tests used fake servers without tool processes. A fake ACP server that starts a detached `sleep` and a real-app background-command probe reproduced it on the pre-fix build.
- **Prevention**: an owner of an agent process that runs tool commands reads the process table before its first signal and ends its descendants' groups too (`OwnedGroup`'s `processes`); a leader that already exited cannot be walked, so that path signals at once. When adding a provider, check where its tool commands run (`ps -o pid,ppid,pgid` while one runs) and test Stop with a command that ignores cancel. A command the tool shell backgrounds and abandons before the stop (`cmd &` in a shell that exits) already has parent 1 and is not found. Check each group owner: `grep -rn "new OwnedGroup(\|ownProtocolProcess(" app/src/main --include="*.ts" | grep -v "\.test\.ts"`; the Codex app-server's commands (`runners.ts` `ownProtocolProcess`) are not verified.
- **Fix**: 69184cce7 — end an ACP server's tool command process groups with the server.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-22, Claude runner `runners.ts`)**: a Claude Code background command that ignores SIGTERM (`bash -c "trap '' TERM; sleep 31"`) outlived the next send, the background line's Stop and quit with parent 1, because the Claude run's `OwnedGroup` had no `processes` and the CLI ends its task groups only by SIGTERM; it now passes `listProcesses`, and `kill()` reads no table, since a Claude kill races the model call the CLI makes 16-119 ms after its background set empties and a `ps` read takes about 20 ms, so a group owner that kills on a deadline gets its descendants from an earlier stop or not at all; the Codex app-server is still unverified; `grep -rn "new OwnedGroup(\|ownProtocolProcess(" app/src/main --include="*.ts" | grep -v "\.test\.ts"`.

### 2026-11-21 — Export viewer tests clicked the canvas while the stage was still moving

- **Area**: the real-DOM fixture in `app/src/main/exportViewer.test.ts` (selection, Option measurement, the shorthand inspector row and the reopening gesture).
- **Symptom**: on the sharded CI run, and on a loaded machine, a click into the canvas selected nothing, and a 30 s wait for `#selection-box` or a styles row timed out. Local single runs passed.
- **Root cause**: the canvas is an out-of-process iframe, and Chromium routes a press into it by the board it last composited. Playwright aims at the shell's current layout. A click sent just after the stage moved, when the panel opened or ⇧0 rescaled, therefore reached the shell's `#canvas` element instead of the canvas document. A related cause: one test waited for an intermediate render (the kept Cart) instead of the click's own result (Buy). This is a separate cause from 'Export viewer tests sent input before the canvas inspector opened': input that arrives after readiness can still miss the canvas while compositing lags.
- **Net**: CI shard 1/3 on the feature PR caught it. Local verify runs hit it as an occasional 30 s timeout that passed on rerun. Readiness waits and ordinary local runs missed it. Overloaded repeated runs of the file reproduced it, and with a probe they passed 738 runs.
- **Prevention**: before a press into the canvas after anything that moves or rescales the stage, point at the target until a trusted `pointermove` reaches it inside the canvas (the fixture's `pointAt`). Assert the click's final state, not an intermediate render. Search with `rg -n 'frameLocator\("#canvas"\)[^;]*\.click\(|canvasOf\([^)]*\)[^;]*\.click\(' app/src/main/exportViewer.test.ts`.
- **Fix**: fc3c0a7e6 — a `pointAt` probe before the clicks, and the reopening test waits for Buy.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-21, Hide inspector tooltip test)**: the timeout occurred before any canvas click. Delaying the inspector's ResizeObserver reproduced the same failure: the host was already 800 px wide, but Show inspector still had `aria-disabled="true"` and ignored Enter. Keyboard tests wait for its enabled state before pressing Enter once. Direct canvas clicks after the stage moves use the trusted-move probe. Inspect both input paths: `rg -n 'inspector-show|clickCanvas|pointAt' app/src/main/exportViewer.test.ts`.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-22, folded viewer hover, release gate shard 1/3)**: the search above covered clicks only, so a lone `hover()` just after ⇧0 still went to the shell's `#canvas` element and `expect.poll` waited for a hover mark no later move would draw; a stress run with hardware acceleration off (the CI macOS runners have no GPU) reproduced it, and forcing the first move onto the shell made it fail every time. Guard canvas hovers with `pointAt` like presses (the Option measurement hover after `selectDirect` already follows a confirmed move); fix f8f6eac5a; `rg -n 'frameLocator\("#canvas"\)[^;]*\.(click|hover)\(|canvasOf\([^)]*\)[^;]*\.(click|hover)\(|^\s+await \w+\.hover\(' app/src/main/exportViewer.test.ts`.

### 2026-11-21 — Codex ran an onboarding call written with `$PWD` in its sandbox and set the repository up with `doan init`

- **Area**: `cli/internal/cli/skill.md` (Onboarding paragraph), the Codex `doan` prefix rules (`codexRuleText` in `app/src/main/agentSetup/plan.ts`, the eval's `evalCodexRuleText` in `eval/lib/codex-rule.ts`), `eval/lib/transcript.ts` (`seqList`).
- **Symptom**: in agent-eval O3b, Codex (0.170.0 and 0.171.0, 4 of 4 runs) wrote `doan onboarding project inspect --path "$PWD" …`. The call returned `app_not_running` ("Cannot connect to Doan") although `doan onboarding start` had reached the app a moment earlier, and the agent took the guide's setup fallback, `doan init`, so the project was never added to Doan. Earlier Codex versions wrote the path out and passed.
- **Root cause**: Codex applies its `prefix_rule` only to a command whose words are all literal; a parameter expansion (`"$PWD"`), like the heredoc in U-024, makes the shell script unmatched, so it runs in the sandbox, where the app socket is blocked. The guide said only that onboarding paths are absolute, which `"$PWD"` satisfies, and its fallback treats a blocked socket as "Doan unreachable" without saying how that happens.
- **Net**: caught by the eval's automatic checks (`inspected_repo_path`, `no_doan_init`) once the Codex version changed; nothing in the CLI or its tests can see how an agent's shell spells an argument. Investigating it also showed the eval reader marking Claude's correct `for i in $(seq 1 15); do doan onboarding status …; done` poll as incomplete, which failed four absence checks; the reader now reads a whole-number `seq` list (not counting down without a step, where GNU and macOS's BSD `seq` differ, nor after a `seq` function, an alias or an IFS change), and a replay of 963 recorded runs flipped only four such polls to complete.
- **Prevention**: guidance that has an agent pass a value to a `doan` command Codex must run outside its sandbox asks for literal words (no variables, substitutions, heredocs, pipes or redirections), and an eval scenario exercises it with Codex; when a Codex run fails with `Cannot connect` after another `doan` call in the same session succeeded, compare the two calls' shell text in `codex-rollout.jsonl` before suspecting the app. `rg -n '<absolute>|absolute path|--message' cli/internal/cli/skill.md app/src/main/conversation/prompt.ts`
- **Recurrence (2026-11-21, agent-eval O2, `cli/internal/cli/skill.md` Onboarding)**: Codex joined a read to the first call (`sed -n '95,105p' …; doan onboarding start --json`), which is not a command of literal words either, so it ran sandboxed and the setup fell back to `doan init`; W8 Codex then joined `doan open … && shasum …` the same way, so the guide's command syntax line now asks for every `doan` call alone, in literal words (no `;`, `&&`, `|` or `$VAR`), instead of a rule per command, and a reviewer of guidance for sandboxed `doan` calls checks both the arguments and how the call is joined. `rg -n 'call alone|literal words' cli/internal/cli/skill.md app/src/main/conversation/prompt.ts`

### 2026-11-20 — The promotion divider offered Open on its own canvas

- **Area**: the bound marker in `app/src/renderer/ConversationThread.tsx`.
- **Symptom**: a conversation promoted to a canvas with no stable ID showed “Moved to” and Open while already in that canvas’s panel.
- **Root cause**: the panel gave the thread only the optional ID. The thread could not distinguish an ID-less open canvas from the Overview.
- **Net**: the existing marker tests used IDs for both matching and nonmatching canvases. Rendering the same marker with a path-only target reproduced the incorrect action.
- **Prevention**: check matching and different paths when both canvases lack IDs, and compare IDs when either canvas has one. Search with `rg -n 'openCanvasId|openCanvasPath' app/src/renderer/ConversationThread.tsx`.

### 2026-11-20 — A listed frame extended below the iframe that painted it

- **Area**: initial layout in `canvasScript.ts` and iframe sizing in `CanvasController.ts`.
- **Symptom**: the breadcrumb and highlight identified the second frame, but the header controls inside that frame were blank.
- **Root cause**: at DOMContentLoaded, pending linked stylesheets left the document 364 px tall. CSS moved the second frame to y=347 and grew the document to 988 px just as the iframe adopted 364 px. The viewport-growth guard mistook this for a viewport-dependent document and froze the iframe height. The controls at y=444 fell outside its painting area.
- **Net**: screenshots, measured frame positions and the actual iframe height exposed the clip. A successful CLI reply and a correct frame label both missed it. Font and viewport-growth tests began with styles already applied.
- **Prevention**: hold an initial stylesheet response across parsing, then verify both the displayed iframe height and a painted pixel near the bottom, including after a reload without a frame jump. Keep the genuine viewport-growth and late-font controls. Search with `rg -n 'firstLayoutAt|nextHeight|layoutHeight' app/src`.

### 2026-11-20 — CLI recovery named a menu item the user could not find

- **Area**: the embedded CLI guide, `doan active`, and the recovery steps shared by `open`, `open --prototype` and `handoff`.
- **Symptom**: the guide and `active` said “Add Project…”. The shared steps said “Add Projects”. The app lists “Add Projects…” under File.
- **Root cause**: the guide and CLI tests repeated the singular label or accepted a partial match. The app's menu test checked the actual label separately.
- **Net**: comparing the instructions with the File menu exposed the mismatch. Requiring the complete menu path made eight existing tests fail.
- **Prevention**: when a menu item changes, compare the menu definition with the embedded guide and each command's recovery steps, and assert the full path in the focused tests. Search with `rg -n 'Add Projects?' app/src/main/index.ts cli/internal/cli .agents/skills`.

### 2026-11-20 — A project left the list before its conversations stopped

- **Area**: the removal confirmation in `homeRun.ts`, the conversation service and the `folders:remove` handler.
- **Symptom**: one failed interruption could leave work running after its worktree disappeared from the list. Two worktrees or conversations with the same display name could also appear on both sides of a partial result.
- **Root cause**: the renderer treated dispatching the interrupts as completion. Group membership, the live turn and the runner's actual exit can all change after the dialog opens. Display names identify neither a worktree nor a turn.
- **Net**: the single-worktree success path missed mixed outcomes. Two-worktree tests held an exit, refused one stop, replaced its turn and added work before the final removal. A same-title fixture caught ambiguous results after Retry.
- **Prevention**: hold one process past the stop reply and check registrations before exit, after one failure and after Retry. Compare the successful and failed labels together. Search: `rg -n 'confirmRemove|interruptProjectTurn|projectRemovalBusy' app/src`.

### 2026-11-20 — The prototype hid an unread ending and reopened another conversation

- **Area**: read markers and list refreshes in the prototype conversation panel.
- **Symptom**: after a read-marker write failed, the folded panel lost its unread badge. Reopening could select a more recent conversation instead of the one with the unread ending.
- **Root cause**: a set of conversation IDs suppressed unread endings without tracking which write had succeeded. A write for an ending already saved by the other window can also succeed without a list notification, leaving an earlier list read as the last one in flight.
- **Net**: tests that settled each write before the next update missed the race. Holding the write for ending 4 while publishing ending 6 reproduced the wrong badge and target. The two-window probe checked both again after the successful retry.
- **Prevention**: test refusal and rejection, overlapping writes, and the final list notification arriving before an already-saved write replies. Check the badge and the conversation ID after reopening. Find the write and confirmation paths with `rg -n 'markSeen|seenMarks|markConversationSeen' app/src/renderer`.

### 2026-11-20 — A late settings reply could replace a newly entered server URL

- **Area**: the Add server by URL dialog in Settings.
- **Symptom**: dismissing and reopening a pending addition let its eventual success close the next dialog, or its failure restore the earlier URL.
- **Root cause**: the response checked only that an Add dialog was open. The field and cancellation actions also remained available after submission.
- **Net**: ordinary save and retry tests awaited the response before changing dialogs. The regression tests hold both requests, settle the earlier one first and check the second URL and the shared Settings lock.
- **Prevention**: test late success and failure after unmount and re-entry, including the lock callback. Find dialog response handlers with `rg -n 'submitAdd|current.current.*kind|onLock' app/src/renderer/SettingsMcpConnectors.tsx`.

### 2026-11-20 — Long breadcrumb labels disappeared before their breakpoints

- **Area**: the canvas header in `styles.css` and the exported viewer in `shell.css`.
- **Symptom**: with long project, frame and canvas names, an 820px header showed only the project thumbnail. At 720px the app also lost the frame label.
- **Root cause**: the project and frame had high flex shrink factors. Their minimum sizes reserved room for icons and padding alone, so their text could shrink to zero while its display rule still said it was visible.
- **Net**: short frame names passed the viewer boundary test. Measuring the text boxes in Electron and the exported file with long names exposed their zero widths.
- **Prevention**: verify the visibility of the text itself at each breakpoint with several long names competing for space. Also check the remaining icon and its accessible name below the breakpoint. Find priority-based shrink rules with `rg -n 'flex: 0 (1e[0-9]+|10000)' app/src/renderer/styles.css cli/internal/export/viewer/shell.css`.

### 2026-11-20 — The inspector element menu drew a thick horizontal scrollbar

- **Area**: the shared menu scrollbar in `app/src/renderer/ds/surfaces.css`.
- **Symptom**: horizontal scrolling in a deep element tree exposed a much thicker thumb than vertical scrolling.
- **Root cause**: Chromium uses the styled scrollbar's width for the vertical axis and its height for the horizontal axis. The menu set only width, so the newly scrollable axis used the default height.
- **Net**: dogfooding caught the difference. The menu style test covered the quiet thumb and vertical track margins. The earlier tree probe measured the viewport with both bars present but checked only row visibility and scrolling.
- **Prevention**: cover both scrollbar dimensions and track insets in the menu test. With both axes overflowing in Electron, subtract the borders and client dimensions from the outer dimensions to measure both gutters. Find styled scrollbars with `rg -nF '::-webkit-scrollbar {' app/src --glob '*.css'`.

### 2026-11-20 — MCP connectors offered Reconnect to a new Claude account

- **Area**: `McpService.logoutForAuth`, the connector store's `everConnected` history and `connectorState`.
- **Symptom**: after switching Claude accounts, Settings said the new account had previously connected to a server that only the old account had used.
- **Root cause**: logout removed the old token but left connection history keyed by provider and server. A pending logout also turned a signed-in status into Reconnect needed even without history.
- **Net**: the replacement test checked logout commands and pending records. It did not seed connection history for both providers. The regression now checks the saved history and the action shown for both a successful and a pending logout.
- **Prevention**: test replacement with equal, different and missing account IDs. On a confirmed switch, include a server absent from the provider's list and a server used by both providers. Check the action while logout is pending. Search with `rg -n 'everConnected|reconnect_needed' app/src/main/mcpConnectors -g '!*.test.ts'`.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-20, sign-out review)**: signing out and then signing in to another account also inherited the old history. The fresh sign-in has no previous account ID to compare. The regression now follows both actions for Claude and Codex and checks the other provider’s history. Clear history on sign-out before Claude skips per-server logout. Search with `rg -n 'logoutForAuth|clearHistory' app/src/main/mcpConnectors`.

### 2026-11-20 — Claude MCP sign-in exited successfully without saving its token

- **Area**: `AGENT_WRITES.claude` and `CLAUDE_REFRESH` in `app/src/main/agentAuth/fence.ts` and the macOS path checks in `fence.seatbelt.test.ts`.
- **Symptom**: in Doan 0.1.22 the login exited 0, but the next status check reported that the connector was still signed out.
- **Root cause**: Claude Code 2.1.329 takes a profile-local lock before saving OAuth client information or tokens, even when it writes the credentials to the macOS Keychain. The fence allowed the credential file and its suffixes. The lock has its own basename. Before taking it to save refreshed tokens, Claude also needs the account refresh lock and its owner record or a per-server MCP refresh lock.
- **Net**: a sandbox denial during sign-in identified the missing lock. The earlier turn with a tool call never exercised this write, and inspecting a signed-in profile showed no lock after the writer released it. A 401 exercised reactive MCP refresh and masked a separate failure: the CLI logged that it had skipped proactive refresh. An expired account fixture also stopped before the token exchange.
- **Prevention**: when probing provider writes, include a fresh fenced login and check the stored credential from a second process. Use expired account and MCP fixtures with a local token endpoint. Check the rotated credentials in storage and confirm the first MCP request uses the new token before any 401. Pin both the required names and nearby denied names under the real Seatbelt policy. Find the policy and its Claude callers with `rg -n 'AGENT_WRITES|profileFencedCommand|fenceArgs\("claude"' app/src/main -g '!*.test.ts'`.

### 2026-11-20 — The stale-press test opened the drawer during the canvas click

- **Area**: the real-DOM drawer test in `app/src/main/exportViewer.test.ts`.
- **Symptom**: CI run 44653970670 still found an empty selection after the fixture waited for inspector readiness and layout. All three drag tests passed.
- **Root cause**: the test opened the drawer on the canvas's pointerdown message. The canvas selects on click. In Electron, placing the drawer over the release point sent pointerup to a drawer row and the canvas emitted no selection. With the drawer clear of the pointer, the test could also pass after the shell had already handled the press with canvas focus. CI recorded the assertion, but no pointer trace.
- **Prevention**: hold the genuine press payload and intervening canvas messages until the real click produces its selection. Focus the drawer, deliver the buffered messages in order, then let the shell render the selection. A control that accepted a press with drawer focus closed the drawer and failed the visibility assertion.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-20, exported viewer drawer)**: the same empty selection reappeared with the press buffered.
