In a local Electron trace the board scrolled to reach the oversized frame, then the shell's iframe element received the click and the canvas document sent no press. For focus tests, keep the target in view with the inspector open and click its center. Search with `rg -n 'compactCart|click\(\{ position' app/src/main/exportViewer.test.ts`.

### 2026-11-20 — Export viewer tests sent input before the canvas inspector opened

- **Area**: the real-DOM fixture in `app/src/main/exportViewer.test.ts`.
- **Symptom**: CI sometimes observed no text-drag message in the shell focus and blur tests, or an empty selection in the drawer test.
- **Root cause**: clicking Show inspector opens the shell panel synchronously, but the canvas starts handling input after it receives `inspect-mode`. The fixture treated completion of the click as readiness. Opening the panel also shrinks the board before its resize observer repositions the stage.
- **Net**: the tests passed in ordinary local runs. Holding mode delivery in an Electron probe reproduced the CI assertion values with the original clicks and drags. Delaying only the board resize moved a cached pointer target by 140 px in the same fixture, although the drag still succeeded in that run.
- **Prevention**: before sending canvas input, wait for the canvas iframe's first inspection reply and the stage position for the resized board at the requested zoom. Keep the real input and its outcome assertions. Search with `rg -n 'Show inspector|showInspector|boundingBox' app/src/main/exportViewer.test.ts`.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-20, exported inspector input)**: Waiting for the first reply hid a real click that activated a canvas button during opening. Hold the mode or pan command at the iframe, click a native control, and check activation, selection after Option release or a board clear, and focus after both Escapes. Keep the focus fixture in view at its test zoom. Search with `rg -n 'inspect-mode|openingGesture|showInspector' app/src/main/exportViewer.test.ts cli/internal/export/viewer`.

### 2026-11-20 — The exported inspector copied a token from outside the selected element’s CSS scope

- **Area**: `app/src/main/cssomMatched.ts` and the generated canvas export inspector.
- **Symptom**: a scoped text color appeared with the winning RGB value but copied the outer rule’s token. Padding set only inside the scope disappeared from the panel.
- **Root cause**: the viewer read the applied value from the browser and reconstructed its source in a separate CSSOM walk. The walk skipped `CSSScopeRule`, so the row resolver received an older declaration or no author declaration for that property.
- **Net**: a source audit found the missing grouping rule. The existing browser test covered layers, media and supports, but had no competing scoped and unscoped declarations. A real DOM test caught both the wrong copy value and the missing row before the fix.
- **Prevention**: when adding a grouping rule to the collector, check the panel’s visible rows and copy values on both sides of its boundary. Put a competing declaration later in the stylesheet so source order alone cannot pass. Search with `rg -n 'CSSScopeRule|CSSContainerRule' app/src/main`.

### 2026-11-20 — Quick Open and Home preset search ranked a substring after 𠮷 as a word start

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/searchTokens.ts`, shared by Quick Open and Home preset and skill search.
- **Symptom**: searching for `art` could put `𠮷art` above `x art` and highlight the first occurrence in `𠮷art x art`. Home also ranked a preset titled `𠮷art` above a skill with the description `x art`.
- **Root cause**: both scorers and the Quick Open highlighter used a UTF-16 offset from `indexOf` and tested one preceding code unit. The low surrogate of 𠮷 alone matched neither the letter nor the number category.
- **Net**: tests with Korean syllables covered single-code-unit letters. The new rank and highlight tests caught the split pair. A first fix using an end-anchored Unicode category check passed in Node but failed in the Electron 45.3.4 renderer.
- **Prevention**: classify the complete preceding code point. Verify ranking with the substring indexed first and highlighting with the stronger occurrence second, including in Electron. Search for single-unit lookbehinds with `rg 'slice\(at - 1, at\)|charAt\(at - 1\)' app/src/renderer`.

### 2026-11-20 — A canvas that became available after the worktree menu opened could disappear silently

- **Area**: `WorktreeSwitch.tsx` and `WorktreeSwitch.dom.test.ts`.
- **Symptom**: pressing a worktree row after it changed from “Reading…” to selectable and then to “No home here” left the canvas open without the expected notice.
- **Root cause**: the menu remembered only the roots selectable when it opened. A root offered later in the same opening was indistinguishable from one that had always been disabled.
- **Net**: the DOM tests covered a read completing and a canvas disappearing in separate menu openings. Joining the transitions reproduced the missing callback.
- **Prevention**: keep offer history through each committed list update while the menu is open. Test both a press after the offer disappears and a press after reopening with the same row still disabled. Search: `rg -n 'offered\.current|new Set\(model\.rows' app/src/renderer`.

### 2026-11-20 — Failed eval runs left temporary directories behind

- **Area**: `eval/run.ts` self-test build and real/fixture run cleanup.
- **Symptom**: a failed CLI build left its output directory in place. A failure writing the evidence error report left the fixture workspace in place.
- **Root cause**: the build ran before the cleanup block began. Workspace disposal shared a block with evidence collection, which could still throw while reporting its own failure.
- **Net**: the audit identified both paths. Fixture runs covered ordinary disposal, but only the failed build and failed error-report tests in `eval/lib/fixture.test.ts` checked which directories survived.
- **Prevention**: acquire temporary resources inside the cleanup scope and give disposal its own `finally` when evidence collection can throw. Search with `rg -n 'mkdtempSync|keepEvidence|ws.dispose' eval/run.ts`.

### 2026-11-19 — project_not_open named only the folder's base name, so a same-name checkout looked already added

- **Area**: `cli/internal/cli/open_rules.go` (`notOpenMessage`, `notOpenSteps`), `prototype_rules.go`, `handoff_rules.go`, `handoff.go`; tests `open_test.go` `TestRunOpenNotOpenNamesTheRoot`, `TestOpenReplyGuidance`, `prototype_rules_test.go`, `handoff_rules_test.go` `TestHandoffNotOpenNamesTheRoot`.
- **Symptom**: an outside user reported `doan open .doan/canvases/rain-tone.html --json` answering `project_not_open` ("Doan does not have the folder \"subway-mong\" open") four times after they had added "subway-mong" in Doan and relaunched it. The only recovery the `nextSteps` gave was to add the folder, which they had done. `doan handoff` named no folder at all.
- **Root cause**: the app registers roots by exact path, and ARCHITECTURE §12 counts another checkout or worktree of a saved project as not open, but the message identified the root by `filepath.Base`, which is the same for every checkout of one repository. The cause of the report is unconfirmed (no access to the reporter's machine); ruled out in code: a startup window without saved roots (the socket starts after the list is ready), two installed apps both answering (single-instance lock per userData), and an app/CLI spelling mismatch at the same version (both store and send the on-disk spelling, best effort when an ancestor cannot be listed).
- **Net**: an outside report caught it. Tests pinned the message by the base name and the steps by "Add Projects" only, so nothing checked that the message tells two roots of the same name apart.
- **Prevention**: a message about a root the app matches by exact path names the whole root; JSON keeps it exact and text output goes through `EscapeControl`. When the CLI cannot tell causes apart, add a conditional step rather than asserting one. Search: `grep -rn 'filepath.Base([a-zA-Z.]*[rR]oot)' cli --include='*.go'`; the remaining `canvas_not_found` hits in `open_rules.go` and `prototype_rules.go` are safe, because the app found that root and only the canvas is missing.

### 2026-11-19 — Codex eval runs ignored the workspace's doan rule, so no doan call reached the fake app

- **Area**: `eval/lib/agent.ts` (`isolateUserInstructions`), `eval/lib/workspace.ts` (`prepareWorkspace`); tests `agent.test.ts`, `workspace.test.ts`.
- **Symptom**: in round `open-not-open-261119` both Codex runs (W4, R10) failed with `app_not_running` and `delivered:false`: every `doan` call that needs the app socket ran inside the `workspace-write` sandbox. Claude runs in the same round were fine. Each Codex `stderr.log` began with `warning: Untrusted project-local configuration is disabled. Run codex exec --trust …`, and the run still exited 0.
- **Root cause**: the eval allows `doan` outside the sandbox only through the project-layer rule it commits to the temp repo (`.codex/rules/doan.rules`), and codex-cli 0.169.0 loads a project's `.codex/` layer only when `$CODEX_HOME/config.toml` marks that project `trust_level = "trusted"`. Rounds on Codex 0.167.1 delivered these calls; the 0.169.0 `stderr.log` of an earlier D5 round (`doc-check-r2-d5`) already carries the warning, unnoticed because D5 needs no socket. The temp repo is new on every run, so no config trusts it. A `-c projects."<path>".trust_level="trusted"` override does not count (probe), and `codex exec --trust` saves the trust into `$CODEX_HOME/config.toml`, which the run's home linked to the user's file. Codex reports the skipped layer as a warning, not an error, and the runner did not read it.
- **Net**: caught by a real round whose Codex runs needed the socket. Missed by the runner's tests and self-test, which use a fake agent and so never see what a real Codex loads, and by the D5 round, whose checks did not depend on the rule.
- **Prevention**: the run's CODEX_HOME holds a copy of the user's `config.toml` plus a trust table for the temp repo only (`agent.test.ts`, `workspace.test.ts`); probes on 0.169.0 showed that trusting the first worktree also covers a linked second one, and that a non-Git folder is trusted by its own path. Never link a file in a run's CODEX_HOME that Codex itself writes, except the sign-in files. After a Codex version change, run one socket scenario (W4) before a full round, and when a Codex lane fails on the socket, search its stderr first: `rg -l 'Untrusted project-local' .scratch/agent-eval/*/*-codex/stderr.log`.

### 2026-11-19 — Hover and selection outlines of a clipped element reached across neighbouring frames

- **Area**: `app/src/main/canvasScript.ts` (`rectOf`, new `visibleRect`), read by `postHover`, `describe` (the select target), `send` (the layout's `selected`) and the element tree; drawn by `CanvasController.drawMarks`, `placeSize` and `drawMeasure`.
- **Symptom**: in Select and the Picker, hovering an element wider than the box that clips it (a scenery strip inside a phone's door window, moved by a `translateX` loop, clipped by the window's `overflow: hidden`) drew the dashed outline around the strip's whole box, far outside the frame and across the next frames. Because the hover is re-read on every pointer move, the outline also jumped with the animation. The selection outline, the size bubble and the ⌥ measurement read the same rect.
- **Root cause**: `rectOf` posted `getBoundingClientRect()` as is. That rect is the element's layout box after transforms; it says nothing about what its ancestors clip, so it is the visible area only when no ancestor clips. The script already modelled which ancestors clip, but only for the document height (`frameBottom`, Y only), and the marks never used it.
- **Net**: caught from a user screen recording. Missed by the unit tests, whose fake DOM has no clipping, and by every earlier runtime probe, whose canvases never put an element wider than a clipping box. Now a pure test pins `visibleRect` (both axes, the absolute and fixed escape, boxes overflow does not apply to), a real-DOM test in `canvasScript.test.ts` checks the hover, select and layout rects of a translated strip in an `overflow: hidden` window (red on `x: -48, width: 600` before the fix), and a real-app probe checked the outline, the size bubble and the ⌥ gap.
- **Prevention**: any rect the canvas script posts for the app to draw over the canvas must be cut to what shows, with `visibleRect` or the anchors' `clip`. `grep -n "getBoundingClientRect\|getClientRects" app/src/main/canvasScript.ts` lists every place that reads a box; one not passed through `rectOf`, `visibleRect` or the chip anchors' clip is a candidate. Not verified: the text-range line rects (`getClientRects` of the selected Text nodes) and the frame list's rects post unclipped boxes too; a text range inside a clipped window would paint past it the same way.
- **Fix**: e4f2525f8, overlay rects intersect every clipping ancestor, chosen by `frameBottom`'s rules on both axes.

### 2026-11-19 — The approval card could still show less than what Allow approves: a provider's parsed fragment, an MCP argument, invisible characters

- **Area**: `app/src/main/conversation/transport.ts` (`codexPermission`, `commandText`, `claudePermissionSummary`), `acp.ts` (`acpPermission`), `mcpConnectors/codexApproval.ts`, `conversation/cardText.ts`; tests in `transport.test.ts`, `acpRunner.test.ts`, `codexApproval.test.ts`.
- **Symptom**: after "The permission approval card showed the agent's own description…" (2026-11-18), a Codex command approval whose line Codex parsed into one action showed that action's text, which can name only part of the line, while Allow answered for the whole command. A Claude MCP tool's card led with an argument named `command`, `path` or `url` and did not name the tool. Bidi controls and invisible code points reached the card raw. Codex network rules, a file change's `grantRoot` and ACP "Always allow" saved something the card did not name.
- **Root cause**: the summary builders took the friendliest field a request carried, and a provider's display hint (`commandActions`, documented by Codex as "best-effort parsed command actions for friendly display") looked like the operation itself. Generic argument names (`command`, `path`) were read the same way for every tool, so an MCP tool's argument passed as a built-in tool's operation. No step treated the card's text as untrusted display text.
- **Net**: caught by the 2026-11-19 security audit's completeness pass over the previous fix, then confirmed with an isolated Codex app-server probe against a mock model provider (temporary `CODEX_HOME` and HOME, no account). Missed by the 2026-11-18 fix and its tests, whose Codex fixtures had parsed actions equal to the whole line, and by its review, which checked text sources (agent words vs operation) but not provider display hints or character classes.
- **Prevention**: an approval's summary comes from the field the answer covers (Codex `params.command`), never from a provider's display parse; a wrapper is removed only by decoding it exactly, else the text shows as sent. An MCP tool's summary names the tool and server before its arguments. Every card text passes `clip`/`visibleText` (`cardText.ts`), and an agent value inside Doan's sentence goes through `quoted`. Tests pin a multi-segment line that parses to one action. Search: `rg -n 'commandActions|\.slice\(0, 512\)|“\$\{' app/src/main/conversation app/src/main/mcpConnectors` — `commandActions` may feed only activity and stage (never a permission summary); a `.slice(0, 512)` on permission text should be `clip`.

### 2026-11-19 — Closing provider settings path by path kept leaving profile stores a fenced turn could write and a later run reads

- **Area**: `app/src/main/agentAuth/fence.ts` (`AGENT_WRITES`, `agentRule`, `agentParams`, `codexProfileArgs`), `app/src/main/conversation/runners.ts` (`claudeRunSettings`); tests `fence.test.ts`, `fence.seatbelt.test.ts`, `runners.fence.test.ts`.
- **Symptom**: after three rounds of adding closed paths (entry 2026-11-09 'An agent planted provider settings in its own profile…' and its recurrences), a fenced turn could still write profile state that later processes read or run: Codex's runtime folders, databases and caches at the profile root, Claude's user rules, output styles and auto memory, and the ACP agents' instruction, rule, skill, hook and permission files.
- **Root cause**: the fence opened the whole profile and closed a list of known settings paths, so every store a provider added or that the list's author did not know about stayed open; a CLI version bump adds such stores without notice. A path rule also cannot reliably keep a store nested inside a folder the CLI must write (auto memory beside Claude's transcripts).
- **Net**: caught by a security audit reading the installed binaries' strings and a signed-in profile's entries, then confirmed with an isolated Codex probe. Missed by `fence.test.ts`, which compared the policy text and parameters and never applied the policy, so no test said which paths a fenced process could write.
- **Prevention**: the outer fence closes the agent's own folder to writes and reopens only what the CLI writes (`AGENT_WRITES`), and Codex's permission profiles keep the profile read-only to commands. `fence.seatbelt.test.ts` applies the real policy and lists, path by path, what stays writable and what is refused; a store that must stay writable but sits beside instructions is turned off at the flag tier instead (Claude auto memory). When a provider's minimum version rises, run a turn with an isolated config folder (a mock model endpoint works for Claude and Codex), diff the folder against the allowlist and add a case per new path; `rg -n 'AGENT_WRITES|autoMemoryEnabled' app/src/main -g '!*.test.ts'`.

### 2026-11-19 — The packaged app still accepted Chromium's remote-debugging switches after its fuses were set

- **Area**: `app/src/main/index.ts`, `app/src/main/remoteDebugging.ts`, `app/package.json` (`build.electronFuses`); test `remoteDebugging.test.ts`.
- **Symptom**: the signed app started with `--remote-debugging-port` or `--remote-debugging-pipe` and opened Chromium's DevTools protocol, although the fuses were meant to close the binary's debugging entry points.
- **Root cause**: Electron's fuses cover Node's entry points (run as Node, `NODE_OPTIONS`, Node inspector flags), not Chromium switches, and no fuse exists for remote debugging; the hardening was judged by the fuse list, which reads as complete.
- **Net**: caught by the security audit's second round, which reviewed the signed binary's debugging entry points rather than the fuse wire. Missed by `verifySignedPackage.mjs`, which checks the fuse wire only, and by any launch of the packaged app with Chromium switches.
- **Prevention**: the packaged app exits in the main script, before Chromium opens its DevTools server (it does so after the main script starts; a probe of the packaged app showed no port and exit 1 for both switches). When a new way to attach a debugger or load code appears, check it against a packaged launch, not the fuse list. `rg -n 'remote-debugging|electronFuses|hasSwitch' app/src/main app/package.json app/scripts --glob '!*.test.ts'`.

### 2026-11-19 — An exported prototype lost a followed link when the frame navigated before the viewer read the goto

- **Area**: `cli/internal/export/viewer/isolate.js` (followed-link decision), `prototype.js` (`acceptMessage`), `app/src/main/exportViewer.test.ts` ('an href the canvas sets again after ours came off…').
- **Symptom**: when a canvas listener set a link's href again after `isolate.js` took it off, the browser navigated the frame and the goto was sometimes lost, so the documented "the link is still followed" did not hold. The real-DOM test of that limit hung to Vitest's 20 s limit in two CI first attempts and passed on re-run.
- **Root cause**: the decision ran in a 0 ms timer so it could read the final `defaultPrevented`, which assumed the document outlives one task. A navigation that commits first takes the timer with it, and a message the old document posted just before reaches the viewer with `event.source === null`, which `acceptMessage` refuses (as it must: a replaced iframe's late message looks the same). The app's prototype window has the same timer but refuses every canvas navigation (`prototypeFrame.ts`), so its document always stays.
- **Net**: missed by 340 local probe runs, even at load 58: the commit is slower than the timer unless the renderer is busy right after the click. Caught by the audit's reading of the CI hang and the PR review that traced it to the timer; reproduced deterministically by queuing a 300 ms busy timer before the click (10 of 10 lost); the test now runs with and without it.
- **Prevention**: a script in a canvas document that defers a decision to a later task must decide in `beforeunload` too when the document may navigate away (after the dispatch: `event.eventPhase === Event.NONE`), since a receiver that checks `event.source` drops whatever the old document posts late. A real-DOM test of a navigation race forces the order (a busy timer queued before the action) rather than waiting for load to find it. Search: `rg -n "setTimeout\(" cli/internal/export/viewer app/src/main/canvasScript.ts app/src/main/prototypeScript.ts` and check each timer that posts after a click whether the document can navigate first.

### 2026-11-18 — The canvas export viewer kept the srcdoc navigation bugs the prototype page had already fixed

- **Area**: `cli/internal/export/viewer/measure.js`, `isolate.js`, `app/src/main/exportViewer.test.ts`
- **Symptom**: in a canvas exported with `doan export`, a `<a href="#pricing">` did nothing in Chromium and loaded the viewer page into the canvas frame in WebKit, and `history.pushState(s, "", "#tab")` threw `SecurityError` in both, stopping a router script. `links` did not list the `#` link, since export treats fragments as in-document. The same canvas works in the app, whose board loads it from an http URL.
- **Root cause**: both exported pages run the canvas as a `srcdoc` document, which resolves fragments and History URLs against the outer file's URL, but the workaround was written for the prototype page only (`isolate.js`), when its navigation was probed. The canvas viewer's in-canvas script (`measure.js`) was pinned to the app's board script, which never needs it, so nothing compared it with the prototype page's script.
- **Net**: caught by an `/audit` review, which compared the two viewer scripts that share the srcdoc setup, then reproduced in exported files with Playwright Chromium and WebKit. Missed by the viewer tests, which pin `measure.js` only to `canvasScript.ts`, and by the prototype work, which probed only its own page.
- **Prevention**: a fix for a behaviour of the `srcdoc` setup goes into every script that runs first in an exported canvas document (`isolate.js` and `measure.js`), with a text pin between the copies and a real-DOM test in `exportViewer.test.ts` that loads the document as `srcdoc`. `rg -ln 'pushState|sameDocument' cli/internal/export/viewer` must list both scripts; `rg -n 'srcdoc' cli/internal/export/viewer/*.html` lists the pages that host a canvas.

### 2026-11-18 — The buddies and the dot field stayed at 1x after the window's scale changed

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/Doany.tsx`, `dockedBuddyShell.ts`, `CanvasAgent.tsx` (`DotField`), `devicePixels.ts`.
- **Symptom**: Home's buddy, the docked buddy and the agent indicator's dot field sized their canvas backing store only from a content-box ResizeObserver or once at mount. A `devicePixelRatio` change (the window moved to a display of another scale) left them at the old size: in the built app at an emulated DPR 2, Home's canvas stayed 114 wide (documented 228) and the docked one 56 (documented 112), drawn blurry.
- **Root cause**: a scale change resizes nothing in CSS, so no content-box observer fires; nothing listened to the ratio itself. The first fix observed `device-pixel-content-box`, which the spec says changes with the scale, but in Electron 45 it did not fire for a scale change emulated over CDP (`Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride`), while a `(resolution: <dpr>dppx)` media query did.
- **Net**: found by a source audit; confirmed in the built app with a CDP DPR override and a draw-size read before the fix. The `device-pixel-content-box` version passed its unit tests (they only pinned the observer option) and failed the same probe; the media query version passed it. A real move between displays of different scale is not verified (one display on the probing Mac).
- **Prevention**: a canvas whose backing store follows `devicePixelRatio` also listens for its change (`onDevicePixelRatio`), and its test changes the ratio without any resize. Verify a DPR behaviour with a probe that changes the ratio, not with a test of an observer option. Search: `rg -n "devicePixelRatio" app/src --glob '!*.test.*'`.

### 2026-11-18 — A repeated Escape could close the busy Move to Trash alert

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/CanvasTrashDialog.tsx`.
- **Symptom**: while the confirmed move was out, the alert refused its cancel (`onCancel` prevented it), but it had no Escape keydown handling, so Chromium could still close the modal dialog on a repeated Escape while App held the move as pending.
- **Root cause**: preventing a `<dialog>`'s `cancel` event does not stop Chromium from closing it on a repeated Escape; the sibling alerts that refuse cancel while busy (SettingsDialog, ConversationDeleteDialog) handle the keydown for this, and the trash alert was written from the `onCancel` half only.
- **Net**: found by a source audit comparing the alerts; pinned by a unit test of the keydown (busy: prevented and not cancelled; idle: cancelled; composing: left alone). Not reproduced with a real double Escape in the app.
- **Prevention**: a modal `<dialog>` that can refuse its cancel also takes Escape's keydown (prevent, then the same dismiss). Search: `rg -l "onCancel=" app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'` and check each refusing dialog has an `onKeyDown` Escape handler (on 2026-11-18 the ones without it always cancel).

### 2026-11-18 — A project conversation's queue made while its rate-limited turn waited for judgement went out before the reset

- **Area**: `app/src/main/conversation/service.ts` (`judge`, `queue`, `limitEnded`, `queueAfterEnd`); test `service.usageLimit.test.ts` 'a project conversation's queue made while its limited turn waits for its judgement'.
- **Symptom**: a project conversation (no canvas) whose turn failed `rate_limited` shows working until its promotion judgement, so the composer queues; the judgement then flushed that queue, with the turn's own re-queued message, at once instead of at the reset. The provider refused it again: one more failed turn and a wasted request. A canvas conversation in the same state waited for the reset.
- **Root cause**: a held queue goes out from more than one place after a turn ends (`ended` for a canvas turn, `judge` for a project turn, `queueAfterEnd` for a moved queue, `sendDue` for a schedule). The rule "a queue behind a limited turn waits for the reset" was written as a guard in `queueAfterEnd` only; `queue()` sets a hold only while a judgement is pending, so the unguarded `judge` path was reachable for project conversations alone.
- **Net**: caught by the 2026-11-18 audit's static trace of `judge`. Missed by the usage-limit tests, which queued only on canvas conversations; the one project case queued nothing.
- **Prevention**: every path that flushes a held queue after a turn end checks `live.limitLookup` and `this.schedules` first, or runs only after a `turn.done`; a usage-limit test that queues covers a project conversation (`h.startProject()`) as well as a canvas one. `rg -n "this\.flush\(" app/src/main/conversation/service.ts` and confirm each caller is guarded or follows a done turn (`sendDue` is the schedule itself).

### 2026-11-18 — An existing Claude session read as missing when its transcript was large or held one bad byte

- **Area**: `app/src/main/conversation/prepare.ts` (`claudeSessionExists`), `io.ts` (`readRegular`, `claudeTranscript`); test `prepare.test.ts` 'claudeSessionExists'.
- **Symptom**: a Claude transcript over 256 MiB, or with one byte that is not UTF-8, made every send and resume of its conversation fail `session_unavailable`, and each send read the whole transcript synchronously on main first.
- **Root cause**: the existence check proved the file exists by reading it with `readRegular`, whose size limit and fatal UTF-8 decode are meant for Doan's own bounded files, and swallowed every throw as "missing". Claude Code owns the transcript and bounds neither its size nor its bytes. Its twin `claudeTranscript` checked only the file type, so the two disagreed on the same file.
- **Net**: caught by the 2026-11-18 audit (main agents review). Missed by the prepare tests, which used small, valid transcripts.
- **Prevention**: an existence or identity check of an agent-owned file opens it with `openRegular` (O_NONBLOCK, regular file) and closes it, never reads it; a read of such a file has a stated bound and a reason. `rg -n "readRegular\(" app/src/main --glob '!*.test.ts'` and confirm each target is Doan-owned or read on purpose. Safe agent-owned reads: `io.ts` reads a Claude session registry record (64 KiB, a small JSON file) and the transcript of a session `/clear` just made (8 MiB; it holds only the clear's lines yet).

### 2026-11-18 — A handoff's first message that starts with a command name was drawn as a command turn

- **Area**: `app/src/main/conversation/commandTurns.ts`, `providerCommands.ts` (`messageCommand`), `app/src/renderer/commandView.ts` (`threadCommands`, `firstTitleMessage`); tests `commandTurns.test.ts`, `commandUi.test.ts`.
- **Symptom**: `doan handoff --message "/compact the layout …"` started a conversation whose first turn showed the Compacting label, a false "Context compacted" boundary after it (for `/clear`, a hidden context ring), and no title fallback, in this run and after a restart.
- **Root cause**: whether a turn is a command was decided from the stored message's text alone. The send path knows a handoff's text is never a command (`handoff ? null`), but the history readers in main and the renderer re-derived it from text without looking at `origin`. The rule also lived in two copies, and main's copy produced `turns` and `usageStale` fields nothing read.
- **Net**: caught by the 2026-11-18 audit (main agents and maintainability reviews). Missed by the command tests, which never stored a message with `origin`.
- **Prevention**: a stored message is read as a command only through `messageCommand`, which skips `origin`; `commandName` takes text being sent. `rg -n "commandName\(" app/src --glob '!*.test.*'` and confirm each caller reads text being sent or a queued row, never a stored `user.message`. A field a reply carries has a reader, or it is removed.

### 2026-11-18 — The serving scope took any configured path, and canvas requests to other loopback listeners went out ungated

- **Area**: `cli/internal/repo/scope.go` (`Scope`, `Withheld`), `cli/internal/cli/check.go`, `cli/internal/cli/config.go`, `cli/internal/export/resolve.go`, `app/src/main/serve/path.ts` (`inScope`, `withheld`), `app/src/main/serve/loopback.ts`, `app/src/main/index.ts` (`denyPermissions`); tests `repo/scope_test.go`, `cli/scope_test.go`, `cli/config_test.go`, `export/resolve_test.go`, `check_test.go`, `serve/path.test.ts`, `serve/server.test.ts`, `serve/loopback.test.ts`.
- **Symptom**: the serving scope did not exclude `.git` or `.env` paths that `.doan/config.json` listed, and the canvas sessions had no filter for requests to other loopback addresses.
- **Root cause**: the scope's guards were written against the person's own mistakes (the repository root, `.doan` metadata, links out of the repo), not against a config that arrives with a clone; only `doan config` refused root `.git`, and the documented rule kept `repo.Scope` and the server out of it. Chromium gates requests by address space, and a document served from 127.0.0.1 is already in the loopback space, so its requests to other loopback ports never reach the local-network check; the sessions had no request filter of their own.
- **Net**: caught by the security audit's trust-boundary review of canvas content and the local server; an isolated real-app probe showed the request went out before the fix and is cancelled after it. Missed by the serve tests, which pinned what a scope entry may resolve to but never what a scope entry may name, and by any runtime check of canvas requests leaving for other local ports.
- **Prevention**: every scope comparison (equal to an entry or under it) also calls `repo.Withheld` / `withheld`, so the server, `css_not_served` and export keep refusing the same paths; every Electron session that can load canvas content goes through `denyPermissions`, which installs the loopback guard. The guard's name resolution is best-effort. `rg -n 'HasPrefix\(p, s\+"/"\)|startsWith\(`\$\{entry\}/`\)' cli app/src --glob '!*_test.go' --glob '!*.test.ts'`; `rg -n 'fromPartition\(|session\.defaultSession|partition:' app/src/main --glob '!*.test.ts'`.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-19, `repo.Scope` and `serve/loopback.ts`)**: the scope judged a serve entry by its resolved path only while `serve_withheld` also judged its written name, and the guard resolved names apart from Chromium (no hosts file or per-domain resolvers) and did not count this Mac's own interface addresses and `.local` name; the scope now drops an entry withheld as written, and the guard resolves through the session (`resolveHost`) and reads this Mac's addresses per request; `rg -n 'Withheld\(|withheld\(' cli/internal app/src/main --glob '!*_test.go' --glob '!*.test.ts'` and check each judges the same form (written, resolved or both) as its counterpart.

### 2026-11-18 — Agents in Doan conversations held the same app-socket credential and Codex command rule as the person's terminal

- **Area**: `app/src/main/runtime.ts` (`answerLine`, `startRuntime`, `CONVERSATION_RUNTIME`), `app/src/main/agentSetup/plan.ts` (`codexRuleText`, `EARLIER_CODEX_RULES`), `app/src/main/agentAuth/fence.ts`, `app/src/main/index.ts` (conversation `appRuntime`), `cli/internal/cli/socket.go` (`ownSocket`); tests `runtime.conversation.test.ts`, `runtime.test.ts`, `plan.test.ts`, `fence.test.ts`, `prepareFence.test.ts`, `index.test.ts`, `socket_owner_test.go`.
- **Symptom**: an agent in a Doan conversation, in any permission mode, could use every app-socket version, onboarding and project removal included, and Codex's `doan` rule let every `doan` subcommand run outside its sandbox without asking, the ones that write files or change Doan's projects included. The CLI also dialed whatever socket a runtime file named without checking who created it.
- **Root cause**: one runtime file and one token served two callers with different trust: the person's terminal agent and a conversation agent whose mode the person chose as limited. Both the token and the Codex rule were written for the first caller (reach the app at all) and reused for the second without asking what the second should reach. A broad rule with narrow exceptions (`doan handoff` asked) kept every later subcommand on the broad side by default.
- **Net**: caught by a security audit tracing each capability the conversation environment carries; missed by the unit tests, which pinned that the token and the allow rule existed and that each socket version checked a token, never which caller held which token.
- **Prevention**: every credential or rule handed to an agent lists what it allows, and tests pin the refusals as well as the grants (`runtime.conversation.test.ts` refuses v2 and v7 under the conversation credential; `plan.test.ts` pins each `prompt` subcommand). A new socket version or a new `doan` subcommand that writes outside the repository decides both in the same change: whether the conversation credential may use it and whether the Codex rule asks for it. Search run: `rg -n 'prefix_rule|DOAN_RUNTIME|CONVERSATION_RUNTIME|scoped\(' app/src/main cli/internal -g '!*.test.ts' -g '!*_test.go'`.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-19, `codexRuleText` in `agentSetup/plan.ts`, `cli/internal/cli/run.go`; found by a security audit)**: the narrowed Codex rule was still a broad `doan` allow with listed exceptions instead of a list of what may run, and after the credential split a conversation's `doan remove` and `doan onboarding` read as `app_not_running`; the rule now allows only listed read and signal commands, the CLI refuses any argument before the command and refuses both commands in an app conversation before dialling (`conversation_refused`), and `plan.test.ts` checks argv shapes against the rule text, not only the text; `rg -n 'prefix_rule' app/src/main eval/lib -g '!*.test.ts'`.

### 2026-11-18 — The permission approval card showed the agent's own description of a request instead of what it would run

- **Area**: `app/src/main/conversation/transport.ts` (`claudePermissionSummary`, `claudePermissionRequest`, `codexPermission`), `acp.ts` (`acpPermission`), `app/src/renderer/ConversationThread.tsx` (the permission card); tests in `transport.test.ts`, `acpRunner.test.ts`, `ConversationThread.test.tsx`.
- **Symptom**: a permission card in Ask first could read only the agent-written text (Claude's Bash `description`, Codex's `reason`, an ACP tool call's `title`) while Allow approved the command, files or permissions it did not name; a long command was cut at 512 characters with no mark. The "always" choices (Claude's suggested permissions, Codex's proposed command rule) saved a rule the card never named. ARCHITECTURE §7 already said the approval card shows the command.
- **Root cause**: one `summary` string served both the activity line and the approval, and a readable `a || b || c` chain put the friendliest text first; nothing distinguished text the agent writes about an operation from the operation the approval releases. The tests pinned the friendly order, so the order looked intended.
- **Net**: caught by a security review of the agent-process boundary. Missed by the unit tests, which asserted the description won and never asserted that the card contains the command when a description is present.
- **Prevention**: an approval's `summary` is built only from the operation (command, paths, permission list) and cut with `clip` so a cut ends in "…"; agent-written text goes in `reason`, and a choice that saves anything carries `detail`. Tests pin both directions (`describe("approval card content")` in `transport.test.ts`, the ACP case in `acpRunner.test.ts`). Search: `rg -n 'text\((input|params)\.(description|reason)\) \|\||summary: (title|text\(params\.reason\))' app/src/main` — the remaining hit, `claudeToolSummary`, feeds only activity lines, which never carry a command.

### 2026-11-18 — A handoff line after a turn's end kept the unseen dot on the conversation the person was looking at

- **Area**: `app/src/main/conversation/summary.ts` (unseen), `app/src/renderer/overviewRun.ts` (`lastTurnSeq`, `endSeq`), now both on `app/src/main/conversation/seenEvents.ts` `toSee`; tests `overviewRun.test.ts` 'a handoff resolution or missed line after the turn's end…', `summary.test.ts`.
- **Symptom**: main's unseen skipped `handoff.resolved` and `handoff.missed`, but the renderer's copy of the rule skipped only `conversation.*` and `canvas.drawn`. A handoff line written after the thread loaded made the renderer's `endSeq` null (its last event to see was no longer a turn end), so `markSeen` was never sent and the dot stayed while the conversation was on screen.
- **Root cause**: the renderer kept its own copy of main's predicate with a comment pointing at main. When main's list grew (handoff lines, then `canvas.drawn`), only the event the change was about reached the copy; nothing tied the two lists together, and each side's tests used its own fixtures.
- **Net**: missed by unit tests on both sides (each pinned only its own list) and by runtime checks (a handoff needs a real agent). Caught by a whole-codebase audit comparing the two predicates.
- **Prevention**: a rule both main and the renderer apply lives in one renderer-safe module (type-only imports) that both import, or both sides' tests read one fixture; a comment saying "as in main's X" marks a copy to replace. Same unchecked copies of main's fold remain in `threadItems.ts` and `conversationStrip.ts` (the latter cannot import `state.ts`, which pulls `node:path`). Search: `rg -n "as in main's|main's own measure|like main's|main's [a-zA-Z.]+\.ts" app/src/renderer -g '!*.test.ts'`.

### 2026-11-18 — A timed-out inspector read's late cleanup turned the DOM agent off under the next read

- **Area**: `app/src/main/inspector.ts` (`createInspector`: the request queue, `run`'s `finally`), `inspector.test.ts`.
- **Symptom**: on a canvas whose CDP reads take over the 2 s budget, the next selection's read could fail as `unavailable` ("Couldn't read the styles.") although nothing was wrong with it, and superseded reads queued behind a slow one spent the newest read's budget the same way.
- **Root cause**: the queue was released on the raced, budgeted answer, while the timed-out run kept awaiting CDP. When its command answered, it went on sending reads and its `finally` sent `CSS.disable`/`DOM.disable` on the session the next run had just enabled. The "one request at a time" comment described the happy path only; every request in the queue also ran, even those the renderer would drop by `seq`.
- **Net**: caught by audit code reading; missed by `inspector.test.ts`, whose hung command never answered, so the late `finally` never ran, and whose fake debugger did not fail reads after `DOM.disable`.
- **Prevention**: a serialized queue that releases on a timeout must stop the timed-out run from sending more commands and from cleaning up shared state once a later run has started, and should skip queued work a newer request supersedes; its test resolves the hung command after the budget while the next run is mid-read, with a fake that fails reads after disable. Search: `rg -n "Promise\.race\(\[.*run\(" app/src/main --glob '!*.test.*'` (2026-11-18: `thumbnailWindow.ts` is safe, it discards the page and marks the job inactive on the limit).
- **Recurrence (2026-11-19, codebase audit)**: the cleanup checked that no later run had started once, then awaited `CSS.disable` before `DOM.disable`, so a run that started during that await still lost its DOM agent; a check that guards shared state is repeated after every await that precedes the next touch of that state; `rg -n -A2 "if \(.*=== ticket\)" app/src/main --glob '!*.test.*'`.

### 2026-11-18 — Connect signed in to a removed MCP server's old URL after an Add on the same host reused its name

- **Area**: `app/src/main/mcpConnectors/plans.ts` (`reconcilePlan`), `service.ts` (`claudeConnect`, `codexConnect`, `registered`, `prepare`); tests `plans.test.ts` 'reconcilePlan', `service.test.ts` 'a registration of another URL under a reused name' and 'Connect replaces a registration of another URL'.
- **Symptom**: a URL server removed while a provider had no Doan connection kept its registration there; an Add of another URL on the same host got the same `doan-<host>` name, so the provider's reconcile treated the old registration as known, and Connect ran `mcp login` (Codex) or skipped `mcp add` (Claude) on it. The row read Connected for the new URL while the agent's tools, and Codex's keychain key, belonged to the old one.
- **Root cause**: a registration was identified by its name alone, but a name is only unique within the connectors file at one moment: `connectorName` frees a name as soon as the file drops it, while the providers' registrations outlive the file entry until a reconcile. The provider lists already carried each registration's URL (`codexMcpServers`, `parseClaudeMcpList`, the `URL:` line of `claude mcp get`) and nothing compared it.
- **Net**: caught by the codebase audit, which traced the Remove-while-signed-out path into a later Add. Missed by the reconcile and Connect tests, which only used names whose URL never changed. An isolated probe (Claude Code 2.1.327, Codex 0.169.0) showed both CLIs print the registered URL string unchanged, so comparing it as is cannot misread a valid registration as stale.
- **Prevention**: anything that decides a provider registration is Doan's server must compare the registered URL with the stored one (`staleUrl` in `plans.ts`), not just the name; a test for it registers the old URL and adds the new one under the same name. A pending-logout record is safe by name because it keeps the URL it logs out. Not verified: the status read (`codexRead`, `claudeGet`) still reads a same-name registration's sign-in without comparing its URL, which shows only while a reconcile has failed. Search: `rg -n "name === (server\.)?name\b|\.some\([a-z]+ => [a-z]+\.name ===" app/src/main/mcpConnectors app/src/main/conversation/mcpStartup.ts --glob '!*.test.ts'`.

### 2026-11-18 — An open tooltip still took the click meant for the control under it: Radix's positioning box took it, not the capsule

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/ds/surfaces.css` (`.doan-tooltip`, `[data-radix-popper-content-wrapper]:has(> .doan-tooltip)`), `app/src/renderer/components/ui/tooltip.tsx`; test `ds/controls.test.ts` 'tooltips never take pointer events'.
- **Symptom**: with an inspector row's tooltip open (opened by hover or keyboard focus, placed under the row), a press or hover on the next rows hit the tooltip's box instead of the row. The same held for every shared tooltip on every screen: DESIGN 'Tooltip' says a press on a tooltip reaches the control underneath, and it did not.
- **Root cause**: the 2026-11-07 fix ("tooltips … never take clicks") gave the capsule `.doan-tooltip` `pointer-events: none`, but Radix's popper renders the capsule inside a positioned wrapper (`[data-radix-popper-content-wrapper]`, `min-width: max-content`) the capsule's size, and that wrapper kept the default `pointer-events`. The rule named the element that draws the tooltip, not the box that receives the pointer, and the pin read the CSS of the capsule only.
- **Net**: caught by the canvas inspector's `/design-sync` probe hit-testing the next row (`document.elementFromPoint`) under an open row tooltip, which returned the wrapper; Playwright's actionability check reported the same ("… intercepts pointer events"). Missed for eleven days by the unit test, which checked a CSS rule rather than what the pointer hits, and by every earlier real-app probe, which hovered the trigger rather than the control beside it.
- **Prevention**: a pointer rule for Radix portalled content (tooltip, popover, menu) is checked on the wrapper as well as the content: in a probe, open it over another control and hit-test that control (`document.elementFromPoint`), not the CSS. `ds/controls.test.ts` now pins the wrapper rule. Search: `rg -n "pointer-events: none" app/src/renderer --glob '*.css'` and check each rule that targets a Radix `Content` class also covers its `[data-radix-popper-content-wrapper]`.

### 2026-11-18 — Space after a click on a canvas toolbar tool pressed the tool instead of panning

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/CanvasTools.tsx`, `app/src/renderer/canvasTool.ts` (`toolShortcut`)
- **Symptom**: after a click on a canvas tool (Select, Hand, Picker, Capture area, Play prototype, Export canvas), Space pressed that tool again instead of panning; the same held in the export viewer's toolbar.
- **Root cause**: a mouse press on a `<button>` moves focus to it in Chromium, and `toolShortcut` rightly leaves Space to a focused control (`interactiveTarget`), so the pan rule and the default focus move collided only after a pointer press. The first fix put `onMouseDown` preventDefault on the toolbar `<div>`, but React events bubble through portals by component tree, so it would also have stopped mousedown in the frame list (`FrameListBody`, a Radix Popover portal rendered inside the toolbar's tree).
- **Net**: caught by a person who clicked a tool and then held Space (BACKLOG). Missed by the toolbar unit tests, which checked click actions and Tab stops but never where focus lands after a pointer press. The portal case was caught in the self-check by reading `FrameListBody`; no test covered it.
- **Prevention**: a canvas toolbar control sets `onMouseDown` preventDefault on each button, never on a container that renders portal content; `CanvasTools.test.tsx` checks every tool button. A new control layered over the canvas gets the same check, and a container-level React handler is checked against portals it renders. `grep -rn 'role="toolbar"\|Portal' app/src/renderer --include='*.tsx'`

### 2026-11-18 — A click on an export viewer tool left focus on it, so Space pressed the tool instead of panning

- **Area**: `cli/internal/export/viewer/shell.js` (the Select and Hand buttons), `app/src/main/exportViewer.test.ts`.
- **Symptom**: in an exported file opened in Chromium (and Playwright's WebKit), clicking Hand and then Select left focus on Select; holding Space then pressed the focused button, which `toolShortcut` rightly leaves alone as an interactive target, so the view did not pan.
- **Root cause**: Chromium focuses a button on a pointer press, while the key rules treat Space on a focused button as that button's press. Every tool click therefore turned the canvas's Space into a button key; nothing in the page separated focus that came by pointer from focus that came by keyboard.
- **Net**: found by hand and recorded in BACKLOG; missed by the unit tests, which pressed keys only after programmatic focus. The new real-DOM test in `exportViewer.test.ts` needed `window.focus()` on the viewer frame first: in `launchElectronDom` the viewer is a frame of the test window, and a press that moves no focus leaves the keys with that window, so the test went red for the wrong reason until the frame held the keys. A Playwright probe of a real `doan export` file (`.scratch/runtime-test/viewer-toolbar-focus/probe.mjs`) reproduced the bug in Chromium and WebKit before the fix and passed after it; real Safari, which does not focus a clicked button, was not run.
- **Prevention**: a control the person clicks and then expects a page key (Space, arrows) to act past takes `mousedown` `preventDefault()` so a pointer press does not move focus; `:active` still shows in Chromium and WebKit. In a real-DOM test of a page loaded as a frame, focus that frame's window before a pointer press that should keep focus. Search: `rg -n 'mousedown|onMouseDown' cli/internal/export/viewer app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'`.

### 2026-11-17 — Window activation put focus on Hide sidebar after the sidebar had recovered it in the background

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/ShellSidebar.tsx` (the focused row's recovery layout effect), `app/src/renderer/activationFocus.ts` (`refocusOnActivation`), main's `showWindow` for `doan open`.
- **Symptom**: with focus on a conversation row of the viewed canvas list, `doan open` of another canvas from a terminal ended with focus on the header's Hide sidebar button, not the old canvas row. Without window activation, or from an ordinary canvas row, focus stayed on the canvas row.
- **Root cause**: the sidebar moved focus to the canvas row while the window was in the background, but activation restores focus at the platform level to the element the page last had focus on when it lost window focus; that row was removed, so Chromium focused the first focusable control. `document.activeElement` already said the canvas row, so every check run before activation looked right.
- **Net**: recorded in BACKLOG by an earlier real-app probe; missed by every unit test, which has no window focus. The fix (re-focus on the next window `focus`, at once and after a task, cancelled by a pointer press) is covered by `activationFocus.test.ts` only; the real activation path was not probed again.
- **Prevention**: a focus recovery that can run while the window is in the background (`!document.hasFocus()`) goes through `refocusOnActivation`. Search for recoveries that only check the body: `rg -n "activeElement === document.body" app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'`; not verified: App's own recovery (`focusCurrent`) and the prototype window's frame focus can meet the same activation.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-18, audit)**: nine more recoveries (thread, docked composer and queue rows, Settings > Agents and MCP connectors, the canvas conversation list, the prototype window's reloaded frame, the inspector panel) focused in the background without it, missed because they test `activeElement !== body`, `!active` or a rAF state; they now hand focus through `useActivationFocus`/`focusThroughActivation` (`activationFocus.ts`); `rg -n "activeElement (===|!==) document\.body" app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'` and check each hit that moves focus against `rg -ln "useActivationFocus|refocusOnActivation" app/src/renderer` (not checked: ListRetryButton, ComposerParts, Onboarding, ProjectSettingsPage, App's `focusCurrent`).
- **Recurrence (2026-11-19, audit)**: the five places left unchecked above (App's `focusCurrent`, `focusResolved` and first run's composer hand-off, the Onboarding Agent step, the Project Settings page's title focus, `useRetry` and the presets popover's Retry), and the update popover's return to its button and the conversation strip's move to Dismiss, focused in the background; several never test the body at all (a `seq` arrival, a closed dialog, a resolved read), so the body search could not find them; they now go through `useActivationFocus` (App holds it in a ref so its focus functions stay out of effect dependencies), and a component that focuses from an animation frame or a promise callback without the helper is a candidate: `for f in $(rg -l 'requestAnimationFrame\(|\.then\(' app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'); do rg -q '\.focus\(' "$f" && ! rg -q 'useActivationFocus|focusThroughActivation|refocusOnActivation' "$f" && echo "$f"; done` (not checked: the native activation of every site, which no probe covered).
- **Recurrence (2026-11-20, main window problem heading)**: the heading took focus on an inactive arrival but skipped the activation hand-off. The regression test caught the missing replay even though native window switches kept the heading focused in both builds. Check individual heading calls even in components that already use the helper, and test that each replay finds the current heading only while the original scene still owns focus. Search: `rg -in '(heading|\bh2\b).*\.focus\(' app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'`.

### 2026-11-17 — The eval reader failed Codex runs that read presets from a Python heredoc, and its first fix read a list the call extends

- **Area**: `eval/lib/transcript.ts` (`withoutHeredocsAndComments`, `pythonDoanCalls`, `interpreterMayRunDoan`, `runGroups`), `eval/scenarios/D1/presets.ts` (`preset_reads_allowed`).
- **Symptom**: a Codex run that read only allowed presets through `subprocess.run(['doan', 'design-skill', …])` in a `python3 -` heredoc failed `preset_reads_allowed` (D10g, D10h), because any heredoc that names doan and starts a process made the read incomplete. The first fix, which reads literal argv lists, read `subprocess.run(['doan', 'design-skill'] + [name])` as the preset list and `[…][:3]` as the unsliced words, so an out-of-scope read would have passed.
- **Root cause**: Codex's command records hold only the processes Codex starts, so a call from inside a script exists only as script text, and the reader had no reading of Python. Matching a literal by its own closing bracket says nothing about the expression it sits in.
- **Net**: the fail-closed false failure was seen in real D10g/D10h rounds and kept in BACKLOG; the fail-open shape was caught by `/code-review` probing operators after the list. Missed by the first tests, which only varied what was inside the list, and by the transcript replay, whose recorded runs hold no Python-hosted doan call.
- **Prevention**: a static reader of a literal in another language accepts it only when the token after it ends the argument (`,`, `)`, `}`), as `execCalls` does for `cmd:`; its tests include an operator, a slice and a call after the literal, a comment and a string holding the call, and the same body under an inline script (`python3 -c … <<EOF`, where the heredoc is data). Search: `rg -n "literal\(tokens|PY_ARGV_CALL|JSON\.parse\(token" eval/lib --glob '!*.test.ts'` (2026-11-17: all three check the token after the literal).
- **Recurrence (2026-11-21, `eval/lib/transcript.ts` `NAMES_DOAN`; agent-eval D10g, D10h)**: Claude and Codex runs that wrote canvases from a `python3 - <<'PY'` heredoc failed `preset_reads_allowed` again, as an unreadable command, with only allowed presets read; the reader took canvas markup (`doan:canvas`, `doan:width`, `doan:` links) for the word doan, and the matcher now excludes `doan:` followed by a non-space; the shape of those runs was not kept, so keep the run directory of the next such fail before changing the reader again. `rg -n 'NAMES_DOAN =' eval/lib --glob '!*.test.ts'`
- **Recurrence (2026-11-21, agent-eval D10g and D10h)**: allowed reads failed beside a zsh loop and a generated Python script because the transcript described the parent shell. Exercise both an allowed read and an out-of-scope read through a child process on the run's PATH, and score the recorded argv. Check remaining shell-text consumers for rules that still need them: `rg -n 'ctx\.calls\.commands|transcript\.includes\("doan' eval --glob '!*.test.ts'`.

### 2026-11-17 — The prototype window's conversation panel showed 12-hour times with macOS set to 24-hour

- **Area**: `app/src/main/prototypeWindow.ts` (`new BrowserWindow`), `app/src/preload/prototype.ts`, `app/src/renderer/limitReset.ts` (`localClock`), `app/src/main/clockHourCycle.ts`.
- **Symptom**: with macOS's 24-hour time on, the prototype window's panel read reset and scheduled times as "3:00 PM" while the main window's panel read "15:00".
- **Root cause**: the hour cycle reaches a renderer only as a creation argument (`--doan-clock-hour12`) that the window's preload exposes, because the renderer's `Intl` resolves h12 for an en locale even with `AppleICUForce24HourTime` on. Main added it only to the main window's `webPreferences`, and only the main preload read it. `localClock()` reads it through an optional global (`window?.doan?.clockHour12`) with a fallback to `Intl`, so in the prototype window, which has `doanPrototype` and no `doan`, the read came back undefined and the fallback answered without any error.
- **Net**: found by reading the code paths for BACKLOG, not by a test: `localClock`'s test stubbed only `window.doan`, and the prototype window's tests never looked at its `webPreferences`. The fix pins both: the prototype window's creation arguments, its preload's `clockHour12`, and `localClock` with only `doanPrototype` present.
- **Prevention**: a value main passes per window at creation must be in every window whose renderer shares the code that reads it, and a renderer read of a preload value through an optional global must name each window's API, not fall back silently. Check `grep -rn "additionalArguments\|new BrowserWindow(" app/src/main | grep -v '\.test\.'` and `grep -rn "globalThis as" app/src/renderer | grep -v '\.test\.' | grep -i doan` when adding a creation argument or a second window. The thumbnail window (`thumbnailWindow.ts`) has no argument and needs none: it renders canvases only, never the app's renderer code.

### 2026-11-17 — A busy confirm or Retry still took the shared hover and pressed fills, so it looked pressable

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/ds/controls.css` (the shared `.doan-button` / `.doan-icon-button` hover and pressed rules); every busy action drawn with `retryButton` or `aria-busy` (Move to Trash, MCP Remove, the conversation Delete, Retry beside a failure line).
- **Symptom**: a busy confirm or Retry (`aria-disabled` + `aria-busy`, without `data-disabled`) kept its fill at rest but stepped to the hover fill under the pointer and to the pressed fill on press, while a press did nothing; a real-app probe of the Move to Trash dialog saw it.
- **Root cause**: the shared fills excluded only the states that existed when they were written (`:disabled`, then `[data-disabled]` for the disabled look on a focusable control). Busy moved from native `disabled` to `aria-disabled` + `aria-busy` to keep focus, but `aria-disabled` alone cannot be the exclusion (an unavailable control with a tooltip reason draws its own look), so the busy state needed its own exclusion and nothing pointed at the CSS when the markup changed.
- **Net**: caught by a real-app probe that hovered a busy confirm; missed by the controls CSS tests, which pinned each selector as written and never listed which states a fill must skip.
- **Prevention**: a new state attribute that makes a control unpressable is added to the shared fills' `:not(...)` list in the same change, and `ds/controls.test.ts` 'every shared hover and pressed fill skips a data-disabled or busy button' requires every fill line to carry the full list. Screen-level hover rules outside the shared ones: `rg -n ':hover:not\(:disabled\)|:active:not\(:disabled\)|:not\(:disabled\):hover' app/src/renderer --glob '*.css'` (2026-11-17: only the header update button, which never turns busy).

### 2026-11-17 — Removing a project or trashing a canvas left state keyed by it behind

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/App.tsx` (the menus' Remove from Doan, `confirmTrash`, `confirmDelete`), `app/src/renderer/homeCards.ts` (`groupRoots`), `app/src/main/index.ts` (`removeRoots`, the canvas trash wiring), `app/src/main/canvasTrash.ts`, `app/src/main/conversation/drafts.ts` (`starts`), `app/src/main/prototype/controller.ts`, `app/src/restore/uiState.ts` (`conversationByCanvas`).
- **Symptom**: four leftovers, none of them visible at the time. The sidebar, problem overlay and Project Settings Remove from Doan left a hidden auto-registered worktree in `folders.json` and `auto-folders.json`; a prototype window on a removed project stayed open; a quit-stopped start whose canvas was trashed or whose project was removed stayed in `drafts.json` forever, counting toward its limit; `conversationByCanvas` kept entries for trashed canvases and deleted conversations, so a device that saw enough canvases would pass ui-state's 64 KiB cap and every later ui-state save would be refused.
- **Root cause**: cleanup was written per store at the time each store was added, keyed to the paths that existed then. `removeRoots` cleared what it knew of (selection, background work, thumbnails), the canvas trash only moved the file, and later root- or canvas-keyed state (`starts`, the prototype window's target, `conversationByCanvas`) was never wired to either. The menus built the group from the renderer's `entries`, which `shownFolders` already filtered, so they could not see hidden roots that `doan remove` (main's full list) removes.
- **Net**: the four were listed in docs/BACKLOG.md 'Known defects' before this fix (how each was first noticed is not recorded); each leftover is silent until a limit is reached or a window lingers. Missed by unit tests that checked each store alone and the removal paths only against the state they already cleared. The new tests pin each cleanup at its trigger (`index.test.ts` removing and trash, `App.autoWorktrees.test.tsx`, `App.canvasTrash.test.tsx`, `App.conversationDelete.test.tsx`, `drafts.test.ts`, `controller.test.ts`), and a focused real-app probe checked all four.
- **Prevention**: extends the 2026-10-25 entry 'Locating a project kept the old root's selection'. A new store keyed by root or canvas wires its cleanup into `removeRoots` (root) and the `trashed` dependency of `createCanvasTrash` (canvas) in the same change, with a test at that trigger; a renderer action meant for the whole project takes its roots from main's full list (`allEntries`, `groupRoots`), never from the shown `entries`/`cards`. Search: `rg -n "function removeRoots|trashed:" app/src/main/index.ts` for the two triggers, and `rg -n "cards\.find\(.*rows\.map|card\.rows\.map\(row => row\.path\)" app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'` for group actions built from shown rows (the sidebar's `props.remove(current.rows…)` is safe because App expands it through `groupRoots`). Not verified: `canvas-seen.json`'s per-canvas records are not cleared by a Move to Trash.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-17, ui-state `conversationByCanvas`)**: entries for a conversation deleted in the prototype window or a canvas deleted outside Doan (terminal, agent, checkout) had no in-app trigger to clear them, so the map only grew; a device-local memory keyed by things that can go outside Doan is pruned against the read lists on every save (`withoutUnlisted`), never by an unread list; `rg -n "Record<string, Record<string, string>>" app/src/restore/uiState.ts`.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-18, a vanished auto-registered worktree and the renderer's stores)**: the shell's `autoRemoved` hook, a third root trigger, kept its own two-line copy of the cleanup and missed the starts, background work and prototype window added to `removeRoots`; the renderer's Remove dropped only the Overview's `root:` draft, leaving the canvas panel's `canvas:` drafts, `CanvasRun` and HomeRun's failed-list memory, and a draft dropped or replaced whole (Remove, Delete, Edit and resend, Edit on Home) gave back only a returned queue's chips, so pasted images stayed until quit. Both main triggers now call one `rootsLeft`, App's `forgetConversations` calls each run's `forgetRoots`, and a whole draft gives back every chip; `rg -n "autoRemoved|function rootsLeft|function removeRoots" app/src/main/index.ts` and `rg -n "forgetRoots\(" app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'`.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-19, launch order)**: `rootsLeft` reached the draft store through an optional reference that main assigned only after `startShell` and two awaits, while the shell's first folder reads can already call `autoRemoved`; a store a root trigger cleans is created before the shell that fires the trigger starts, with an `index.test.ts` case that calls `autoRemoved` inside `startShell`; `rg -n "^let \w+: [\w<>]+ \| undefined;" app/src/main/index.ts` and check each one `rootsLeft` uses.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-19, ui-state `withoutUnlisted`)**: the ID-less canvas row case was kept only when the conversation list was read; with that list still loading or failed, the canvas list dropped the entry; a prune that needs two lists to vouch for an entry drops nothing on either list's word while the other is unread; `rg -n "withoutUnlisted\(" app/src --glob '!*.test.*'`.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-20, draft restoration)**: Remove cleared stored drafts while a start recovery could still append its reply or its newly issued files to the same key. Edit on Home also left newly issued handles behind when navigation or a missing provider prevented delivery. Delay the take reply and attachment issuance independently, remove and re-add the root, and check that an abandoned recovery releases only its own handles, including while Home waits for a send to settle. Search with `rg -n 'restoreStartDraft\(|await draftFrom\(|await this.rebuild\(' app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'`.

### 2026-11-17 — A quit before main accepted a send lost the message, and Restart to Update did not wait for it

- **Area**: `app/src/main/conversation/service.ts` (`start`, `send`/`submit`, `restartHolds`), `drafts.ts` (`sending`, `starts`), the new conversation composers (`HomeComposerView.tsx`, `conversationStore.ts` `takeStartDraft`).
- **Symptom**: a send or start sets its turn busy at once, while the composer shows Stop and the sidebar spinner, but main writes `user.message` and `turn.started` only after preparing and opening the runner (0.1 s to several seconds with real CLI and MCP startup). `restartHolds` counted only accepted turns, so Restart to Update went ahead in that window; the quit then lost Home's first message entirely (no conversation existed yet), and a direct follow-up send left nothing in history or drafts.
- **Root cause**: "running" had two meanings. The renderer and `working()` counted a turn from the press, while `restartHolds` and the appQuit write counted it from acceptance; between the two, only the composer's memory held the message, and every quit path assumed the history or the queue file already had it. Sends that wait for a judgement or a previous process were outside both counts.
- **Net**: caught by an outside user report, reproduced with a fake `claude` that delays `initialize` and a Playwright quit. Missed by the unit tests, which pinned `restartHolds` "from acceptance to the end" and checked quits only on accepted turns or on queued messages (already on disk).
- **Prevention**: a message the composer let go must be on disk until acceptance or a refusal outside a quit (`drafts.hold`/`release`); a regression test quits between `open` and acceptance and restarts the service (`service.quitPreAccept.test.ts`). Every gate that asks "is a turn running" must count busy-before-acceptance and waiting sends as `restartHolds` does now; review each remaining accepted-only check against that: `grep -rn "accepted && live.busy" app/src/main | grep -v "\.test\."`.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-18, Move to Trash, `doan remove` and the start draft composers)**: `trashBusy` read only the conversation list and `cliRemoveDecision` only `liveTurns`, both of which name a conversation from acceptance, so a canvas could be trashed or its project removed while its first message was prepared; main now reports `startingCanvases` and `pendingRoots` and the renderer's `trashBlock` reads `CanvasRun.startingOn`. On the renderer side a take-once mark set before the reply was never cleared after an error, and Home's reply landed in a field whose send was still out, where that send's settle kept it under the other project; a failed take is asked again, and a reply during a send waits for it to settle. The remaining accepted-only readers are `doan work`'s, documented so: `rg -n "liveTurns\(\)|conversations\?\.list\(" app/src/main/index.ts`.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-20, busy trash plan)**: the initial refusal outlived the work in the open dialog. Release the work, delay the renewed plan past a target switch, and verify that only a fresh press on the current canvas can move it. Search: `rg -n 'applyTrashPlan|confirmTrash|trashWorking' app/src/renderer/App.tsx`.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-18, `service.ts` `start`/`send` after an aborted quit)**: a quit whose appQuit write failed aborts and leaves the service quitting while the composers stay open, so each press after the busy refusal held another copy and the next launch returned the message several times; while quitting each composer now holds one message under one key (`quitHolds`), pinned in `service.quitPreAccept.test.ts` 'after an aborted quit'; `rg -n "drafts\.hold\(" app/src/main --glob '!*.test.ts'` and confirm a hold made while quitting replaces its composer's earlier one.

### 2026-11-17 — The prototype window stayed blank when its first read failed

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/prototype/PrototypeWindow.tsx` (the first `api.read()`), `prototype/view.ts` (`FIRST_READ_SCENE`).
- **Symptom**: when the window's first read of main's view rejected, the content panel stayed empty with Reload, Fit, Responsive and Export disabled and no message; only a later `onChanged` from main could bring it back.
- **Root cause**: the read's rejection handler was `() => {}`. Every scene the window draws comes from main's view phase, and a failed read has no view and so no phase: the empty handler left the window in its "no view yet" state, which draws nothing, with no path out.
- **Net**: found by a source audit of cross-screen recovery; missed by the window's tests, which only resolved the first read. Now pinned by `PrototypeWindow.test.tsx` 'a failed first read' and `WindowShell.test.tsx` (scene, busy Retry), and a real-app probe that makes `prototype:read` reject.
- **Prevention**: a read that a screen cannot draw without gets a failure state with a Retry, never an empty rejection handler; an empty handler is allowed only where a later event or read covers the same state. `rg -n --glob '!*.test.*' ', \(\) => \{\}\)' app/src/renderer` lists the remaining empty rejection handlers. Safe ones: first run's catch-up read of a completion (the session event still delivers it and the step stays usable), the read-state check on entering a project (a missed problem toast only; problems also arrive as events) and an MCP row's Check again (the row keeps its state and its button).
- **Recurrence (2026-11-17, MCP Connectors and Add Projects)**: the MCP Connectors page's entry read rejecting before any snapshot left the page blank with Add unavailable (now "Couldn’t read the MCP connectors." with a busy Retry), and a rejected read of a retired onboarding operation left the setup or clone step waiting forever (now asked again on the next published state); both pinned by tests (`SettingsMcpConnectors.test.tsx`, `projectStep.test.ts`).
- **Recurrence (2026-11-20, Add Projects session)**: rejection or an unsuccessful first response left the panel blank because only success mounted the form. Hold the first read through failure, repeated retry, departure and success, and verify that shell focus survives a late reply. Search: `rg -n 'readAddPage\(' app/src/renderer`.

### 2026-11-17 — A missing agent CLI read "Not available right now" on Home and in Settings, while its Agents row said Not installed

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/agentAuthView.ts` (`providerShortStatus`, `providerUnavailableReason`), `SettingsAgents.tsx` (New conversations), `quickOpen.ts` (`unavailableCanvasMessage`), `homeComposer.ts`, `problemView.ts`, `App.tsx` (`replyChipRefusal`).
- **Symptom**: a connected provider whose CLI was missing showed "Not available right now" in Home's agent picker and "<agent> isn’t available right now" under Settings › New conversations; only a refused send said it wasn’t installed. Project-problem toasts said "Project is unavailable" without the name the Home composer used for the same state.
- **Root cause**: the short status switched on readiness causes with a `default` branch, so a cause it did not list fell silently to the vague line. Settings' sentence was a second hand-written switch over the same causes, commented "same causes as the picker", so neither could drift loudly; the project toasts likewise kept their own copy of the composer's sentences.
- **Net**: caught by a cross-screen UX audit comparing wording per state. Missed by unit tests, which pinned each function's strings on its own and listed `cli_missing` among the "Not available right now" cases.
- **Prevention**: wording for one state that two screens show comes from one function or table (`BLOCKED` in `agentAuthView.ts`, `projectReadingLine`/`projectUnavailableLine` in `problemView.ts`); a test names every cause the function maps, so a new cause cannot hide in a `default`. Search for remaining twins with `grep -rnE "default: return \"|same causes? as|keeps its own copy" app/src/renderer --exclude='*.test.*'` (the URL-validation default in `mcpConnectorsView.ts` is a single-screen message, not a twin).
- **Fix**: a6ea89faa — one source for agent-unavailable and project-problem wording.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-20, agent picker)**: minimum-version failure and protocol incompatibility shared Needs an update and Update even though the latter opened a Check again notice. The picker and fixed-default warning now name the separate causes, and tests compare each picker action with the matching Settings row. Existing conversations keep their own Update recovery. Search: `rg -n "version_unsupported|version_incompatible" app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'`.

### 2026-11-17 — A base rule an audit called dead still coloured and positioned the sidebar rows

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/styles.css` (`.row`, `.err`, `.shell-side .row`), `app/src/renderer/ds/controls.css` (shared hover and pressed fills).
- **Symptom**: a UX audit listed the legacy `.row { … }` and `.err { … }` base rules as unused because heavier rules override them. Deleting them as the audit said would have turned the project and canvas rows from `text-subtle` to the inherited standard ink and dropped their `position: relative` and `white-space: nowrap`, because `.shell-side .row` sets display, size, padding and font but not those three. Deleting `.err` changed the `font-variant-numeric` of `.ob-chip.err` and `small.err` from `normal` to the inherited `tabular-nums` (the `font` shorthand had reset it). The same audit found per-screen `[data-disabled]:hover` patches that existed only because the shared hover and pressed fills excluded `:disabled` but not `[data-disabled]`.
- **Root cause**: "overridden" was judged rule by rule from the selectors, not property by property on the elements. A shorthand (`font`) or a property that the heavier rule never sets (`color`, `position`, `white-space`) leaves the lighter rule live. The `row` class token is still on every sidebar tree row, so the lighter rule matched real elements. The disabled patches came from the shared rules naming only the native state, while DESIGN's busy and refused controls use `aria-disabled` + `data-disabled` to keep focus.
- **Net**: caught by checking which sidebar rules set `color`, `position` and `white-space` before deleting, then by a real-app probe that compares computed styles with the deleted rules inserted back at index 0 of the same stylesheet. Missed by the audit's static reading and by unit tests, which pin rule text, not computed values.
- **Prevention**: before deleting a CSS rule as dead, list the elements it matches in the real app and diff their computed styles with and without it (`sheet.insertRule(<old rule>, 0)` in a runtime probe); move every property that differs into the surviving rule. A state that keeps focus (`data-disabled`) is excluded wherever its native counterpart is, in the same `:not()`. Search for screen patches that restore the disabled fill: `grep -rnE 'data-disabled\]:(hover|is\(:hover|active)' app/src/renderer --include='*.css'`; light base rules whose class is still a secondary token on rendered elements: `grep -nE '^\.[a-z][a-z0-9-]* \{' app/src/renderer/styles.css`, then grep the class in `className` strings.

### 2026-11-17 — Retry cleared its own failure line before the request answered, so the button and focus vanished

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/ListRetryButton.tsx` (`retryButton`, `useRetry`), `SettingsAbout.tsx`, `SettingsNotifications.tsx`, `SettingsAgents.tsx`, `BuddyPicker.tsx`, `conversationStore.ts` (`retryThread`), `ConversationThread.tsx`, `HomeComposerView.tsx`, `DockedComposer.tsx`; the Move to Trash and MCP Remove confirmations (`App.tsx`, `CanvasTrashDialog.tsx`, `SettingsMcpConnectors.tsx`).
- **Symptom**: pressing Retry under a settings save or read failure, the thread read error or a composer notice reset the failure state at once (`setFailed(null)`, `load(true)` clearing the read, the thread going back to `loading`, a send clearing `notice`), so the line and the Retry unmounted while the request was out and keyboard and VoiceOver focus fell to `<body>`. A failure that came back redrew the same line with focus already lost.
