Move to Trash and MCP Remove closed before main answered, so a failure arrived later as a toast or a row line away from where it was asked.
- **Root cause**: each screen wrote its own Retry as "clear the error, then ask again", the simplest state update, while only the conversation list Retry followed DESIGN 'Busy action'; no shared control carried the busy model, so every new failure line copied the clearing pattern. Separately, a focus fallback scoped to the Retry's row or section found no other control on About, whose page-level Retry sits outside every section, and dropped focus to `<body>` on success.
- **Net**: the UX audit found the clearing by reading sources; unit tests on each screen asserted only that Retry called the request again, never what stayed on screen while it ran. The real-app probe caught the About success fallback, which the hook-mocked unit tests could not (they have no DOM to focus).
- **Prevention**: a Retry beside a failure line uses `retryButton` with `useRetry` (or a store state such as the thread's `retrying`) and clears its line only when the request succeeds; a test holds the request open and asserts the line and a busy Retry are still there. Find Retries drawn by hand and failure states cleared before the request: `rg -n '>Retry</button>|setFailed\((null|false)\)' app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'`. Not covered yet: the preset popover's Retry (`ComposerParts.tsx`, `view.kind === "error"`) still swaps to its loading state while the list is read again. A busy Retry's success focus is checked in a real-app probe, not only with mocked hooks.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-17, preset popover and `/` list Retry)**: the preset list's Retry (Home and the docked composer) still cleared the reply to its loading state, so the line and the Retry unmounted; it now keeps the failed reply while the read is out (a separate `retryPresets`/`loadPresets` from the popover-open read, which still starts from loading), with one `useRetry` owned by the composer so the popover and the `/` list show the same read, and focus goes to the popover's search box on success; `rg -n 'presets: undefined|this\.presets = undefined' app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'`.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-19, canvas load failure Retry)**: the canvas page's "Couldn’t load the canvas" Retry ran a reload that turned the controller's status to `loading` at once, so the line and the Retry unmounted and focus fell back to the canvas region before the reload answered; a failure line drawn from a status its own Retry changes keeps a host-side Retry state (`canvasRetry.ts`) that draws the line through that status until the request answers; `rg -n 'status === "failed" \?|>Retry</button>' app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'`.

### 2026-11-17 — A cut Quick Open detail never showed its tooltip: the span measured was inside the box that clips

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/NameTip.tsx` (`isCut`), `app/src/renderer/QuickOpenDialog.tsx` (`.qo-detail`, `.qo-context`).
- **Symptom**: a Quick Open result whose second line ended in an ellipsis showed no tooltip on hover, while the name above it did.
- **Root cause**: a name's tooltip opens only while the name is cut, measured as the trigger (or a box inside it) being wider than it shows. The trigger was the inline `.qo-context` span; the ellipsis belongs to its parent `.qo-detail`, and an inline box has no scroll or client width of its own, so the measure was always "fits". Static markup tests cannot see widths.
- **Net**: caught by the real-app probe that hovered a cut Quick Open line; missed by the unit tests, which fake the widths, and by the markup tests, which only show where the trigger sits.
- **Prevention**: a cut-only tooltip's trigger is the element with `overflow: hidden` and `text-overflow: ellipsis`, or contains it; never a child of it. When adding one, check the trigger's CSS and hover a cut instance in a probe. Search: `rg -n "<NameTip" app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'` and look up the trigger class of each hit in `styles.css` for `text-overflow`.

### 2026-11-17 — A docked attachment refusal was cleared before it drew

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/DockedComposer.tsx` (`edit`, the shape layout effect, `reportFor`).
- **Symptom**: an attachment refusal the host reported to the docked composer (ten files and the limit line; a refused file taken out of an empty draft) never showed: the chips changed, the notice was gone in the same render.
- **Root cause**: the layout effect that brings the stored shape in line with the drawn one called `edit({}, key)`, the person's edit path, which also drops the shown refusal. A host that reports a refusal and changes the chips in one batch changes the shape too, so the catch-up looked like the person answering the refusal.
- **Net**: caught by an independent code review tracing a capsule turning into a box. Missed by the composer tests, which reported the refusal on a draft with text and changed no chips, and by the real-app probe, which checked only that some refusal showed after a drop, not the limit line.
- **Prevention**: state sync that runs from an effect (shape, measured wrap) never goes through a handler that clears transient state meant for the person's own edits; pass the sync through a separate path. A test of a host-reported notice starts from an empty draft and has the host add and remove chips in the same step. Search: `rg -n -A3 'useLayoutEffect\(\(\) => \{' app/src/renderer/*Composer*.tsx | rg 'edit\('`.

### 2026-11-17 — A new IPC request was refused in the real app while every unit test passed

- **Area**: `app/src/main/index.ts` (`handle`), `app/src/main/senderAllowed.ts` (`mainOnly`), `app/src/preload/index.ts`.
- **Symptom**: the Retry of the failed project-list save (`folders:save-again`) always answered "not saved"; the toast came back with Retry even after the folder was writable again.
- **Root cause**: `handle()` answers a channel only when `senderAllowed` lists it for the sending window, and refuses everything else with the handler's fallback value (`false` here), which is also a valid answer. Adding the handler and the preload call compiles and looks complete; the allowlist is a third place nothing points to.
- **Net**: caught by the real-app `/runtime-test` probe (make the userData folder read-only, remove a project, make it writable, press Retry). Missed by the shell, App and preload unit tests, which call `Shell.saveAgain` directly or mock `window.doan`, so no test crossed `ipcMain.handle`; `senderAllowed.test.ts` lists channels by hand and had no entry for the new one.
- **Prevention**: a new `handle("<channel>", …)` adds the channel to `senderAllowed.ts` and to the matching list in `senderAllowed.test.ts` in the same change, and its runtime probe presses the control in the built app rather than calling main directly. To list handled channels no window may send: collect `rg -o 'handle\("[^"]+"' app/src/main/index.ts` and check each with `senderAllowed(channel, "main") || senderAllowed(channel, "prototype")` (none missing after this fix; channels registered through other modules, such as `AUTH_CHANNELS`, are not in that search).

### 2026-11-17 — The same conversation read as finished in its row while Delete said the agent was working

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/conversationList.ts` (`conversationWork`), `app/src/renderer/conversationDelete.ts` (`deleteAvailability`), `app/src/main/conversation/summary.ts` (`working`).
- **Symptom**: a conversation whose turn had ended but whose queue was about to go out, or whose project turn waited for its promotion judgment, showed "Conversation finished — unread" in the sidebar, the title menu and the Home strip, while its Delete was unavailable with "The agent is working on this conversation."; between queued turns the sidebar spinner went off while the board indicator stayed.
- **Root cause**: a summary carries two working signals, the history's `phase` and main's `working`, which also covers preparing, a queue about to go out and a turn awaiting its judgment. The list's slot read only `phase`; Delete, the board indicator, the Show conversation badge and the composer read `phase || working`. Each reader picked a signal on its own, and the summary type does not say which one means "working" for the person.
- **Net**: caught by the cross-screen UX audit comparing the two rules. Missed by unit tests of each reader, which built summaries with `working` matching `phase` (`working: false` with `phase: "ended"`), never the mixed state.
- **Prevention**: a reader that decides whether a conversation works reads `phase === "running" || working` (waiting stays its own state), and its tests include `{ phase: "ended", working: true }`. Find readers that use `phase` alone: `rg -n 'phase === "running"' app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'` and check that each also reads `working` or says why not. `steerPhaseOf` in `conversationStore.ts` is a safe exception: it reads `working` after `phase` as its own `holding` phase, because Send cannot steer a turn that has ended.

### 2026-11-16 — Eval agents and judges ran with the user's own global instruction files

- **Area**: `eval/lib/agent.ts` (`isolateUserInstructions`), `eval/lib/workspace.ts` (`prepareWorkspace`, `keepCodexSessions`), `eval/run.ts` (`judge`, `keepEvidence`).
- **Symptom**: a full round's D7b-codex, and both D7b and one D5 Codex repeat, answered "Say “구현 시작해”" and changed nothing: the rule came from the maintainer's `$CODEX_HOME/AGENTS.md`, which every Codex run and every Codex judge loaded. The runs failed their scenario checks and read like a guide problem.
- **Root cause**: the runner kept the user's agent homes on purpose (sign-in and model settings live there) and isolated only session ids, MCP servers and PATH; Codex 0.167 reads `$CODEX_HOME/AGENTS.override.md` or `AGENTS.md` with no setting to skip them (`--ignore-user-config` skips only `config.toml`), and Claude Code loads `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` or `$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR/CLAUDE.md` with user settings. Nothing in a run's evidence names which instruction files were loaded.
- **Net**: caught when classifying the round's failures by reading the final answer and finding the rule's source in the rollout; missed by the self-test (fixture agents load no instructions) and by the real-state guard (it watches what a run writes, not what it reads).
- **Prevention**: every process the runner starts as an agent or judge gets its environment from `isolateUserInstructions`: Codex a CODEX_HOME of its own linking only the user's `auth.json`, `auth.lock` and `config.toml` (links, because Codex writes refreshed tokens in place through `auth.json` and coordinates through `auth.lock`; verified with a symlinked `auth.json` and `codex login --with-api-key` in a temporary home), Claude `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_CLAUDE_MDS=1` (it keeps user settings, so the model stays; no seed may carry a project memory file, `agent.test.ts`). When an agent version adds a user-level instruction or context source (a new memory file, a plugin hook that injects context), check whether a run loads it. User-level plugins, skills and hooks still reach Claude runs (user settings load); they are not isolated here. `rg -n "agentEnvironment\(process.env\)|isolateUserInstructions" eval --glob '!*.test.ts'` should list only uses that pass through `isolateUserInstructions`.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-18, `eval/lib/workspace.ts` `prepareWorkspace`, `eval/lib/agent.ts` `isolateUserInstructions`; found by the round-1 `/audit`)**: the run's own CODEX_HOME sat under the workspace in `/tmp`, which Codex's `workspace-write` sandbox opens to the agent's shell together with its folder and `$TMPDIR` (codex-cli 0.169.0 `codex sandbox -P :workspace`), while Codex itself writes tokens through that home's links and keeps the rollout read as evidence there; and the locks were linked only when present, though Codex creates `auth.lock` and `auth.json.lock` only at a sign-in or refresh. The home now lives under `/var/tmp` (closed to that sandbox, short for Codex's `ipc/ipc.sock`) and both locks are linked whenever `auth.json` is; before handing a sandboxed agent a path that an unsandboxed process trusts, probe the sandbox's writable roots with `codex sandbox -P :workspace -- touch <path>`. Not fixed: the rest of the workspace (`bin/doan`, the fake app's `rt/` logs read as evidence) is still under `/tmp`, because a worktree scenario's agent writes the main repository's `.git` there (ARCHITECTURE §14: the eval is not a sandbox); the judge's home under `/tmp` is safe because the judge runs `--sandbox read-only`. `rg -n 'mkdtempSync\(' eval --glob '!*.test.ts'`.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-19, `eval/run.ts` `judge`; found by the round-2 `/audit`)**: the Codex judge's CODEX_HOME, with links to the user's sign-in, stayed under `/tmp` on the reasoning that the judge itself runs `--sandbox read-only`, but the sandbox that matters is the one of the other processes running at the same time: the two agents' lanes run side by side, so a Claude run's Codex judge lived while Codex agents ran under `workspace-write`. Every Codex home is now made by `outsideCodexSandbox` (`/var/tmp`); when judging whether a path is safe from a sandboxed agent, check every process that runs concurrently, not the owner's own sandbox. `rg -n 'mkdtempSync\(|outsideCodexSandbox\(' eval --glob '!*.test.ts'` (the self-test CLI build, the workspace base and R8d's layout directory remain under `/tmp`).

### 2026-11-16 — Process runners settled on the exit event and dropped output still in the pipe; the "flaky" machine tests were this race

- **Area**: `app/src/main/onboarding/machine.ts` (`runProcess`), `app/src/main/agentAuth/manager.ts` (`spawnAuthProcess`, read by `AuthManager.exec` and the MCP connectors' `run`); tests `machine.test.ts`, `process.test.ts`, `manager.test.ts`.
- **Symptom**: CI runs read `echo eof` as `{ stdout: "", exitCode: 0 }` in 37 ms, and `machine.test.ts` failed now and then in two audit rounds, each time retried as a flaky test. The same code reads the `--version` before a run, the login-shell probe and legacy external runs, so a lost line misreports as `runtime_unsupported`, `session_unavailable` or `cli_missing`; a provider status or MCP command whose group was gone in the leader's exit tick returned cut JSON (`check_failed`).
- **Root cause**: Node emits a child's `exit` when it is reaped, independently of its pipes; data the child wrote before exiting can still be unread and arrive in a later I/O callback. `runProcess` resolved on `exit` and destroyed the pipes, a choice made so that a daemon inheriting a pipe could not strand the caller, with no bound that kept the late output. `spawnAuthProcess` resolved `gone` from the group's ESRCH in the same callback, so `exec` returned two microtasks later. The ordering depends on load, which is why it surfaced only on busy CI runners and read as a flaky test.
- **Net**: caught by the round-5 audit (lifecycle and contracts reviewers) from the CI logs of the failed runs. Missed by every test, which wrote and exited at once on an idle machine, and by the earlier rounds, which treated the failures as timing noise.
- **Prevention**: a runner returns a child's output only after its pipes close (`close`, or both streams' `close`), bounded by a short grace after exit (500 ms) for a daemon that keeps a pipe; a deadline that falls in that grace reports the exit, not a timeout. Tests use a helper that writes after the leader exits (`(sleep 0.2; echo late) &`, or a `setsid` writer for a group), which holds the race open every time, plus a daemon that keeps the pipe to pin the bound. A test that fails intermittently on output is read as a product race until shown otherwise. Search: `rg -n 'once\("exit"|on\("exit"|onExit\(' app/src/main -g '!*.test.ts'` (not verified here: `conversation/runners.ts` and `acpRunner.ts` call `transportDied` on exit; `clone.ts` keeps stderr as message text only; the pty spawner's output is diagnostics only).
- **Recurrence (2026-11-20, Codex and ACP conversations, agent authentication and Git clone)**: leader exit could discard the last turn, auth reply or clone diagnostic. Group cleanup starts at exit, but RPC deadlines and service exit must let buffered replies settle first. Drive leader exit, pipe delivery and group disappearance independently in fake-child tests. Cross an RPC deadline during the 500 ms drain and keep a group alive past it to check ownership. Search with `rg -n 'child\.exit\.then|once\("exit"|transportDied' app/src/main -g '!*.test.ts'`.

### 2026-11-16 — A stricter "refused" rule in the eval reader turned every redirected preset read into a refused command

- **Area**: `eval/lib/transcript.ts` (`parse`, `runGroups`, `readDoanArgs`/`shapeAccepted`, new `readCommands`), `eval/scenarios/D4b/base.ts` (`writesProfile`), every check that reads doan commands through `doanArgs`/`doanPositionals`.
- **Symptom**: after the reader started refusing argument shapes the CLI refuses (extra operands), `doan design-skill document --json > /tmp/x` read as `design-skill document > /tmp/x` with three operands, and `… 2>&1` split at the `&` into a refused command and a command `1`; required preset reads (D5, D10g, D11e, D13 …) failed, `no_preset_body_read` and `preset_reads_allowed` passed beside a body read, open/export checks missed redirected commands. Replaying the recorded transcripts showed 37 Claude runs whose preset reads changed.
- **Root cause**: the tokenizer never knew redirections: they stayed words (and `&` in `>&`/`&>` split the command), which was harmless while extra words were ignored; the new shape rule made those words decide the verdict. D4b had meanwhile grown its own redirect guessing over quote-stripped words, so a quoted `'>'` read as an operator.
- **Net**: caught by the round-4 `/audit`, which ran the reader on a redirected read; missed by the unit tests, whose fixtures were the shapes being refused and never a real agent's command line with a redirection, and by the eval self-test, whose fixtures have none either.
- **Prevention**: a change that makes the reader refuse or drop more commands is replayed against the recorded transcripts in `.scratch/agent-eval/` with the old and the new reader, and every command whose words, refusal or completeness flips is read before merging; fixtures for a reader change include the suffixes agents actually append (`> file`, `2>&1`, `2>/dev/null`, `| head`, a heredoc). Redirections now leave the words in `parse` and reach checks only through `readCommands`; a check never infers an operator from a word. `rg -n "shapeAccepted|refused = true|redirect" eval --glob '*.ts' --glob '!*.test.ts'` (2026-11-16: only the reader and D4b, which reads `readCommands`).
- **Recurrence (2026-11-16, `eval/scenarios/D4b/base.ts`, `eval/lib/transcript.ts`; found by the round-5 `/audit`)**: D4b still matched `~`/`$HOME` on quote-stripped words, so `'~/.zshrc'` and `'$HOME/.zshrc'` failed it and `D=$HOME; echo x >> "$D/.zshrc"` passed; the tokenizer split `\>` and `[[ a > $b ]]` into redirections; and an interpreter fed a heredoc, a script file or a pipe read as complete. The first fix marked every heredoc that named doan, which the replay showed turning 11 recorded document-editing runs incomplete, and its home copy dropped loop variables (`~/$f`), which a counterexample probe in review caught. The reader now gives each word and target a `homePath` where the shell expands into the home directory (variables and `export` assigned just the home directory included) and checks read that instead of the spelling; a heredoc counts only when it names doan and starts a process. `rg -n '\(\?:~\||\\\$HOME' eval --glob '!*.test.ts'` should list only D4b's `PROFILE`, which file-tool paths use (no shell expands them).
- **Recurrence (2026-11-18, `eval/lib/transcript.ts` `withoutHeredocsAndComments`; found by the round-1 `/audit`)**: a backslash-newline line continuation split a command, so a continued `doan design-skill audit \` read as a refused call and `--json` as its own program (D1 passed beside a real preset read, O3's `no_doan_init` missed `doan \⏎init`, continued export/open calls failed); no fixture had a continuation although 12 recorded commands used one. The reader now drops the pair outside single quotes before splitting words, as the shell does, and the replay of 957 recorded runs changed only 11 `sed` continuations into one command each; reader fixtures include a continued command. `rg -n '\[;&\|\\n\]' eval --glob '!*.test.ts'` (only the reader's tokenizer).

### 2026-11-16 — Every Copilot process ran JavaScript an agent could rewrite in its profile, outside the fence

- **Area**: `app/src/main/agentAuth/fence.ts` (`fencedEverywhere`, `profileFencedCommand`, formerly `claudeFencedCommand`), `agentAuth/manager.ts` (`exec`), `agentAuth/usageRead.ts` (`rpcRead`), `conversation/prepare.ts` (the list-read fence and the `--version` check); tests `fence.test.ts`, `manager.test.ts`, `usageRead.test.ts`, `prepareFence.test.ts`.
- **Symptom**: a real-app decoy probe on develop planted a wrapper `index.js` in the Copilot profile's package cache (`Library/Caches/copilot/pkg/darwin-arm64/<version>/`), a `SessionStart` and a `UserPromptSubmit` hook under `.copilot/hooks/` and a server in `.copilot/mcp-config.json`, as a fenced Copilot turn can. The usage read at launch and on refresh, the status check (`copilot -p`), the model list read, `copilot login` and the `--version` check before a run all ran the planted code outside every fence; it read `conversations/` and listed the vault. Cursor's model list read started a server from the profile's `.cursor/mcp.json` the same way, with no approval recorded. Cursor's status check and `--version` and Antigravity's status check and list read ran none of their planted files.
- **Root cause**: the fence assumed that "the ACP providers always run inside this fence", so their profiles got no settings rule, while every process Doan starts for them outside a conversation ran unfenced on the old reasoning that it runs no model tool. Copilot is a loader that imports its own JavaScript from a cache under HOME, which Doan sets to the profile, and picks the newest version there, with no signature check; the package is code, not settings, so no settings list would have named it. `--version` was thought safe from the loader's strings (it returns before the import), which holds only when no newer package is cached.
- **Net**: caught by the round-3 audit (boundaries, contracts, lifecycle and maintainability reviews, from the loader's strings) and confirmed by the decoy probe; the fixed build's probe found the `--version` path, because the wrapper of the cached 1.0.112 package fired during a list read from a process that was not the ACP server. Missed by round 1's probe of these providers, which planted only repository configuration, never the profile.
- **Prevention**: before leaving a provider process unfenced, list the code it loads as well as the configuration: a CLI that loads its own code from HOME, a cache or an update directory under the profile runs agent-written code, so every process of that provider runs in the outer fence (`fencedEverywhere`), `--version` included; probe with a wrapper that records `sandbox_check` and then hands over to the real file, so the CLI keeps working and every process that loads it shows. Every list read but Codex's runs in the outer fence like a run. Search: `rg -n "options\.spawn\(|\bspawn\(|runProcess\(" app/src/main/agentAuth app/src/main/conversation app/src/main/mcpConnectors -g '!*.test.ts'` and check each provider spawn with the profile as HOME or config dir reaches `fencedCommand` or `profileFencedCommand`. Still unfenced, probed: Cursor's status and `--version`, Antigravity's status (`manager.ts` `acpExec`), Claude's auth commands; not probed: Cursor's and Antigravity's sign-in, and Cursor's Node compile cache under the profile. The tool facts' `--version` runs with the real HOME, whose own Copilot cache an outer-fenced agent can write too (BACKLOG 'Unfenced Doan processes').
- **Recurrence (2026-11-16, round-4 audit)**: Cursor's launcher starts Node with `NODE_COMPILE_CACHE` under its HOME (the profile), and Node runs a cache entry whose header carries the real source's length and checksum whatever bytecode it holds; a decoy probe forged one from a same-length `index.js` and it ran in the unfenced `--version` before a run, read `conversations/` and listed the vault. Cursor joins `fencedEverywhere`; a cache that a runtime fills itself (compile caches, `__pycache__`, JIT or package caches) counts as code the CLI loads, so check what the launcher sets (`NODE_COMPILE_CACHE`, `PYTHONPYCACHEPREFIX`, user site-packages) before leaving a provider process unfenced, and a fenced version check that `sandbox-exec` itself failed is `process_exited`; same search, plus `rg -n 'COMPILE_CACHE|PYCACHE|site-packages' ~/.local/share/cursor-agent/versions/*/cursor-agent`. Still unfenced: Antigravity's status and sign-in (user site-packages probed, not loaded), Claude's and Codex's `--version` (not probed), and the readiness `--version` with the person's HOME (BACKLOG).

### 2026-11-16 — A probe that denied reads of the Claude profile made the CLI rewrite `.claude.json` and lose the login's account

- **Area**: runtime probes against the persistent provider profiles (`.scratch/provider-profile/userData`), Claude Code 2.1.325's `.claude.json` handling.
- **Symptom**: to see which profile paths `claude auth status` reads, it was run under `sandbox-exec` with `file-read*` denied for the Claude profile and writes left open. It still printed the login, and the CLI replaced the profile's 144 KB `.claude.json` with a 357-byte default. The next app launches read the Claude connection as `account_mismatch` (the record's `oauthAccount` was gone), so the Claude usage read failed; it looked like a regression of the change under test until the file's mtime matched the probe.
- **Root cause**: the CLI treats a config file it cannot read as missing and writes a fresh one. A read-only command is not read-only once its reads fail, and Seatbelt deny logs are not readable from the session, so the deny gave no answer and did damage.
- **Net**: caught by the next real-app probe (Claude usage `read_failed`, auth state `account_mismatch`) and traced through the file's size and mtime; restored from the CLI's own `backups/.claude.json.backup.*`, checked against the stored account and organization. Missed before the run: nothing compared the profile's files before and after a by-hand command.
- **Prevention**: never deny reads of a real or persistent provider profile to a provider CLI; trace reads on a disposable copy (`cp -c -R`), or plant decoys and run the command. Before and after any by-hand command against a persistent profile, record the size and mtime of its account files (`.claude.json`, `.copilot/config.json`, vault working copies) and compare. Search: `rg -n "deny file-read" .scratch/runtime-test -g '*.sh' -g '*.mjs'`.

### 2026-11-16 — Choosing Claude's fence by detecting what the repository may run kept leaking, one shape at a time

- **Area**: `app/src/main/conversation/prepare.ts` (`runFence`; the removed `claudeRepoSources`, `frontmatterHooks`, `claudeSources`), `app/src/main/agentAuth/fence.ts` (the removed `claudeRepoCode`, `claudeSandboxEnabled`, `claudeFenceSettings`; new `claudeFencedCommand`), `conversation/runners.ts` (`claudeRunSettings`), `agentAuth/usageRead.ts` (`claudeRead`), `mcpConnectors/service.ts` (`run`), `agentAuth/manager.ts` (`mcpStep`); tests `prepareFence.test.ts`, `runners.fence.test.ts`, `fence.test.ts`, `usageRead.test.ts`, `service.test.ts`, `manager.test.ts`.
- **Symptom**: after the 2026-11-15 fix a decoy probe on develop still ran repository and profile code outside every fence, reading decoys in the Copilot profile and `conversations/`, listing the vault and writing into another profile: skill frontmatter hooks Doan's parser read as "no hooks" (a UTF-8 BOM, `--- ` with trailing blanks, an indented mapping, a flow mapping, `SKILL.MD`); an AWS `credential_process` reached through the project settings' `env` (Bedrock selected, `AWS_CONFIG_FILE`); and `mcpServers` / `projects[*].mcpServers` an agent can write into the profile's `.claude.json`, started by a sandbox-on turn and by `claude mcp list`/`get`. The Claude usage read, unfenced, started the server of a `.mcp.json` in a folder above userData at every launch. Plugin-shaped folders under `.claude/skills/` and a Git clean filter did not run on Claude Code 2.1.325, and a skill written mid-turn could not be invoked, but nothing in the design would have caught them if a later CLI ran them.
- **Root cause**: the fence was chosen per turn from a list of what the repository might make the CLI start, and the switch away from the outer fence (Claude's own sandbox, which holds only the model's commands) was driven by settings the repository and the agent control. Such a list must match the CLI's parsers, loaders and environment handling exactly, and every miss is an escape, while the CLI changes under it. Processes Doan starts outside a conversation were left unfenced on the reasoning "they run no model tool call", which ignores that the CLI starts configured MCP servers itself, and their folder's parents (`~/Library/Application Support`, `~/Library`, `~`) are writable by any outer-fenced agent.
- **Net**: caught by the 2026-11-16 audit (boundaries, contracts) and confirmed by a real-app decoy probe of each shape on develop; the fixed build was probed with the same decoys (all EPERM, every sampled Claude process under Seatbelt except `claude auth status`). Missed by the 2026-11-15 fix, whose probe checked the shapes it detected rather than the detector's parser against the CLI's, and whose search note judged the usage read safe by its empty folder without the `.mcp.json` walk up from it.
- **Prevention**: do not choose a fence by detecting what the repository or a profile may run; every Doan Claude process that loads settings an agent can write (turns, list reads, the usage read, MCP connector commands, a sign-out's MCP logout) runs in the outer fence, with Claude's own sandbox off at the flag tier, and starts nothing when the fence cannot be built. A new Claude spawn goes through `fencedCommand` with `fenceArgs("claude", …)` or `claudeFencedCommand` and gets a test asserting `sandbox-exec` and the resolved `PROFILE`. Before leaving a provider process unfenced, list every configuration file it reads, including a walk up from its folder, and who can write each one. Search: `rg -n "settingSources|pathToClaudeCodeExecutable|COMMANDS\.claude|\.exec\(provider" app/src/main -g '!*.test.ts'` and check each hit reaches `claudeFencedCommand` or `fenceArgs("claude"`; `rg -n "fencedCommand\(|claudeFencedCommand\(" app/src -g '!*.test.ts'`. Still unfenced by design: Claude's sign-in, status and own sign-out (`manager.ts` `exec` without `fenced`), which read the profile's settings files the fence closes to writes.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-16, round-3 audit and decoy probe)**: the step "before leaving a provider process unfenced, list every configuration file it reads" was not applied to the other providers: every Copilot process imported agent-writable code from its profile's package cache and started the profile's hooks and MCP servers, and Cursor's list read started a profile MCP server; every Copilot process and every list read but Codex's now runs in the outer fence, and `claudeFencedCommand` is `profileFencedCommand` (entry 2026-11-16 'Every Copilot process ran JavaScript…'); `rg -n "profileFencedCommand\(|fencedCommand\(" app/src -g '!*.test.ts'`. The Claude config paths Claude's own sandbox used to close (`local/`, `jobs/`, `daemon/`, `seed-admin/`, `workflows/`) stay writable to fenced turns: planted files there ran in none of `claude auth status`, `logout` or `login` (up to its link).

### 2026-11-16 — A lease released after quit's forced drop emptied the stored sign-in

- **Area**: `app/src/main/agentAuth/manager.ts` (`drop()`, `quit()`).
- **Symptom**: at quit, a lease whose process group outlived quit's wait was force-dropped (its count set to 1 and dropped, so the working copy was absorbed and scrubbed). When that group was later observed gone, the lease's release called `drop()` again: the count went to -1, `absorb()` read the scrubbed profile as no files, and `saveVault()` stored an empty vault. At the next launch the connection was still listed but the CLI ran with no login.
- **Root cause**: `drop()` treated any count at or below zero after its decrement as the last holder, with no notion of a release that holds nothing; quit's forced release and the lease's own release decremented the same counter. `acpLogout` already clamped its own decrement; `drop()` did not.
- **Net**: caught by the codebase audit, which traced the late-release routes (a conversation group's exit callback, the MCP and usage quit waits) against quit. Missed by the quit tests, which released nothing after `quit()`.
- **Prevention**: a release that can run after a forced release must find nothing held and do nothing; a reference count never goes below zero, and the last-holder work runs only on the step from one to zero. Test: acquire, quit, then release, and assert the vault still holds the credential. Search `rg -n '\?\? [01]\) - 1' app/src/main --glob '!*.test.ts'`.

### 2026-11-15 — A repository's Claude settings ran its hooks and MCP servers outside Doan's fence, behind an accepted limit

- **Area**: `app/src/main/conversation/prepare.ts` (`runFence`, `claudeRepoSources`, the fenced `use`), `app/src/main/agentAuth/fence.ts` (`claudeRepoCode`), `app/src/main/conversation/runners.ts` (`claudeRunSettings`); tests `fence.test.ts`, `prepareFence.test.ts`, `runners.fence.test.ts`.
- **Symptom**: a repository whose `.claude/settings.json` set `sandbox.enabled: true` made a Doan-connection Claude turn start with no outer `sandbox-exec`; its `SessionStart` hook and its `.mcp.json` server (`enableAllProjectMcpServers`) then read decoys in the Copilot profile and `conversations/`, listed the vault and wrote into the Copilot profile. Model and skill list reads had no fence at all, so the same hook and server did it at app start, without a conversation and without the sandbox key. The first fix still missed two shapes, found by a probe of its build: a `.mcp.json` with no enable key anywhere (the CLI started its server through the SDK without approval), and `awsAuthRefresh`/`awsCredentialExport` with Bedrock selected in the settings' `env`.
- **Root cause**: ARCHITECTURE listed "repository hooks may run outside the fence" and "list reads are not fenced: they run no model tool call" as accepted limits. Both were judged by what the model can do, but the CLI starts the repository's hooks and MCP servers itself, beside the model and outside Claude's command sandbox; and the switch to the sandbox-on mechanism was driven by a setting the repository controls. The limit read as a residual risk, so no probe ever tried it, and it hid a path a cloned repository reaches on its first open.
- **Net**: caught by the 2026-11-15 audit (boundaries) and confirmed by a real-app probe with decoys, including list reads at launch. Missed by the fence's earlier probes, which tried the model's own commands and file tools and the agent's own profile settings ("An agent planted provider settings in its own profile…", 2026-11-09), never code the CLI starts from the repository. The first fix's detection list was written from the CLI's documented approval model (`.mcp.json` needs an enable key) and its hook and MCP keys, and only the probe of each shape on the fixed build showed the SDK path skips approval and that credential helpers run too.
- **Prevention**: every Claude process Doan starts with the repository's settings sources runs under the outer fence (list reads always; a turn whose project or local sources name hooks, MCP servers or plugins, with the sandbox off at the flag tier), and a source Doan cannot read counts as naming them. A detection list of what starts repository code is checked shape by shape with a decoy probe on the built app (every key, file and helper it counts or leaves out), not taken from documentation. An accepted limit in ARCHITECTURE that says code "may run outside" a boundary gets a probe that runs that code with decoys before it is accepted, and is rewritten as a rule when the probe reaches the decoys. A new spawn of a provider CLI that loads repository configuration (`settingSources` with `project`, a Codex or ACP server with the repository as cwd) states which fence it runs under. Search run: `rg -n "settingSources" app/src -g '!*.test.ts'` (2026-11-15: `usageRead.ts` loads them with Doan's empty `usage-probe` folder as cwd, so no repository's; `runners.ts` `openClaude`, which every Doan-connection Claude turn and list read now reaches with a fence).
- **Recurrence (2026-11-16, round-2 audit and decoy probe)**: the detection list missed frontmatter spellings its parser did not read, an `env`-selected AWS `credential_process` and profile `.claude.json` MCP servers, and the usage read started a `.mcp.json` from a folder above userData; detection was replaced by fencing every Doan Claude process (entry 2026-11-16 'Choosing Claude's fence by detecting…'); `rg -n "settingSources|COMMANDS\.claude" app/src/main -g '!*.test.ts'`.

### 2026-11-15 — A FIFO in a repository or an agent profile held main or a libuv thread on open

- **Area**: `app/src/main/serve/server.ts` (`openNoFollow`), `seenRecords.ts`, `seenFingerprint.ts`, `onboarding/gitConfig.ts`, `agentAuth/providers.ts`; new `regularFile.ts` (`readRegularFileSync`)
- **Symptom**: a FIFO named as a canvas asset, a seen record or a listed canvas held a libuv thread in `open(2)` until a writer appeared (four such assets stall serving, thumbnails and seen writes for every folder); a FIFO `.git`, `commondir`, `config` or include, or a FIFO `.claude.json` / `.copilot/config.json` in a provider profile froze main, and `[include] path = /dev/zero` grew main without bound.
- **Root cause**: each of these opens already rejected non-regular files with an `isFile()` check, but on the descriptor after `open`, and a blocking `open` of a FIFO for reading does not return until a writer exists, so the check never ran. `readFileSync` has no type check at all and reads a device until it ends. The rule "opening a FIFO must never block main" lived in `conversation/io.ts`, `attachments.ts` and `acpRunner.ts` only, not in a shared helper the other readers used.
- **Net**: caught by the 2026-11-15 audit's boundary review from the existing in-code invariant. Missed by every reader's tests, which covered links, oversized files and permission errors but no FIFO, although the 2026-10-21 entries ask for special-file cases at content-derived paths. A FIFO test needs a rescuer that opens the write end after a delay (`app/src/main/fifo.fixture.ts` `withFifo`), or a blocking reader hangs the test worker instead of failing.
- **Prevention**: open repo-, agent- or profile-writable files with `O_NONBLOCK` before any `isFile()` check, and read such files on main through `readRegularFileSync` (or `conversation/io.ts` `readRegular` when the parent must not be a link); add a `withFifo` case to each reader's tests. Search: `rg -n "readFileSync\(|\bopen\(|openSync\(|readFile\(" app/src/main --glob '!*.test.ts' | rg -v O_NONBLOCK`, then keep only paths a repository or an agent can write. Not fixed here and still on that search (2026-11-15): `conversation/runners.ts` `profileServers` and `mcpConnectors/service.ts` `profileServers` read a provider profile's `config.toml` / `.claude.json` with `readFileSync`; `onboarding/projects.ts` reads a repository's `.gitattributes` with async `readFile`; `agentAuth/usageRead.ts` and `conversation/prepare.ts` `settingsFile` stat before a blocking read, which leaves only a swap race.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-15, PR #215 review)**: the new Claude repository-code check (`conversation/prepare.ts` `frontmatterHooks`) read every `*.md` under the repository's `.claude/skills|agents|commands` with `statSync` + `readFileSync`, and a FIFO there passed the directory and size checks and blocked main; an entry that is not a regular file or directory now counts as naming hooks, and settings and frontmatter files go through `readRegularFileSync`; `rg -n "readFileSync\(" app/src/main -g '!*.test.ts'`.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-16, round-2 audit)**: the sites listed above as not fixed, plus Cursor's credential cookie and the vault working-copy reads (`storage.ts` `readWorking`, `collect`), still read agent- or repository-writable files blocking or lstat-then-read, and the Git include walk had no total budget; they now open `O_NONBLOCK` (credentials also `O_NOFOLLOW` through `readRegularFileNoFollowSync`) with a size cap, and includes stop after 64 reads as `uncertain`; same search. Still on it and agent-writable: `storage.ts` `writeSetting`/`mergeSetting` (a profile settings file, Copilot's included), `manager.ts` `hookLinks` (the sign-in link file in a temporary directory) and `agentSetup/swap.ts` (lstat, then read).

- **Recurrence (2026-11-16, round-3 audit)**: the line above overstated the fix: `conversation/runners.ts` `profileServers` was unchanged. It, `storage.ts` `writeSetting`/`mergeSetting` (read before every provider process, the launch usage read included), `manager.ts` `hookLinks` and `agentSetup/runner.ts` `observe` now read through `readRegularFileNoFollowSync` (`readRegularBytesSync` for the link to the person's Claude settings) with a cap; a Doan-owned setting that is not a regular file is replaced; same search. Still on it: `agentSetup/swap.ts` reads its moved-aside file through the `readFileSync` it is handed, after an lstat.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-16, round-4 audit)**: `agentSetup/swap.ts` `swapFile` read the moved-aside file with the injected blocking `readFileSync`; it now reads through `readRegularFileNoFollowSync` capped at the bytes Doan read, and the Claude profile's merge keeps its staged, probe and aside names in `agent-auth/`, which the fence closes, so a fenced turn cannot rename a FIFO or its own file onto them; same search.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-20, eval result readers)**: checkers, fake-app snapshots and evidence retention also consumed agent-written files. Resolve internal links within scope, admit a regular descriptor within the caller's byte limit and retain the same bytes used to judge CLI provenance. The descriptor fixtures in `eval/lib/artifact.test.ts` cover ordinary and refused results. Search with `rg -n 'readFileSync|cpSync' eval -g '*.ts' -g '!*.test.ts'`.

### 2026-11-15 — A group stop made every caller wait its full 2 s bound, even for a group gone in milliseconds

- **Area**: `app/src/main/conversation/process.ts` (`OwnedGroup.stop()`/`kill()`), and through it every list read, Codex close, sign-out and quit with a live runner.
- **Symptom**: the first model or skill list read per connection reached the composer about 2 s after the provider answered; quit and a sign-out waited at least 2 s whenever a runner was alive. Release and ownership were right; only the latency was wrong.
- **Root cause**: the caller's promise was a bare `setTimeout(resolve, 2000)`, independent of the liveness poll that already observed the exit and released the group. The bound added after 2026-10-23 'A utility-process kill request left its caller pending' became the only way to settle, not a ceiling.
- **Net**: the whole-codebase audit found it from the code; unit tests only covered a group that never goes (the bound) and advanced fake timers past 2 s, so an early settle was never asserted, and real list reads felt only "a bit slow".
- **Prevention**: a bounded wait races the real completion and the timer (`Promise.race([work, timeout])`), or the completion observer settles the waiters, as `OwnedGroup.observe()` now does. Test both sides: a group that goes after 100 ms settles at about 100 ms, a group that stays settles at the bound. Search `rg -n 'setTimeout\(resolve' app/src/main --glob '!*.test.ts'` and check that each timer is raced against, or cleared by, the work it bounds.

### 2026-11-15 — A failed conversation index or undo write left its temporary file for good

- **Area**: `app/src/main/conversation/store.ts` (`replace()`, `acceptChange()`'s undo record).
- **Symptom**: a short write or ENOSPC/EIO left `index-<uuid>.tmp`, `deletions-<uuid>.tmp` or `undo-<uuid>.tmp` in the conversations directory; nothing sweeps it, so each failure added one more file.
- **Root cause**: only the rename failure unlinked the temporary file; the write sat in `try { write } finally { close }`, and the store never lists its directory at start. The drafts store in the same directory removes its temporary file on a failed write and sweeps its own leftovers, so the two stores disagreed.
- **Net**: the audit compared the two stores; tests only failed the rename, never the write.
- **Prevention**: a staged file is removed on every failure between its creation and its rename (write, close and rename), by the code that created it; never by a name-pattern sweep of another writer's files (2026-10-25 'Startup cache collection deleted a live thumbnail temporary file'). Test a short write and assert no `*.tmp` remains. Search `rg -n 'randomUUID\(\)\}\.tmp' app/src/main --glob '!*.test.ts'` and check each site's write path, not only its rename.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-16, codebase audit)**: the store's `fill()` still left its temporary file when the close after a full write failed; the close now sits inside the guarded block and `storeIndex.test.ts` fails a close. The drafts store has the same close path, but it removes its own `drafts-*.tmp` at start, so its leftover lasts one run; `rg -n 'randomUUID\(\)\}\.tmp' app/src/main --glob '!*.test.ts'`.

- **Recurrence (2026-11-16, round-3 audit)**: `agentAuth/storage.ts` `replace()` and `stage()` ignored `writeSync`'s count, so a short write was fsynced and renamed into place (a truncated vault or metadata); they now throw on a short count and remove their temporary file (`storage.faults.test.ts`); `rg -n "writeSync\(" app/src/main -g '!*.test.ts'` and check each call compares the count.
### 2026-11-15 — Quit re-wrote appQuit to a history that refused writes, aborted, and left later runners open

- **Area**: `app/src/main/conversation/service.ts` (`quit()`).
- **Symptom**: after a turn's event append failed (`failedPersistence`, still busy until its group was gone), Cmd+Q appended `interrupted/appQuit` to the same history, which refused it again. The quit aborted, and the throw ended the loop, so conversations after it in the map never had their runner closed.
- **Root cause**: Stop already skipped the interrupted write for `failedPersistence`, but quit did not; and quit closed each runner inside the same loop iteration as its throwing write, so one throw skipped every later close.
- **Net**: the audit traced the quit path against Stop's rule; the quit-failure test failed only the first conversation's write and checked the work markers, not the later runners.
- **Prevention**: in a teardown loop, do every step that must happen for all entries (closing runs, clearing work) in its own pass before any step that can throw, and skip writes the entry is already known to refuse. Test two entries where the first throws and assert the second's cleanup ran. Search `rg -n 'failedPersistence' app/src/main/conversation/service.ts` for paths that append without the check.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-16, codebase audit)**: quit still cleared held timers, busy, the steer lookup and steers after the throwing appQuit append, in the same loop; they now run in their own pass, and each owed appQuit write gets its own attempt and is kept for the next quit when it fails (a pass that marks entries ended must not lose a write a retry needs); `rg -n 'this\.append\(' app/src/main/conversation/service.ts`.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-16, round-3 audit)**: keeping a failed appQuit owed made a refusal no retry fixes (the 256 MiB cap, a torn last line) cancel every later Cmd+Q, update install and logout, while delete stayed refused during the quit; a refusal that every later append meets (`refusesForGood` in `store.ts`) is now logged and not owed, and any other failure cancels one quit and is dropped when the next quit's retry fails too. A teardown write kept for a retry needs a bound on the retries and a test that fails it permanently (no mock restore) across two quits; `rg -n 'owedAppQuit|refusesForGood' app/src/main/conversation`.

### 2026-11-15 — The exported prototype followed links the canvas had cancelled, because its own cancel hid the canvas's

- **Area**: `cli/internal/export/viewer/isolate.js` (link decision of the exported prototype page), `app/src/main/exportViewer.test.ts`
- **Symptom**: on a `data-doan-goto` link with an `<a href>`, a canvas that cancelled the click from a window bubble listener (`preventDefault()` or `returnValue = false`) still moved to the next frame. Each fix round closed one path and the next review found another: a listener the canvas added during the click, a native `preventDefault` saved before our instance shadow was installed, `Object.preventExtensions(event)`, an href added during the click.
- **Root cause**: `isolate.js` itself had to cancel the anchor's default (a `srcdoc` link would otherwise load another document into the frame) and then judged the canvas's cancel from the same event. Once anything has cancelled an event, `defaultPrevented` cannot say who did, and there is no hook that runs after every listener but before the activation, so every "record before our cancel" or "watch calls after it" scheme leaves a path open. The prototype window's script never cancels, which is why the shared click table (all `element.click()` on links without an href) passed.
- **Net**: missed by the shared table and by the batch's own `/code-review`, since no row put an href under a late cancel; caught by an independent cross-family review reproducing it in a real Electron DOM, then by an exported-file browser probe. A `window.stop()` alternative passed over `file://` but lost the race in Chromium over http (the navigation committed before the 0 ms timer).
- **Prevention**: a script that must decide on the page's own cancel must not cancel the same event itself; remove the default's cause instead (here the link's `href` and XLink `href` are taken off in a window bubble listener added during capture and put back after the decision), and test cancels from every phase, added during the dispatch, through saved native methods and on a non-extensible event, each with and without an href. Search run: `rg -n 'defaultPrevented' app/src/main/*.ts cli/internal/export/viewer --glob '!*.test.*'` (2026-11-15: `prototypeScript.ts` reads it without cancelling; `measure.js` reads it for Escape only when it cancelled nothing; `shell.js` and `lib.js` read it for the page's own keys, not a canvas verdict).

### 2026-11-15 — On a 1x display the selection line left a pixel gap on one side and covered the element's edge on the other

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/styles.css` (`.mark`, `.mark:not(.jump-point)`, `.dims`, `.dims.above`, the `@media (resolution < 2dppx)` block after them); test `CanvasController.test.ts` 'grows the selection box by the line width actually drawn, at 2x and at 1x'.
- **Symptom**: the canvas selection and hover line is drawn outside the element: the mark's padding box is the element's rect, with a 1.5px border and a −1.5px margin. On a 1x display at 100 % the probe read, across the element's left edge, line · canvas · element pixels (a one-pixel gap), and on the right edge the line standing on the element's last pixel column.
- **Root cause**: Chromium floors a border width to whole device pixels (`getComputedStyle` reads `1px` at 1x for `1.5px`), but not a margin, so the box still started 1.5px out while its line was 1px wide; the overlay box and the canvas iframe then snapped the half-pixel position to different device pixels. At 2x 1.5px is exactly 3 device pixels, which hid it.
- **Net**: caught by the `/runtime-test` probe's pixel read at 1x (`webContents.capturePage` across both edges, `--force-device-scale-factor=1`). Missed by `/design-sync`, which measured computed styles at 2x only, and by the first 1x probe, which judged CSS boxes (rect ± border) rather than drawn pixels and sampled at a fractional zoom, where the element's own antialiased edge pixel looked like contact.
- **Prevention**: an outward line whose offset is a margin or a `calc()` must use the width actually drawn at every resolution the app runs at: either a whole-pixel width, or a `resolution` media rule after the base rules that sets the floored width and its matching offset (a test reads both blocks and their order). Check such geometry at 1x and 2x by device pixels on both opposite edges, at 100 %, not by computed boxes. Search: `rg -n -e 'border[a-z-]*:[^;]*[0-9]\.[0-9]+px' -e 'margin[a-z-]*:[^;]*-[0-9]\.[0-9]+px' -e 'outline[a-z-]*:[^;]*[0-9]\.[0-9]+px' app/src/renderer --glob '*.css'` (2026-11-15: only `.mark` and `.mark:not(.jump-point)`, the place fixed here).

### 2026-11-15 — A running turn's in-memory canvas missed a move that a refused send had already saved

- **Area**: `app/src/main/conversation/service.ts` (`liveTurns`, the Live entry's `turnOf`, `refreshCanvas`, `submit`), `app/src/main/workOthers.ts`.
- **Symptom**: to keep the `doan work` answer path off the disk, `liveTurns()` was changed to read each running turn's root, provider and bound canvas from memory, recorded when the turn was accepted and on `promoteTo`. A send to a running conversation whose canvas file had moved was refused as busy, but `submit` had already called `refreshCanvas`, which saved the new path to `index.json`. The index said `moved.html`, memory said `a.html`, so `others` for `moved.html` missed that conversation and listed it on the old path.
- **Root cause**: the cache was written from the writers the author knew (`start`, acceptance, `promoteTo`) and the claim "binding changes only after a turn ends" was never checked against every `store.save` that can change `canvas`; `refreshCanvas` runs before the busy check, so a refused request still writes metadata.
- **Net**: caught by the independent re-review, which listed every writer of `canvas` and reproduced the divergence with the real `ConversationService` and store in a temp directory. Missed by the batch's own tests (acceptance, promotion, delete) and its self-review.
- **Prevention**: when an in-memory copy replaces a read of stored state, grep every write of each copied field (`rg -n 'store\.save|acceptChange|canvas:' app/src/main/conversation/service.ts`) and update the copy after each successful save, including saves on paths that end in a refusal; pin the divergence case (a refused request that writes) with a test that compares the stored and the cached value.

### 2026-11-14 — A cancelled clone's screen hid the failure of a folder chosen from it behind the cancelled status

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/Onboarding.tsx` (`ProjectScreen`, the `cloneCancelled` branch), `app/src/renderer/projectStep.ts` (`inspectPath` keeps the screen it was called from as `back`, `failed`).
- **Symptom**: after a cancelled clone, Choose local folder in the cancelled notice picked a folder of another repository (or one gone): the inspection failed with `remote_mismatch` or `project_unavailable`, but the screen still showed only "Cloning was cancelled. Choose a folder or try again." No alert, no focus on it, and the Add Projects page's Add project was disabled by the error nothing on screen named. The branch predated project-step-actions; that feature made it worse by removing the page's Retry from the cancelled notice.
- **Root cause**: a failure lands on whatever phase its caller passes as `back`, so any phase a folder sheet can be opened from can carry an error, but the `cloneCancelled` branch was written as a pure state screen and drew its own fixed status notice without reading `notice`. The state type allows every phase with an error, and nothing ties a phase's branch to rendering it.
- **Net**: caught by the independent code review (by reading the path from `chooseExisting` through `inspectPath`'s `back`). Missed by unit tests, which rendered the cancelled screen without an error and clone errors only on the `clone` phase; by `/design-sync`, which measured each drawn state alone; and by the draft, which has no tile for the combination.
- **Prevention**: every `ProjectScreen` branch for a phase a failure can land on renders `notice`, and a branch with its own status notice shows it only when there is no error (`notice ?? statusNotice`). When adding a phase or a sheet opened from a screen, list where `failed` can land and check the branch: `rg -n "this\.failed\(" app/src/renderer/projectStep.ts` (2026-11-14: `choose`, the calling screen as `back` in `inspectPath` and `runSelect`, the reinspect's failure screen, `clone`, the ready phase; `back` in `inspectPath` is any URL screen, `cloneCancelled` included) against `rg -n "ProjectNoticeView " app/src/renderer/Onboarding.tsx` (2026-11-14: choose, candidates, setup, `cloneCancelled`, clone, ready all render `notice`; `checking`, `selecting`, `cloning` and `completing` are progress phases no failure lands on). Pinned by `AddProjectsPage.test.tsx` and `Onboarding.test.tsx` "after a cancelled clone, a chosen folder's failure shows its own alert".

### 2026-11-14 — A draft placed select-time failures on the screen of a check that never reports them

- **Area**: `.doan/canvases/new-project-folder.html` (`shipped-notices-choose`), `app/src/main/onboarding/projects.ts` (`unread`, called from `register` and `confirmProject`).
- **Symptom**: the draft drew `config_invalid` and `project_unreadable` "after Choose folder" under the local folder card on the choose screen. In the app a folder with a broken config or an unreadable `.doan/` passes the check and shows as a chosen card; main reports these codes only when the folder is added, so they appear on that folder's screen after Add project (in first run right after Choose folder, which selects at once). `/design-sync` timed out waiting for a notice that state never shows.
- **Root cause**: the draft placed notices by error code, and the code does not say which stage reports it. Inspection lists a candidate without reading its config; only the selection's `ls` (`register`) and the completion's re-read (`confirmProject`) turn a config error or a CLI read failure into `config_invalid` or `project_unreadable`.
- **Net**: caught by `/design-sync` driving the state for real with a broken and an unreadable folder. Missed by the draft review and the feature documents, which listed the codes as Choose folder failures without checking main.
- **Prevention**: before placing a notice for an onboarding code in a draft or a screen, find the main function that returns it and which operation calls it: `rg -n 'failed\("|code: "' app/src/main/onboarding/projects.ts app/src/main/onboarding/service.ts -g '!*.test.ts'` (2026-11-14: `config_invalid` and `project_unreadable` only from `unread`, called by `register` (select) and `confirmProject` (complete); `inspectPath` returns `project_unavailable` and `remote_mismatch`). A `/design-sync` probe drives each failure through the real operation rather than assuming its screen.

### 2026-11-14 — A failed buddy switch dropped focus to the page body: the row was focused while the modal alert was still open

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/SettingsGeneral.tsx` (Settings > General's buddy switch alert), `SettingsDialog.tsx`.
- **Symptom**: after "Switch to <Name>" and a failed save, the alert closed and the notice showed, but `document.activeElement` was `<body>` instead of the pressed row.
- **Root cause**: the save's promise callback closed the alert with `setAsking(null)` and moved focus in `requestAnimationFrame`. A state update from a promise is not a discrete event, so React commits it on its own schedule, and the frame callback ran first: it focused a row behind a still-open modal `<dialog>`, which the inert page refused, and then the alert unmounted and focus fell to the body. The same code after a click works, because React commits a discrete event's update synchronously, which made the pattern look safe.
- **Net**: caught by the runtime probe (`.scratch/runtime-test/buddy-cooldown/probe.mjs`, a save made to fail through a read-only userData). Missed by `SettingsGeneral.test.tsx`, whose `requestAnimationFrame` stub runs at once and whose `document` stub focuses anything, so the open alert was never in the way.
- **Prevention**: after an async answer, move focus in an effect that runs once the render that closes the dialog (and draws the target) has committed, as `SettingsGeneral`'s `landing` ref and `SettingsMcpConnectors`' `pending` ref do; not in a frame callback from the promise. Test it by asserting focus has not moved before the next render (`SettingsGeneral.test.tsx` 'a failed switch … lands on the buddy'). Search: `rg -n "then\(.*requestAnimationFrame\(.*focus" app/src/renderer` and `rg -n "requestAnimationFrame\(\(\) =>[^;]*focus" app/src/renderer`, then check each hit that runs after `await` or `.then` while a modal is open.

### 2026-11-14 — The installed Doan.app came up on the real userData during a real-HOME probe, and the cause was guessed instead of traced

- **Area**: real-HOME runtime probes (chain 261113-3 link 3 batch U), the installed CLI's app launch (`cli/internal/cli/onboarding.go` `bootstrap`, `launchCommand`), the installed app's `doan://` URL scheme (`/Applications/Doan.app` `CFBundleURLSchemes`).
- **Symptom**: at 13:47, while a probe used the real HOME and the shared signed-in profile, `/Applications/Doan.app` started on the real userData and changed the real `agent-auth`, `runtime.json` and `ui-state.json`; it started again (pid 35469) right after the probe's dev app launched, before any agent turn. The probe's pre-probe backup of the shared profile's Doan state was lost to overlapping runs. Link 1 of the same chain had seen the same real-userData changes without a cause.
- **Root cause**: two things can bring the installed app up from outside it, and neither was watched. (1) The installed CLI launches its own bundle with `/usr/bin/open` only from `bootstrap`, which `doan open`, `doan open --prototype` and `doan onboarding start` call; an agent turn in the probe ran the installed `doan` from `~/.local/bin`. (2) The installed app is the registered handler of `doan://`, so any process that opens a `doan://` URL (a dev app, a test, an agent, a browser) starts `/Applications/Doan.app`, never the dev app. The second launch fits (2) but was not traced. The lead first blamed its own `doan check` and `doan design-skill`; neither calls `bootstrap`, so that attribution, repeated in PR #192, was wrong.
- **Net**: caught by the conductor's real-state fingerprints and a process check after the fact. Missed by the probe setup: it isolated `DOAN_RUNTIME` and userData but left the installed `doan` on `PATH` and the installed app registered for `doan://`, and nobody watched processes during the window.
- **Prevention**: during a real-HOME probe, (a) take the installed `doan` off `PATH` for the probe and its agents, (b) run a process watch over the whole window (`ps -axo pid,ppid,lstart,command | grep '/Applications/Doan.app'`) and record the parent of any Doan.app start before attributing it, as the 2026-11-09 Antigravity entry does for provider CLIs, and (c) treat any code path that opens a `doan://` URL in the probe as a launch of the installed app. Search run: `rg -n "bootstrap\(ctx" cli/internal/cli -g '!*_test.go'` (2026-11-14: `open.go` 42 and 76, `onboarding.go` 258; no other launch path); `rg -n "openExternal\(" app/src/main -g '!*.test.ts'` (2026-11-14: `index.ts` 1006, 1041, 1469, 1510, 1522, 1567, 1575; 1006 and 1041 open provider sign-in URLs, 1522 only `https` links (`linkTarget`), the others fixed settings, install, release-notes and repository URLs; none builds a `doan://` URL, so the second launch's opener is still unknown).

### 2026-11-14 — A list main answered for the canvas it played was applied to the one the prototype window still showed

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/prototype/conversationRun.ts` (`read`, `apply`), `app/src/main/prototype/conversationScope.ts` (`list` scope, `prototypeListElsewhere`), `app/src/main/index.ts` (the prototype role's conversation handler); fixed before merge (prototype-conversation batch UF, "answer the prototype window's list only for the canvas it names").
- **Symptom**: with the prototype window's panel showing conversation ca of canvas A and an unsent draft in it, a play of B followed by a list change in the same root made the window drop ca's draft as if ca had been deleted. Likewise, after A → B → A, the first A read answering last replaced the newer A list and dropped the new conversation's draft.
- **Root cause**: the request named only the root, main judged it against what played when it arrived, and the window applied the answer under the canvas its own view showed. Main's play moves at once while the view waits behind the still capture, so the two named different canvases, and the host read "missing from the list" as "deleted". A key check could not refuse an older read of the same canvas either: A, B, A brings the same key back.
- **Net**: caught by the integrated review's module probe (real `prototypeConversationScope`, `prototypeListRows` and the host). Missed by the host tests, whose fake main kept one pending answer per root and never moved main's target ahead of the view.
- **Prevention**: a request whose answer the receiver applies under its own idea of the target names that target, and the sender refuses a mismatch with an answer that is neither a failure nor data (`not_playing`); an answer that infers deletions applies only if its read began after the last switch (an epoch, not the key). Test with main's real scope as the fake: main ahead of the view, and A → B → A answered out of order. Search: `rg -n "\.deleted\(" app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'` (on 2026-11-14: only this host infers a deletion from a list; App.tsx's hits are the main window's own delete) and `rg -n "listConversations\(" app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'` (`homeRun.ts` reads the main window's whole root, which has no view to trail).

### 2026-11-14 — The prototype window dropped the drawing main had pushed for the canvas it was about to show

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/prototype/conversationRun.ts` (`setView`, the relayed drawing and promotion ids); fixed in 63e2bb0fd before merge (prototype-conversation batch U), after a review note had asked to clear them on a switch of canvas.
- **Symptom**: a Home conversation drew a new canvas and the main window's panel showed "A project conversation is drawing this canvas", but the prototype window playing that canvas showed nothing.
- **Root cause**: main pushes the new target's drawing as soon as the play changes, while the prototype view can wait behind the still capture. The host cleared the relayed id when the new canvas's view arrived, which by then was the new canvas's own value. A relay that carries only an id, not the target it is for, cannot be cleared by the receiver's own idea of when the target changed.
- **Net**: caught by the real-turn `/runtime-test` probe (`probe-b.mjs`), the first run that made a drawing at all (earlier runs edited an existing canvas, which is not a drawing). Missed by the unit test that pinned the clearing: it sent the view first and main's push after, the one order where clearing is right.
- **Prevention**: on a switch, a host that holds relayed per-target state asks main again instead of only clearing it, and a push that lands after the read began wins (a turn counter). Test both orders of push and view. Search: `rg -n "readDrawing|readPromoted|onDrawing|onPromoted" app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'`.

### 2026-11-14 — The main window's panel never showed the conversation the prototype window's first message started

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/canvasRun.ts` (`resolve`, the new conversation state), with the prototype window as a second place that starts canvas conversations; fixed in 13d3fb2be before merge (prototype-conversation batch U).
- **Symptom**: with a canvas that had no conversation, the first message sent from the prototype window started the canvas's current conversation, but the main window's canvas panel stayed on "New conversation" with the suggested prompts. A send from it would have started a second conversation and archived the prototype window's.
- **Root cause**: once the panel picked the new conversation state (`shown === null`), `pickCanvasConversation` keeps it on every list read, so the person's New is never overridden. The panel could not tell that New from the default it fell back to because nothing existed, and until this feature no other window could make a current conversation appear on a canvas the panel shows.
- **Net**: caught by the real-turn `/runtime-test` probe (`.scratch/runtime-test/prototype-conversation/probe-b.mjs`) looking at the main window after the prototype window's first send. Missed by every unit test: the prototype host's tests drove only its own run, and `canvasRun.test.ts` had no case of a current conversation appearing on a canvas left in the default new state.
- **Prevention**: a state picked because nothing existed is marked as a default (`defaultNew`) and gives way to what newly appears, while the person's choice does not. When a feature adds a second place that creates something another screen picks from a list, test that screen with the item appearing from elsewhere. Search: `rg -n "shown === null|context.shown === null" app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'` — the panel's rule lives in `canvasConversation.ts` (`pickCanvasConversation`) and `canvasRun.ts`; the hits in `canvasNotice.ts` and `quickOpen.ts` are other values named `shown`, and the Overview keeps its own `current` rule (`overviewRun.ts`).

- **Recurrence (2026-11-14, the same batch's rerun of the probe)**: the first fix took "newly appeared" from the canvas state's `previous` list, which a canvas opened after its root's list was read does not have (the unit test read the list after opening the canvas, the app reads it at launch). The panel now keeps the ids listed when it fell back to the new state (`defaultNew`), and `canvasRun.test.ts` covers the list read before the canvas opens.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-14, the integrated review)**: `resolve` set `defaultNew` whenever a read picked the new state, so the first read after the person chose New made it the default, and a conversation started elsewhere later took it over with the person's draft. Only the default new state now takes the listed ids again (the person's New is `shown === null` without `defaultNew`); `canvasRun.test.ts` covers select(null), an unchanged read, then a new current conversation. Prevention addition: a test of the person's choice puts an unchanged read between the choice and the change it must survive.

### 2026-11-14 — The prototype composer offered a disabled Queue instead of Stop while a turn ran with an empty field

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/DockedComposer.tsx` (`action`, `steerAllPlan`'s `draft`, with the `playing` prop); fixed in 5759304f1 before merge (prototype-conversation batch U, independent review).
- **Symptom**: in the prototype window's panel, while an agent worked and the field was empty, the Send slot showed a disabled Queue rather than Stop. The composer has no shortcut that stops a turn, so the window could not stop it. Send on an idle turn behind an empty queue turned into Queue the same way, and ⌘Enter's Steer all always saw a draft.
- **Root cause**: the slot's action read the request text, and the request starts with the pinned playing frame's marker (`{{1}}`) whatever the person typed. A chip the host adds to every message is part of what is sent but not of what the person wrote; `composerAction` takes "is there anything to send" from the text it is given and cannot tell the two apart.
- **Net**: caught by the independent review rendering the composer with `turn: "running"` and an empty field. Missed by the batch's tests, which drew the pinned chip and checked the sent message only with `turn: "idle"`.
- **Prevention**: anything the host adds to every request (the playing frame) goes into the request and the place limit only; Stop, Queue, Send and Steer all read the field's own message (`dockedMessage(draft, …)`). A test of a host-added chip covers a running turn with an empty field. Search: `rg -n "composerAction\(|steerAllPlan\(" app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'` — `DockedComposer.tsx` is the only caller.

### 2026-11-14 — A report kept by canvas key came back when the same canvas played again

- **Area**: `app/src/main/prototype/showing.ts`, `app/src/main/prototype/controller.ts` (`canvasGeneration`).
- **Symptom**: the prototype window reported that its panel showed conversation c1 on canvas A; after a play of B and then A again, main took c1 as shown before the window had said so again, and could leave out a notification for c1 that the panel was not showing yet.
- **Root cause**: the report was kept with the canvas key it was made for and compared with the key playing now. A key comparison cannot tell "still that play" from "that canvas again after another one"; the report's validity belongs to the play, not to the canvas.
- **Net**: caught by the independent review (RM2) running the module through A → B → A without a new report; the first fix's test went A → B and then sent another report, which cleared it, so the return was never tested.
- **Prevention**: state that holds only until the window reports again is tied to a generation that moves on every change of what plays (`canvasGeneration`), not to the key it was made for; test A → B → A with no new report. Grep: `rg -n 'canvasKeyString\(' app/src/main --glob '!*.test.*'` (on 2026-11-14: `showing.ts` now by generation; `index.ts` `prototypeResume` compares by key, which a return revives with the same canvas's earlier resume session, the same canvas's own data, so it is left; `windowState.ts` keeps each canvas's last frame list by key on purpose).

- **Recurrence (2026-11-14, prototype-conversation M3 review)**: the other side of the same rule. The drawing project conversation relay invalidated its record by generation, but told the main window of a new target only when the target's value changed, after a view. Views wait behind a still capture and coalesce, so A → B → A during a capture sent no target at all (the last A equalled the one already sent), the main window never reported again, and the drawing notice stayed gone while the project conversation kept drawing. Caught by the independent review (RM4) with the real controller and a held capture. Fixed in `prototype/controller.ts` (`canvasChanged`, told at once on every generation move) and `prototype/targetRelay.ts` (the target carries the generation, and a report carries it back so one made for an earlier play of the same canvas is refused), tested in `targetRelay.test.ts` with the real controller, a held capture and the drawing record. Prevention addition: a push deduplicated by value must include whatever invalidates the receiver's state (here the generation), or the invalidation must itself trigger the push; and a signal that rides on a coalescing channel (views behind a capture) is sent on its own. Grep: `rg -n 'text === [a-zA-Z]*[sS]ent|=== sent\b' app/src/main --glob '!*.test.*'` (on 2026-11-14: the controller's view, which carries its run gen; the prototype window's work and entry pushes and composer settings, whose receivers keep no state a generation invalidates; the drawing and promoted id pushes, whose value is null after any generation move).
- **Recurrence (2026-11-14, the integrated review)**: the renderer side. The prototype host sent `setShowing` again only when the shown id or the canvas key changed, so views coalesced from A → B → A (same key, same conversation) sent nothing, while main had dropped the report on the generation move; a conversation the focused open panel showed was notified. The host now also sends on every new run (`view.gen`, which every play moves), tested in `PrototypeConversation.mainScope.test.ts` with main's real showing record. The grep above covered `app/src/main` only; also run `rg -n "\.key !== this\.key|showing\.(id|key|gen)" app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'` (on 2026-11-14: only this report).
### 2026-11-14 — Closing the prototype window removed an attachment the main window's draft had taken back

- **Area**: `app/src/main/attachmentHandlers.ts` (was inline in `app/src/main/index.ts`), `app/src/main/attachments.ts` (`remove`, `release`, `consume`, `recheck`), `conversation:takeDraft`.
- **Symptom**: with the prototype window's conversation panel, an attachment id issued to that window and queued with a message came back as a draft after a failed turn; the main window took the draft and rechecked the id into its own composer. Closing the prototype window then removed it, and the main window's send failed with `attachment_missing`. Its first version released every id on close instead, which made a still-queued message's recheck fail the same way. Neither reached develop.
- **Root cause**: an attachment id is a store entry with no notion of which window holds it, while drafts are per window and an id moves between windows (a returned queue goes to whichever window takes the draft first, and a recheck turns a sent id into that window's draft again). Cleanup kept "every id ever issued to this window" and acted on all of them; the store's `remove` and `release` also took any id from any window, so one window's request could reach another's draft.
- **Net**: the release-on-close version was caught by the batch's own self-check (it reasoned through the queue's recheck) before its commit; the remove-on-close version passed every test and was caught by the independent review (RM), which ran the extracted handler block on a real `AttachmentStore` and `queueFiles` (proto queue → main takes and rechecks → proto close → `attachment_missing`). Missed by the batch's tests, which covered the pure scope and the preload but not the main handlers' wiring of a window's lifetime to the store.
- **Prevention**: anything issued to a window and cleaned up when it closes is keyed to its current holder (window and instance), moves with the request that hands it over (`takeDraft`, `recheck`), and is refused to any other window; a close acts only on what that window still holds, and never on a sent or queued id. Test at the handler boundary with the real store: issue → queue → another window takes it back → close → send. Grep: `rg -n 'closed\(|ownerClosed' app/src/main --glob '!*.test.*'` (on 2026-11-14: `attachmentHandlers.ts` by owner, `agentAuth` `ownerClosed(owner)` by the window's webContents id, both keyed to the holder).

- **Recurrence (2026-11-14, review round 2 of the same batch)**: the first fix checked ownership only on the three attachment channels and kept an id with its window until the window closed.
