Two holes stayed: a start, send or queue carried any id the store resolves, so the prototype window could queue a draft attachment the main window had taken back (its next send then failed with `attachment_missing`); and a queue the service had already reclaimed to send (`queueFiles.reclaim` puts the ids back into the draft entries) was still the window's, so a close during the next turn's prepare removed it and the queued message failed its acceptance recheck. Also, a removed id lost its owner and an unregistered id was allowed, so another window could recheck it again. Caught by the independent review (RM2) with the real store and module; fixed in `attachmentHandlers.ts` (`conversation`: only the sender's own ids, and once main accepts the request the ids leave the window for the conversation; the prototype window may not name an id main never gave it; a removed id keeps its window), tested through the real `conversationHandler` and `queueFiles` (reclaim, then close, then recheck). Prevention addition: every path that consumes an id (`start`, `send`, `queue`, and the service's `consume` and `reissue`) is part of the ownership model, not only the attachment channels; check with `rg -n '\.consume\(|addCaptured\(|reissue' app/src/main --glob '!*.test.*'` (on 2026-11-14: the conversation ipc's consume behind `attachmentHandlers.conversation`, the service's reclaim/reissue holding conversation-owned ids, and the main-only capture).
- **Recurrence (2026-11-18, audit)**: the prototype window's `closed` handler returned early when a Play between its `close` and `closed` had already made the next window, so that window's attachment cleanup never ran; `closed(contents, replaced)` now always releases the window's own attachments and skips only the shared resets, pinned by `prototypeWindow.test.ts` (close, play, then closed). The fake window emitted `closed` inside `close()`, so no test reached the gap. Search: `rg -n '!== created\) return' app/src/main --glob '!*.test.*'` (on 2026-11-18 only the `close` handler, which owns nothing per window).

### 2026-11-14 — A list read moved the prototype window's first message, still being sent, into the conversation it started

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/prototype/conversationRun.ts` (`sync`, the carry of a draft to a new current conversation); fixed in c5c1b7d17 before merge (prototype-conversation batch U).
- **Symptom**: from the new conversation state, a list read that named the conversation this window's own send had just started (main's `listChanged` can come before the start's reply, POSTMORTEM 2026-10-26) moved the new state's draft — the message being sent — under `conversation:<new id>`. The composer empties only the draft key the send left from, so the sent text would have come back in the new conversation's field.
- **Root cause**: the carry treated every switch to a new current conversation as one the main window started, but the composer's send owns its draft key until the reply, and a move made meanwhile is invisible to it (`DockedComposer` clears `drafts.get(at)` only when it is still the draft it sent). The carry also went beyond its rule: only a previous conversation's draft follows a new current one.
- **Net**: caught by the batch's own `/code-review` reading the carry against the start reply's ordering. Missed by the first tests, which carried only between two existing conversations and never from the new state with a send in flight.
- **Prevention**: a move of a draft driven by a list read or an event never takes a draft whose send can be in flight (the new conversation state's while a start is pending); pin it with a test that answers the list before the start's reply. Search: `rg -n "carryDraft\(|carryPrototypeDraft\(|moveDraft\(" app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'` — the Overview's `moveDraft` after a list (`overviewRun.ts` `afterList`) moves a conversation's draft to the new state on a promotion or removal, not the other way, and the two `carryDraft` calls run on the person's own Start a new conversation.

- **Recurrence (2026-11-14, the same batch's independent review)**: the fix guarded only the new conversation state's draft; a conversation → conversation carry could still move a reply whose send had not been answered. The prototype host now counts its own starts, sends and queues by the draft key they left from (`PrototypeConversationRun.bodyCalls`) and carries no draft while one is in flight, from either state (fixed in 5759304f1, test 'a previous conversation's draft whose send is in flight stays where the send will empty it'). The Prevention above holds for both directions: the in-flight check is per draft key, not per state.

### 2026-11-14 — An eval check that compared a prototype export only with a regenerated one accepted the canvas viewer

- **Area**: `eval/scenarios/X2/checks.ts`, `checks.test.ts`; the X2 fail fixture.
- **Symptom**: X2 judged the agent's file by byte equality with a fresh `doan export --frame <id> --prototype` run of the same CLI. While `doan export --prototype` still assembled the canvas viewer, the agent's file and the reference were the same viewer bytes, so a wrong page kind passed; the X2 fail fixture failed only on its command. The command check also required the final path as `-o`, so exporting to another path and copying the file to the requested one failed.
- **Root cause**: equality with a reference regenerated by the code under test proves the file came from that code, not that it is the page the task asked for: when the code regresses, the reference regresses with it. Command intent (canvas, frame, `--prototype`) and the artifact's location were checked as one thing.
- **Net**: caught by the independent review of the eval batch (Claude), which compared an ordinary export with a prototype one, before the first recorded round, so the check could still change without a new scenario id. The implementer's unit tests and the self-test passed, because their reference shared the incomplete assembly.
- **Prevention**: pair a regenerated-output equality with properties of the requested result that do not come from that run, and a contrast case: X2's `output_not_canvas_viewer` requires the prototype tab title and the no-JavaScript notice inside a `<noscript>` (matched through the page's `p`/`span` wrapping) and bytes that differ from an ordinary export, and its test makes viewer bytes, even when the reference has the same bytes, fail only that check. Keep command intent apart from where the final file lands. Search: `rg -n 'readFileSync\(reference\).*equals' eval` → X1 and X2; X1 also checks inlined assets and local references, X2 the page kind.

### 2026-11-14 — A queue taken back as a draft was still sent by the flush already under way

- **Area**: `app/src/main/conversation/service.ts` (`flush`, `archive`, `stopRuns`, `giveBack`); tests in `service.handoff.test.ts` 'a queue taken back before its flush sends' (an archive, a `stopRuns`).
- **Symptom**: a done turn's queue is flushed a tick after the terminal event's handling. An archive or a Stop landing in that tick handed the queue back as a draft, yet the flush still sent the copy it had captured, so the message went out and also sat in the composer as a draft; after an archive the send could restore the archived conversation. The gap predates the multi-handoff work; that work made it reachable more often, because a handoff turn's queue now settles after several awaited starts.
- **Root cause**: `flush` captured the queued items and their attachment ids, then deferred the send with `Promise.resolve().then` and checked nothing when it ran. The paths that give a queue back (`archive`, `stopRuns`, a handoff cancel) drop the live entry's hold but never touch that captured copy, so dropping the hold was not enough on its own.
- **Net**: caught by the third independent review round (Codex) of the handoff wiring; the earlier rounds and the implementer's tests ran a flush to completion and never put an archive or a Stop between the terminal event and the deferred send.
- **Prevention**: a deferred callback that acts on state captured before the deferral must first check that it still owns it: the same live entry and the same hold object (`this.live.get(id) !== live || live.holding !== hold`), and a conversation still current. Test each such callback by landing an archive and a `stopRuns` between the capture and the callback. Search: `rg -n "Promise\.resolve\(\)\.then|queueMicrotask|setImmediate\(" app/src/main` and check each hit in production code rechecks ownership; at this fix the other hits re-read their state when they run (`wakeDue` reads `schedules` afresh). Related: 2026-10-27 'Awaited conversation work outlived its current turn', 2026-11-13 'A Stop pressed while a send was still being prepared did not stop it'.

### 2026-11-13 — One missing handoff in an eval run was reported as three failed checks

- **Area**: `eval/lib/handoff.ts` (`handoffsForCanvases`), `eval/lib/handoff-canvases.test.ts`; R11 fail fixture.
- **Symptom**: an R11 run that handed off to one of its two new canvases, after writing it and with a non-empty message, failed `handoff_each_canvas_once` and also `canvases_written_before_requests` and `handoff_messages_not_empty`. Read by check id, as `/agent-eval` records rows and U-issues, the run said the agent asked before writing and sent an empty message, neither of which happened.
- **Root cause**: the per-request checks were gated on the aggregate count (`eachOnce && …`), so a count failure turned every per-request check false. The negative tests asserted only `failed(log).includes("handoff_each_canvas_once")`, which passes whatever else fails.
- **Net**: caught by the independent review of the eval batch (Claude) reading the self-test's `checks.json`; the implementer's tests and the self-test passed, since the fail fixture only has to fail at least one check.
- **Prevention**: keep completeness (count, paths) in one check and judge per-item validity only over the items present (`accepted.length > 0 && accepted.every(…)`); for a single-cause negative case assert the exact set of failed ids (`assert.deepEqual(failed(log), ["…"])`), not `includes`. Search: `rg -n "once &&|eachOnce &&" eval/scenarios eval/lib --glob '!*.test.ts'` → `eval/scenarios/R10/checks.ts` `canvas_written_before_request` is still gated on `once`, so a run with two requests also reports the canvas as written late; and `rg -n "\.includes\(\"handoff" eval/lib/handoff-canvases.test.ts` → three negative cases still use `includes`.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-15, `eval/scenarios/R10/checks.ts`, `eval/lib/handoff-canvases.test.ts`)**: the two places this entry's search named stayed open until the `/audit` maintainability review; R10's `canvas_written_before_request` now judges every accepted request for the new canvas and the negative cases assert exact failed sets; `rg -n "once &&|eachOnce &&" eval/scenarios eval/lib --glob '!*.test.ts'` and `rg -n '(failed|failures)\(.*\)\.includes\(' eval --glob '*.test.ts'` for negatives that still assert one id among others (not all single-cause; the handoff cases are now exact).
- **Recurrence (2026-11-16, `eval/scenarios/R10/checks.ts`; found by the `/audit` maintainability review)**: `handoff_path_is_new_canvas` and `handoff_message_not_empty` still read only `accepted[0]`, so a bad second accepted request showed only as a count failure; both now judge every accepted request; `rg -n 'accepted\[0\]' eval --glob '!*.test.ts'` (R10's remaining hit only picks the message shown in the detail).

### 2026-11-13 — A Stop pressed while a send was still being prepared did not stop it

- **Area**: `app/src/main/conversation/service.ts` (`interrupt`, `checkSend`, `submit`, `flush`, `start`); test `service.usageLimit.test.ts` 'a Stop while a send is prepared' (a scheduled send, a queue going out after a done turn, a person's own send).
- **Symptom**: a Stop that arrived while a send waited on `prepare` (the live entry busy, not yet accepted) put an interrupted end in the conversation, yet once `prepare` resolved the send was accepted and `runner.send` still ran, so the agent got a message the person had stopped, and the thread could hide the turn behind its terminal fence. A person's own send had the gap on develop; the usage-limit schedule, whose send at the reset goes through the same path, made it easy to hit.
- **Root cause**: `interrupt` on an entry not yet accepted only buffered an `interrupted` event; nothing marked the pending submit cancelled, and the checks before acceptance looked only at the live entry's identity, which the Stop did not change. `sendTurn` ran even when buffered events had already ended the turn.
- **Net**: caught by the second independent review (Codex) with a deferred-`prepare` probe; the first review's race list and the implementer's tests stopped at the post-limit usage read and never paused a send inside `prepare`.
- **Prevention**: a cancel of work that is past its last await but not yet accepted must leave a mark the acceptance checks read (`live.cancelled`, checked in `checkSend` after prepare and after open), and no send may follow buffered terminal events. Test each await of a send path by holding it with a deferred promise and pressing Stop there. Search: `rg -n "await .*prepare|checkSend\(" app/src/main/conversation/service.ts` and confirm each acceptance path rechecks the cancel mark.

### 2026-11-13 — Codex's usage-limit failure was reported as an unknown error

- **Area**: `app/src/main/conversation/transport.ts` (Codex error classification); test `transport.test.ts` (Codex “You've hit your usage limit”).
- **Symptom**: a Codex turn refused with “You've hit your usage limit … or try again at …” ended as an unknown failure instead of “Codex reached a usage limit”, so the usage-limit copy, Edit and resend wording and the usage-limit schedule never applied to Codex.
- **Root cause**: the classifier matched only the wording Codex used before; the newer message (seen in a Codex 0.144.2 rollout on this Mac) matched none of the `rate_limited` patterns.
- **Net**: found while building fixtures for the usage-limit schedule from real Codex session records; no test held a real Codex limit message.
- **Prevention**: classifier patterns come from recorded provider messages kept as fixtures, and a new provider version's failure text is added to them when seen. Search: `rg -n "usage limit|rate_limited" app/src/main/conversation/transport.ts app/src/main/conversation/fixtures`.

### 2026-11-13 — A toolbar control that turned unavailable while focused was mounted anew and focus fell to the page

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/components/ui/tooltip.tsx` (`TooltipTrigger`, `OwnDescription`), its `aria-disabled` callers (`CanvasTools.tsx` Play prototype, Capture area and Export, `CanvasConversationList.tsx`, `ConversationDeleteDialog.tsx`, `SettingsMcpConnectors.tsx`); test `components/ui/tooltip.test.tsx` 'an asChild trigger keeps one tree whether its control is available or not'.
- **Symptom**: with the size bubble's Play focused, Esc stepped the selection out; the bubble went, focus was handed to the toolbar's Play, and it ended on `<body>`. The same happened to any `asChild` control that turned unavailable while it had focus: Play when an agent removed its frame's mark, Capture area when the draft filled, Export when an export started.
- **Root cause**: `TooltipTrigger` rendered `own ? <OwnDescription control={children} /> : children`. Becoming unavailable (`aria-disabled` with its own `aria-describedby`) changed the component between Radix's trigger and the control, so React unmounted the button and mounted a new one, and the browser moved focus off the removed node. The extra layer existed only to drop Radix's added description id, so nothing in its purpose hinted that it changed the element's identity.
- **Net**: caught by the frame-prototype `/design-sync` real-app probe (bubble focus loss read `document.activeElement` as `body`); the first fix refocused from an App layout effect, and the independent reviewer traced the general cause. Missed by every unit test: the markup and element-tree tests render one state at a time, and the 2026-11-07 tooltip fix that added the layer was checked with forced-open tooltips, never with a control changing state while focused.
- **Prevention**: a wrapper that adapts props for some states keeps the same component tree in every state and decides inside it (here `OwnDescription` always wraps an `asChild` control and drops the id only when `own`). Test identity by rendering both states and comparing the child element's `type`, and in real-app probes flip a focused control's availability and read `document.activeElement`. Search for state-dependent wrappers: `rg -n --glob '*.tsx' --glob '!*.test.*' '\? <[A-Z][A-Za-z.]*[^>]*/?>\s*:\s*children\b|\?\s*children\s*:\s*<[A-Z]' app/src/renderer` (2026-11-13: only `tooltip.tsx`'s asChild/plain split, which does not change with state).

### 2026-11-13 — A native probe's ⌥-click left Option held for the whole desktop

- **Area**: native-input probe tools built from `CGEvent` (the canvas-text-copy PoC's `.scratch/runtime-test/canvas-text-copy/native/input.swift`, and the same pattern as the canvas-hand-tool probe's `mouse.swift`).
- **Symptom**: after a probe step posted an ⌥-click as mouse events carrying `.maskAlternate`, every later "plain" click of the run reached the canvas as an ⌥-click: presses passed through to the draft, focus stayed in the iframe, Tab walked the draft's controls and a later run's drags started no text range. `CGEventSource.flagsState(.hidSystemState)` read `0x20080100` (Option set) after the probe ended, so the person's own desktop had Option held for about five minutes until a `flagsChanged` event cleared it.
- **Root cause**: posting an event with modifier flags to `.cghidEventTap` updates the window server's modifier state, and nothing posted a matching flag release; mouse events created from a `.hidSystemState` source without explicit flags then inherited the stuck Option.
- **Net**: caught because the results contradicted the first run (a plain click left focus in the iframe) and the HID flags were read. The runs taken with the stuck flag were discarded and rerun.
- **Prevention**: a native-input probe sets `flags` on every event it posts (empty for a plain one), posts a `flagsChanged` release with no flags after any event that carried a modifier, and reads `CGEventSource.flagsState(.hidSystemState)` before and after the run, failing the run when a modifier remains. Search: `rg -n --no-ignore "maskAlternate|maskCommand|maskControl|maskShift|\.flags" --glob '*.swift' .scratch` before reusing a probe tool, and check each modifier event has its release.

### 2026-11-13 — A Claude CLI killed before its own cleanup left a registry record that refused every resume in its profile

- **Area**: `app/src/main/conversation/sessions.ts` (`registryIdentity`), `app/src/main/conversation/io.ts` (`claudeRecordVerdict`, `claudeOccupancy`), and the kills that leave records: `runners.ts` (`afterTurnCleanup` "kill", the empty-set kill), `process.ts` (`OwnedGroup.kill`, the 0.5 s SIGKILL of `stop`).
- **Symptom**: in the packaged app with Claude Code 2.1.323, a send fired at `turn.done` was refused `session_unavailable` (canvas conversation 3 of 3; a project conversation after its judgement), and so was a send after a SIGTERM stop that escalated to SIGKILL. Until a later CLI swept the record, every other Claude resume in that profile was refused too, also after an app restart. A live record of another conversation (a turn running a foreground command) refused any other send in the profile, a condition already on develop.
- **Root cause**: two independent changes met. The CLI deletes its own `sessions/<pid>.json` about 30 ms after a done turn, so the turn-end SIGKILL (5-7 ms after `turn.done`), and the SIGKILL 0.5 s into a SIGTERM stop that the CLI did not finish in time, left the record behind. And 2.1.323 writes the record without `procStart`, which `registryIdentity` required, so every record, dead or alive, had an unreadable identity and made the whole profile `unknown`. The fail-closed scan was right to distrust a record it cannot read, but liveness could still be asked from the pid alone.
- **Net**: caught by the real-app check with the installed CLI; the PoC's SIGKILL runs passed because they checked only the transcript and the resume of the same session with 2.1.322 and 2.1.284, whose records carry `procStart` (its real-state check found `~/.claude/sessions/` empty before and after the runs; a later CLI sweeps a leftover record, which the real-app check also saw). Unit tests missed it: every registry fixture carried `procStart`, and an absent one was pinned as unreadable.
- **Prevention**: when Doan ends an external CLI by a signal it cannot handle, list what the CLI cleans up on a normal exit (registry records, lock files, key files) and test the next admission with that leftover in place. A registry field Doan needs may disappear in a newer CLI: keep a reader that degrades to a weaker check (pid only) before failing the whole profile, and pin the record shape of each new installed version in a fixture (the 2.1.323 shape is in `io.test.ts` 'records without procStart'). Related: 2026-11-12 'One Claude registry record Doan could not accept…'. Search: `rg -n "procStart" app/src/main --glob '!*.test.ts'` for identity fields the scan requires (run 2026-11-13: only `sessions.ts` and `io.ts`, both now optional), and `rg -n '\.kill\(\)|"SIGKILL"' app/src/main --glob '!*.test.ts'` for kills of a CLI that keeps state of its own (run 2026-11-13: the Claude runner's two `kill()` calls and `OwnedGroup`'s escalation; `cli.ts` and `onboarding/machine.ts` kill Doan's own CLI and clone, which keep no registry).

### 2026-11-13 — Space over the canvas showed no hand until the pointer moved, and a pan ending there left the hand

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/CanvasController.ts` (`syncLayer`, `hover-hand`), `app/src/main/canvasScript.ts` (`cursorSheet`), `cli/internal/export/viewer/shell.js` (`syncSurface`, `pan` message), `cli/internal/export/viewer/measure.js` (`showHand`).
- **Symptom**: with the pointer resting over the canvas iframe, holding Space or pressing H kept the canvas's own cursor (arrow, crosshair, I-beam) instead of the drag layer's `grab`; it changed only once the pointer moved. The reverse held too: Space held, pointer moved, pointer resting, Space released left `grab` over the canvas until the next move. Over the board's margin both were fine. The export viewer did the same.
- **Root cause**: the canvas is an out-of-process frame (the viewer's sandboxed `srcdoc` iframe too, in Chromium), and Chromium shows the cursor of the frame the pointer last moved over until a real pointer move routes to another frame. A layer raised in the app document under a resting pointer therefore cannot change what shows, while a cursor change inside the canvas document can. In the other direction the app document holds the cursor after a move over the layer, and when the layer goes down Chromium recomputes it over the iframe only when the iframe element's own computed `cursor` changes, and reliably only after a frame without the layer has been drawn. A change in the same task worked only sometimes, and a static `cursor` on the iframe did nothing. Neither `webContents.sendInputEvent` nor a CDP `Input.dispatchMouseEvent` move handed the cursor back, even though the CDP move reached the canvas.
- **Net**: the person caught it in the installed app. A probe that read the OS cursor (`NSCursor.currentSystemCursor`, matched against AppKit's cursor images) while moving the real pointer with posted `CGEvent` moves reproduced it and confirmed each fix. Playwright's synthetic mouse input missed it: the probe built on it saw `grab` over the frame, and webContents `cursor-changed` reported values that disagreed with the screen. Native reads were also disturbed by other windows coming to the front and by posted moves that sometimes did not reach the canvas, so each read now checks the window under the pointer. Code review caught a follow-up: the new hover command sent to a still-loading iframe moved the hover seq ahead of the new document and dropped its hovers.
- **Prevention**: anything that changes the cursor over the canvas from the app document (a layer, an attribute on `.area` or `#board`) also needs the canvas document's cursor (an adopted sheet, as for the crosshair while picking and the hand now), plus an iframe `cursor` value change two frames after it goes away. Check it with a real pointer and the OS cursor, never Playwright's mouse. A new app-to-canvas command on the hover or selection seq is not sent while `loading` (the iframe's script cannot hear it yet). Search: `rg -n "cursor: ?(grab|crosshair|grabbing)" app/src/renderer/styles.css cli/internal/export/viewer/shell.css` (run 2026-11-13: `.drag-layer` and the viewer's `.board[data-pan]` are covered; `.capture-layer`'s `crosshair` rises the same way and is unchecked; `.area[data-picking]` pairs with the canvas's crosshair sheet).

### 2026-11-12 — One Claude registry record Doan could not accept hid every terminal session continue in its profile

- **Area**: `app/src/main/conversation/io.ts` (`claudeOccupancy`), `app/src/main/conversation/sessions.ts` (`registryIdentity`, `registryRecordVerdict`, `mergeOccupancy`); tests in `io.test.ts` and `sessions.test.ts`.
- **Symptom**: while a Claude desktop app Code session was open (bundled claude-code 2.1.309), no Claude terminal session in that profile could be continued; the continue was simply not offered, with no reason shown. Any live non-interactive record (`kind` `bg`, `daemon`), a stale malformed record left by a crash, or a record removed between `readdir` and the read had the same effect.
- **Root cause**: the scan validated every record's fields before checking whether its process was alive, and returned `unknown` for the whole profile at the first record it could not accept, including records of ended processes and records of sessions other than the one being resumed. The fail-closed reason (a record whose `sessionId` cannot be trusted may hold the target) only applies to live records, and a live intact record can only conflict when its `sessionId` is the target.
- **Net**: reported from use and kept in BACKLOG; the read-only probe on 2026-11-12 found the desktop records of 2.1.321 pass every stage, so the original trigger was version-specific, but the rule still blocked on the other cases above. Missed because the tests pinned "fails the whole profile closed on any other version" as the intended end state and every fixture held one record.
- **Prevention**: a fail-closed scan over many external records judges each record by liveness first (skip what belongs to an ended process), then only against the item being acted on, and merges the verdicts (occupied over unknown over free) after reading all of them, instead of returning at the first record it cannot accept. Test it with mixed directories (an ended malformed record plus a live target record; an untrusted live record plus a live target record). Search: `rg -n 'return "unknown"' app/src/main/conversation/io.ts` for per-entry early returns inside a loop.

### 2026-11-12 — An arrow key on the setup panel's storage radio dropped focus to the heading

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/Onboarding.tsx` (`StorageCard` in the setup panel, `useProjectFocus`), `app/src/renderer/projectStep.ts` (`setStorage`)
- **Symptom**: In first run's and Add Projects' setup panel, moving between Include in Git and Keep out of Git with an arrow key left keyboard focus on the panel heading instead of the radio, so the next arrow key did nothing.
- **Root cause**: Keep out of Git outside a Git repository made the radio change re-plan the folder (a new inspection to add or drop the `.git/` line). The re-plan marks the session busy, and busy disables every control on the scene, the focused radio included; a disabled element loses focus, and the scene's focus recovery sent it to the heading. Before, the radio only changed local state, so nothing was disabled under the focused control. Unit tests checked the final plan and the radio's checked state, never `document.activeElement` across the busy window.
- **Net**: Caught by the `/design-sync` measurement of keyboard focus rings (a real Tab and arrow key in the built app, computed style of the focused element). Missed by the unit tests and by two static reviews, which read the radio as a plain value change.
- **Prevention**: when a control's own change starts async work that sets a scene-wide busy/disabled flag, record the focused node before the change and restore it after (`keepFocus` in `useProjectFocus`), and pin it with a test that presses the key and asserts `document.activeElement` after the work settles (`projectFocus.test.ts`). Grep `disabled={busy}` / `disabled={[^}]*busy` on controls that also have an `onChange` that calls an async session method. Check with `rg -n 'disabled=\{busy\}' app/src/renderer` and inspect any `onChange`/radio among the hits.

### 2026-11-12 — A ui-state test waited on the temp file's listing and released a gate that did not exist yet

- **Area**: `app/src/main/uiState.test.ts` ("a write already started does not rename over a later flushSync").
- **Symptom**: the test timed out at 20 s on CI (main push run 43155111199, shard 2/3); it passed locally and on other runs.
- **Root cause**: the fake `writeFile` assigned `release` only after the real write resolved, but the test went on as soon as the temp file was listed in the directory, and the file is listed from `open(wx)`, before the write finishes. When the 50 ms `vi.waitFor` poll landed between the two, `release()` called the initial no-op, the fake waited on a promise nobody would resolve, and `store.idle()` never settled. The test below it ("flushSync also writes a value whose debounced write has started…") sets its flag and `release` in the same tick and never had the gap.
- **Net**: caught by CI. Missed because the window is a few ms on an idle machine; a repro that holds 120 ms between `open` and the write hangs on the old shape every run and passes on the fixed one (the gate is created before the write and the test waits on a flag set after it).
- **Prevention**: a test that hands control to a fake through a callback (`release`, `resolve`) waits on a signal the fake sets in the same tick as that callback, or creates the callback before the fake runs; never on an outside observation (a file listed, a log line, a process started) that can come before the fake reaches its await. Search: `rg -n 'waitFor\(\(\) => expect\((readdirSync|existsSync)' app/src --glob '*.test.*'` and check that each waited-on file is written after whatever the test touches next (2026-11-12: the six other hits wait on an end state or a marker written after the work; `thumbnails.test.ts` sets its gate's flag and `release` in the same tick).

### 2026-11-12 — The docked buddy stood still for good after its WebGL context was lost

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/dockedBuddyShell.ts` (`rig`, `tick`, `wake`); tests in `dockedBuddyShell.test.ts` ("a context lost after it was ready rebuilds the rig…", "a rig that fails to build is tried again…", "a frame requested but never delivered…").
- **Symptom**: in the released 0.1.7 the docked buddy stopped animating until the app restarted: it stood still on the composer, and a click moved it between the composer and the canvas toolbar at once with no hide or rise. Seen after the update's relaunch and after a minimize and restore.
- **Root cause**: on `webglcontextlost`, a texture failure or a missing context, the rig went to the still image (`ready = false`) and nothing ever built it again; only a buddy change or leaving the workspace replaced the rig. Time runs only while `rig.ready`, so every later wake advanced one frame with time stopped, which applies surface changes instantly. A GPU process reset loses every context at once; killing the GPU process in an isolated app reproduced the exact state (`data-state="static"`, still image shown, loop parked, clicks jumping). A plain minimize and restore did not lose the context on the probing Mac, so the user's trigger is inferred, not observed. The loop also trusted a pending `requestAnimationFrame` id (`next` requested only when `raf` was 0), so a callback that never arrived would have parked it for good; that was not observed.
- **Net**: caught by the person on the installed app with a screen recording. Missed because the unit test pinned "goes still when the context is lost" as the intended end state, and the runtime probes ran under Playwright, which keeps `document.hidden` false and focus emulated (BACKLOG listed a real minimize as unverified); none lost a context. The reproduction needed a probe without Playwright: Electron spawned with `--remote-debugging-port` and `--inspect`, the window driven from main, the renderer read with plain `Runtime.evaluate`.
- **Prevention**: a long-lived WebGL surface must rebuild itself after a lost context or a failed build, bounded (here once per stretch it is seen), and its test must cover the recovery, not only the fallback. After a lost context the rebuild waits until the GPU is back (`webglcontextrestored`, else backed-off tries outlasting Chromium's block), never at once: two GPU resets in a row make Chromium refuse WebGL to the page for about two minutes with no restore event (measured 119 s in an isolated Electron 45 app), so a rebuild at once or a few quick tries spend themselves on a GPU that is not there. A self-continuing `requestAnimationFrame` loop re-requests on wake instead of trusting a held id. Probes of visibility, focus or minimize behaviour run without Playwright's emulation. Search: `rg -n "webglcontextlost" app/src --glob '!*.test.*'` (2026-11-12: also `Doany.tsx`, Home's buddy, which stays still after a lost context until Home mounts it again on the next visit; follow-up candidate).
- **Recurrence (2026-11-15, audit)**: Home's buddy (`Doany.tsx`) still stayed still after a lost context until Home mounted it again; `keepBuddy` now mounts it again on `webglcontextrestored` or after backed-off tries (1 s doubling, at most 8) once it had drawn, pinned by `Doany.test.tsx` and `doanyRig.test.ts`. A real GPU reset on Home is not verified. Search: `rg -n 'webglcontextlost' app/src --glob '!*.test.*'` and check each surface also handles `webglcontextrestored`.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-18, audit)**: the docked buddy's once-per-seen-stretch flag was reset by any unseen stretch, so after a hide and show a later loss while seen rebuilt at once instead of waiting for the GPU; a ready rig's loss now marks the stretch rebuilt, pinned by `dockedBuddyShell.test.ts` ('after the buddy was hidden and shown again…'). Every test had started with the flag set. Check each recovery flag against a hide/show cycle before the loss; search: `rg -n 'rebuilt' app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'`.

### 2026-11-11 — Large canvases were cut off for good under a rounded four-corner clip

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/styles.css` (`.workspace > .doan-canvas-panel`, `.doan-canvas-panel > .doan-canvas-head`, `.doan-canvas-panel > .area`), `cli/internal/export/viewer/shell.css` (`.doan-canvas-panel` and its `::after` corner cover); the structural test in `app/src/main/exportViewer.test.ts` ("the board iframe's ancestors never clip with four equal radii").
- **Symptom**: on a DPR 2 display a canvas wider or taller than 8192 CSS px showed the board background past a fixed line, from the first render and after zooming out, and it never filled in: in the app once the conversation panel was folded, and in every export (Electron 45 and desktop Chrome 157). With the panel open the same canvas drew whole.
- **Root cause**: under an ancestor that clips (`overflow` not `visible`) with the same border radius on all four corners, Chromium draws a cross-origin (out-of-process) iframe only within 16384 device px of its compositing-rect origin, the GPU's maximum texture size. A later measurement on another Mac (Apple M1, external display at DPR 2) found a trigger in the canvas too: the cut appeared only when the canvas held a scrolling container with rounded corners (`overflow-y: auto`, content overflowing) past the 8192 CSS px line; the same container near the origin, or a canvas without one, drew whole under the same clip. Folding the conversation panel turned the canvas panel's `24px 0 0 24px` corners into 24px on all four, and the export panel always had them. Measured, not read in Chromium's source: a rectangular clip, unequal radii, an unclipped radius, DPR 1 or a same-process iframe all drew the canvas whole. Nothing in the layout was wrong, which made it look like a GPU memory or tiling limit.
- **Net**: caught by the person on large real canvases; reproduced by isolated probes that paint the board magenta and compare the cut-off line with the compositing origin + 8192 CSS px (1–5 px apart). Missed because no check ever looked at the clip shape above the iframe, the canvases used in tests are far below 8192 px, and the default layout's unequal radii happened to avoid it. The fix keeps the look: the app's panel paints its rounded white without clipping (the header clips its top corners, the area only its bottom ones, so the area's clip can never have four equal radii; the window behind is native vibrancy, so a painted cover cannot match it), and the export panel clips square under a cover in the window's grey. Corner pixels matched the old build within 4/255 on edge anti-aliasing.
- **Prevention**: no ancestor of a cross-origin canvas iframe clips with four equal non-zero radii; round corners above it with a clip whose radii can never all be equal, or with a cover over a square clip. The structural test above pins both stylesheets; a real-app probe of a canvas layout walks up from the iframe and checks each ancestor's computed `overflow` and four radii in every panel layout. Search run: `rg -n "<iframe|createElement\(\"iframe\"\)" app/src cli/internal/export/viewer --glob '!*.test.*'` (2026-11-11: the canvas board, the export viewer and the hidden thumbnails window, whose page sets no radius) and `rg -n "overflow: (hidden|clip)[^}]*border-radius|border-radius[^}]*overflow: (hidden|clip)" app/src/renderer/styles.css cli/internal/export/viewer/shell.css` (2026-11-11: `.right-stack`, the conversation panel, attachment and canvas thumbnails, the usage bar and the export's `.doan-panel`; none holds an iframe).
- **Recurrence 2026-11-14**: `app/src/renderer/styles.css` `.pw-panel` (prototype window). The content panel clipped its run iframe with four equal 24 px radii; it was harmless while the iframe followed the panel's size, and became this entry's shape once Fit kept a frame at its marked size (any mark up to 99999 px). The 2026-11-11 search ran before the prototype window existed, and the structural test pinned only the chains it knew, so a new cross-origin iframe host had no check. Caught by an independent review (VU) before merge, not drawn. Fixed by splitting the clip (`.pw-content` clips the top corners, `.pw-stage` the bottom ones; the panel paints its own, the window behind is native vibrancy so a cover cannot match it), and `exportViewer.test.ts` now walks the prototype window's chain too (red on `.pw-panel { overflow: clip }` before the fix). Prevention: a change that adds a cross-origin iframe host, or lets an iframe grow past its panel, adds that chain to the structural test. Search run 2026-11-14: the iframe search above finds the canvas board, the export viewer, the prototype window (all three pinned) and the thumbnails window (no radius); the clip search no longer lists `.pw-panel`.

### 2026-11-11 — A runner test closed before the fake CLI exited and left an unhandled "Operation aborted"

- **Area**: `app/src/main/conversation/runners.test.ts` ('ending a process that waits for its suggestion').
- **Symptom**: the develop gate (run 42999279246, a docs-only commit) passed all 10197 tests but failed on one unhandled rejection, `Error: Operation aborted` (`errorClass: 'aborted'`) from the Claude Agent SDK, after 'leaves a process alone once its suggestion came'.
- **Root cause**: the tests started a turn with `void runner.send(...)` and let the helper's `finally` call `runner.close()` as soon as the suggestion arrived. The fake CLI exits 20 ms after the suggestion; a close inside that window aborts the Query's controller, and the SDK's pending wait for the process exit rejects with "Operation aborted" (`waitForExit` checks the signal when `exit` fires). `send` rethrows an error that is neither a nonzero exit nor a kill, and nobody held its promise. Production holds it: the service's `runner.send(...).catch(...)` handles the same rejection.
- **Net**: caught by Vitest's unhandled-rejection check on CI. Missed locally because the 20 ms window rarely lines up with `vi.waitFor`'s polling; delaying the fake's exit to 400 ms reproduced it on every run, and the fixed tests pass with that delay.
- **Prevention**: a test keeps every runner promise it starts and settles it (`await sending`, or `.catch(() => {})` when the rejection is expected) before the body returns and the helper closes the runner; a fire-and-forget call in a test is a rejection nobody sees. Search: `rg -n '^\s*void [a-zA-Z]+\.[a-zA-Z]+\(' app/src/main/conversation --glob '*.test.ts'` (2026-11-11: only `service.test.ts` `void skills.skills(...)`, whose read never settles).
- **Recurrence (2026-11-16, codebase audit)**: two auth manager tests left a sign-in, check or sign-out running past their end, whose 1 s status timeout recreated `profiles/` under the removed root, and `acpRunner.test.ts` and `installCommand.test.ts` did not remove every folder they made, leaving about 46,000 folders in `$TMPDIR`; tests now wait for their own attempts (or quit the manager) and remove each folder they create; to find work outliving a test, run it with an empty temporary folder and list what remains: `T=$(mktemp -d); TMPDIR=$T npx vitest run <file>; ls $T`.

### 2026-11-11 — The working-frame marker blinked on every agent write to the canvas

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/CanvasController.ts` (`drawWork`, `workMarkFrame`), `workFrameRect` in `app/src/renderer/agentIndicator.ts`; tests in `CanvasController.test.ts` ("working-frame marker").
- **Symptom**: during an agent's work turn the working frame's highlight blinked fast while the agent wrote the canvas file repeatedly. PRODUCT §5 'Working frame' says it sits on its frame at once on reload.
- **Root cause**: `drawWork` treated "the document is loading" as "no working frame" and set `hidden = true` on every reload, then unhid it when the next layout arrived (464 ms the first time, then 53–100 ms). Unhiding an element restarts its CSS animations (`doan-work-glow`, `doan-work-travel`), so each write showed as a gap plus a restarted glow. A reload of an already laid-out document keeps its previous layout and status `null`, so there was a rect to stay on. The first fix still hid the marker once per run: a reload's first layout can report every frame as 0 × 0 before measuring, and `workFrameRect` reads a 0 × 0 frame as no rect, which `drawWork` took as "frame gone".
- **Net**: caught by the person watching a real agent turn, then confirmed by the lead's probe recording `hidden` and animation time per frame. Missed by the unit test, which pinned the hide during reload as intended behavior ("goes while the canvas reloads"), and by probes that only checked the marker's final position. The 0 × 0 case was caught by the isolated probe after the first fix and by an independent review at the same time; the unit harness sends measured layouts unless a test asks otherwise.
- **Prevention**: an overlay with a looping animation must not toggle `hidden`/`display` across a transient state (reload, re-layout); keep it on the last known geometry and hide only on a real absence, and tell "not measured yet" (all frames 0 × 0) from "frame gone". Tests for such overlays spy on every `hidden` and `className` write across repeated reloads and an unmeasured first layout, not just the end state; real-app probes sample per animation frame and check that `getAnimations()` time never drops. Search run: `rg -n "infinite" app/src/renderer/styles.css app/src/renderer/ds/*.css` and `rg -n "this\.loading \|\||this\.loading \? " app/src/renderer/CanvasController.ts` (2026-11-11: the other infinite animations are the spinner, the conversation caret and shimmer, the agent glow and its caret; the agent glow in `CanvasAgent.tsx` is not gated on reload. The remaining `this.loading` gates are follow, capture-area, pick and timer checks, none of which hide an animated overlay).

### 2026-11-11 — The auto-registration restart test read the folder list before it was saved

- **Area**: `app/src/main/shell.test.ts`, "the auto flag survives a restart".
- **Symptom**: release run 42941499765 attempt 1 failed with only the parent in the restarted shell, while the test expected the parent and the auto-registered child; previous local and PR gates passed.
- **Root cause**: `adopted()` waited for `auto-folders.json`, the first write in the serialized save, but the restart also needs the following `folders.json` write. `Shell.dispose()` closes watchers and the server; it does not await that save. Restart could read the old list. Ignoring an orphan mark after an interrupted save is the existing ARCHITECTURE §7 contract, not evidence that loading lost a persisted child.
- **Net**: CI caught the timing window. A controlled filesystem probe held adoption's folder-list rename until the restart read had captured the old list and reproduced the same missing child three times. With the same held rename released when the persistence check reads the list, the corrected test passed. The final test keeps the exact initial loading entries and the loaded auto flag assertions, adding a bounded wait for the exact saved parent-and-child list before disposal; ordinary repeated runs alone did not establish the cause. No production behavior changed.
- **Prevention**: wait for every persisted prerequisite a restart consumes, not the first file in an ordered save or an unrelated disposal promise. Search: `rg -n 'autoSaved\(|survives a restart|shell.dispose\(' app/src/main/shell.test.ts`. The shared `adopted()` helper also serves live-state tests, so keep the additional list barrier in the restart case; the neighboring adoption test already checks the saved list separately. Keep diagnostic probes under `.scratch/`, not in the production persistence API.

### 2026-11-10 — A quick second trackpad swipe on the buddy row did nothing: momentum kept the first gesture open

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/coverFlow.ts` (wheel input, now `scrollPosition`, `snapTarget`, `WHEEL_QUIET`), `BuddyFlow` in the Buddy step; the rule came from the draft's handler in `.doan/canvases/buddy-choice.html`.
- **Symptom**: on a real trackpad, two swipes in a row moved the row one card; the second swipe registered only after the person moved the cursor first. Unit tests and the synthetic-wheel probe passed.
- **Root cause**: the rule "one card per gesture, a gesture ends after 160 ms without wheel events" assumed a gesture's events stop when the fingers lift. macOS keeps sending momentum wheel events for 1.3–1.7 s after a swipe, and a second swipe started 40–60 ms into that stream, so the quiet gap never came and both swipes were one gesture. Synthetic `mouse.wheel` probes send no momentum, so nothing but a real trackpad could show it.
- **Net**: caught by the person on a real trackpad, then confirmed with a raw wheel log (deltaX and timestamps per event). Missed by unit tests and the Playwright probe, which modelled a gesture as a short burst followed by silence.
- **Prevention**: horizontal scroll that moves discrete items follows the deltas continuously and snaps to the nearest item once events stop, instead of counting gestures; the quiet timer only decides when to snap. Any wheel handler that infers gestures from timing gets a raw-event log from a real trackpad (two quick swipes, a long flick) before it is accepted, and its tests include a recorded momentum tail. Search run: `rg -n "addEventListener\(\"wheel\"|onWheel" app/src -g '!*.test.*'` (2026-11-10: `CanvasController.ts` page-zoom guard and margin wheel, which scroll continuously and count no gestures, and `BuddyPicker.tsx` (the Buddy row, follows deltas and snaps)).

### 2026-11-09 — An agent planted provider settings in its own profile that a later run would execute outside any sandbox

- **Area**: `app/src/main/agentAuth/fence.ts` (`CLAUDE_SETTINGS`, `CODEX_SETTINGS`, `claudeSettingsDirs`, `codexSettingsFiles`, `codexSettingsDirs`, `claudeFenceSettings`, `codexProfileArgs`); tests `fence.test.ts`.
- **Symptom**: the first fence closed userData but left the run's own profile writable. A fenced Claude or Codex agent could then write its profile's `settings.json`, `config.toml`, `hooks.json`, `rules/`, or a skill, subagent, command or plugin there. The next run reads those files, and the next Claude run (with its sandbox on) or Codex run (outside Full access) has no outer fence: hooks, MCP servers and Codex `notify` named there run outside every sandbox. In a probe a Codex `config.toml` MCP server and `notify` read other profiles and `conversations/` even under Doan's permission profile.
- **Root cause**: "the agent's own profile is the provider's own data" treated the profile as data only. It also holds the provider's configuration, and a provider runs its hooks, MCP servers and plugins as separate processes that its command sandbox does not cover. Each review round found one more surface (settings files, then `skills/`, `agents/`, `plugins/`, then Claude `commands/`, the older skill format that takes the same frontmatter).
- **Net**: caught by independent review rounds of the fence; the runtime probes only tested reads and writes of other profiles, so they never tried writing the run's own settings.
- **Prevention**: the fence closes every profile path the provider reads as configuration to writes (outer fence rules, Claude `denyWrite` and `Edit(...)`, Codex `deny` for settings files and `read` for `skills/`, `plugins/`), and keeps them readable where the agent opens skills itself. When a provider gains a configuration file or directory that can name hooks, MCP servers, plugins or commands, add it to `claudeSettingsDirs`, `codexSettingsFiles` or `codexSettingsDirs` with a `fence.test.ts` case, and probe a write to it from a sandboxed command and from the file tool. Search run: `rg -n "claudeSettingsDirs|codexSettingsFiles|codexSettingsDirs" app/src/main -g '!*.test.ts'` (2026-11-09: only `fence.ts`, used by the outer policy, the Claude flag settings and the Codex profile).

- **Recurrence (2026-11-16, round-3 audit)**: the Claude half of this entry (`claudeFenceSettings`, Claude `denyWrite` and `Edit(...)`, "the next Claude run with its sandbox on") describes a mechanism removed on 2026-11-16: every Doan Claude process that loads agent-writable settings runs in the outer fence with Claude's own sandbox off, and `CLAUDE_SETTINGS` stays for the unfenced Claude auth commands. The ACP providers' profiles get no settings rule because every Copilot process and every ACP list read runs in the outer fence (entry 2026-11-16 'Every Copilot process ran JavaScript…'); `rg -n "CLAUDE_SETTINGS|CODEX_SETTINGS|fencedEverywhere" app/src/main -g '!*.test.ts'`.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-16, round-5 audit and decoy probe)**: `<CODEX_HOME>/.env` was missing from Codex's closed files, and every Codex process loads it into its own environment (codex-cli 0.169.0, `dotenvy`); with a planted `PATH`, the CLI's own `git` calls ran a decoy outside any sandbox (it could nest `sandbox-exec` and read a decoy file). It joins the closed files (outer fence and Doan's Codex permission profiles), and `AuthManager.launch` removes one before any Codex process starts, so a file planted before the fix loads nowhere; when closing a provider's settings, list what its binary reads from its home at startup (dotenv and other environment files), not only its documented configuration; `rg -n 'codexSettingsFiles|CODEX_DOTENV' app/src/main/agentAuth`.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-18, security audit)**: the profile's instruction files (Codex `AGENTS.md`, `AGENTS.override.md`; Claude `CLAUDE.md`) and Codex's stored `sessions/`, `memories/` and `shell_snapshots/` stayed writable, and later threads read them; the instruction files join the closed files (outer fence and Codex permission profiles) and the stored directories are read-only in the permission profiles only, since the Codex process inside the outer fence writes them itself; list what the provider reads back into later threads, not only what names hooks or servers; `rg -n 'codexSettingsFiles|codexStoredDirs|claudeSettingsFiles' app/src/main/agentAuth`.

### 2026-11-09 — A deny on userData silently beat the allows that reopened the run's own paths

- **Area**: `app/src/main/agentAuth/fence.ts` (`fenceDenied`, `claudeFenceSettings`, `codexProfileArgs`); tests `fence.test.ts` (no `denyWrite` path is the profile or its ancestor; no Codex `deny` on userData or an allowed path's ancestor).
- **Symptom**: with Claude's sandbox on, `denyWrite=<userData>` beat `allowWrite=<profile>`, so Bash could not write its own profile, and a `permissions.deny` `Read(//<userData>/**)` hid the profile and this turn's pasted image from the Read tool. In Codex Ask first an approved escalated `doan handoff` failed with "Cannot read the Doan runtime file": Codex applies a permission profile's `deny` entries to an approved escalated command but not the narrower `read` and `write` entries under them.
- **Root cause**: the outer Seatbelt policy works as "deny the parent, reopen children" because its last matching rule wins. Claude's settings and Codex's permission profiles do not follow that rule: a deny beats every allow, and an escalated Codex command keeps only the deny entries. Carrying the Seatbelt shape over to them looked equivalent and failed without an error.
- **Net**: caught by the real-app check (the sandbox-on Claude turn and the Codex handoff). Missed by the PoC, which checked that decoys were blocked but not that every reopened path was still reachable on every path (sandboxed command, file tool, approved escalation).
- **Prevention**: in provider settings, deny by listed name only: the entries off the way to an allowed path (`fenceDenied`), never userData or an allowed path's ancestor. Any probe of a deny rule checks both directions on each execution path: the decoy is blocked, and the run's own profile, `runtime.json` and attachments are reachable, including through an approved escalation. Search run: `rg -n "denyWrite|denyRead|\"deny\"" app/src/main -g '!*.test.ts'` (2026-11-09: only `fence.ts` builds filesystem denies; the other hits are permission-option kinds).

- **Recurrence (2026-11-16, round-3 audit)**: the Claude settings half no longer applies: Claude's own sandbox is off in every Doan Claude process, so no Claude `denyWrite` or `permissions.deny` is built; the deny-by-name rule holds for Codex's permission profiles (`fenceDenied`, `codexProfileArgs`); `rg -n "denyWrite|denyRead|\"deny\"" app/src/main -g '!*.test.ts'`.
### 2026-11-09 — A second Seatbelt policy inside Doan's fence failed every command (exit 71)

- **Area**: `app/src/main/agentAuth/fence.ts` (header comment, `codexMechanism`), `app/src/main/conversation/runners.ts` (`openClaude`, `openCodex`), `prepare.ts` (`claudeSandboxEnabled` sources), `service.ts` (`switchCodexServer`).
- **Symptom**: inside `sandbox-exec -p '(version 1)(allow default)'`, an inner `sandbox-exec` with any rule, `codex sandbox -- /usr/bin/true` and every Bash command of Claude Code with its sandbox on exited 71 with `sandbox_apply: Operation not permitted`. One outer fence for every provider would have broken Codex's Ask first, Accept edits and Plan and every Claude turn whose person turned its sandbox on.
- **Root cause**: macOS refuses to apply a second Seatbelt profile with rules to a process that already runs under one. The common nesting check (`sandbox-exec ... sandbox-exec -p '(version 1)(allow default)' /usr/bin/true`) passes because the inner policy has no rule, so it proves nothing.
- **Net**: caught by the nesting PoC before implementation, using an inner policy with one deny rule and each provider's own sandbox command.
- **Prevention**: a provider that sandboxes its own commands never runs inside the outer fence in that mode; it gets Doan's limits as its own settings (Claude's flag-tier settings, Codex permission profiles), and the choice is made per Claude turn and per Codex server mechanism. A misjudged Claude sandbox shows as Bash exit 71, never as an unfenced run. A nesting check always uses an inner policy with at least one rule. Search run: `rg -n "fencedCommand\(" app/src -g '!*.test.ts'` (2026-11-09: `acpRunner.ts`, every mode; `runners.ts` Codex only in Full access and Claude only with its sandbox judged off).

- **Recurrence (2026-11-16, round-3 audit)**: the per-Claude-turn choice in this Prevention is gone: Claude always runs in the outer fence with its own sandbox off at the flag tier (managed settings that turn it on still fail Bash with exit 71, an accepted limit in ARCHITECTURE §7); the per-server choice stays for Codex (`codexMechanism`); `rg -n "fencedCommand\(|profileFencedCommand\(" app/src -g '!*.test.ts'`.
### 2026-11-09 — Real `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/` writes were blamed on Doan's Antigravity; the writer was the person's own `agy`

- **Area**: runtime probes and real-state comparison (`app/runtime/real-state.ts`), Antigravity runs (`agentAuth/environment.ts`, `antigravity.ts`).
- **Symptom**: probes with an isolated HOME showed new logs, a crash log and `cli.log` in the real `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/`, and once a changed `gemini | antigravity` Keychain item, and BACKLOG recorded them as Doan's Antigravity writing outside its profile. Running Doan's server with Doan's environment, wrapped in recursive listings, Keychain attributes and a process watch, changed nothing; the writes came from Orca.app running the person's `~/.local/bin/agy` about every ten minutes, and the Keychain change matched an `agy` log written in the same second.
- **Root cause**: the comparison saw that something changed but not which process changed it, and `real-state.ts` fingerprints `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli` only by the directory's own `lstat`. Another app on the same Mac runs the same provider's CLI under the real HOME on its own schedule.
- **Net**: caught by a PoC that watched processes (`ps` for `agy`) during the real-state window and matched file names and pids (`crashes/crash_<pid>_*`) with the watched runs. Missed by the earlier probes, which compared before and after only.
- **Prevention**: a real-state change in a provider's own directory during a probe is attributed to a process before it is recorded as Doan's: run a process watch for that provider's CLI (and other apps that run it, such as Orca) over the same window, and compare a recursive listing (relative path, size, mtime), not the directory's `lstat`. Search run: `rg -n "antigravity-cli" app/runtime app/src -g '!*.test.ts'` (2026-11-09: `real-state.ts` lists the directory as one fingerprint entry; `fence.ts` names the profile's own skill path).
- **Recurrence (2026-11-14, project-step-actions `/design-sync` and `/runtime-test`)**: an isolated probe failed its real-state check on `~/Library/Application Support/doan/ui-state.json`. `ps` showed `/Applications/Doan.app` running since 18:21, before the probe began, and the file's mtime matched the person's own use of it; the probe's apps all ran with temporary `--user-data-dir`. The probes then compared every key except that one. Prevention addition: before excluding a real-state key, confirm the writer is a process that was running before the probe (record its pid and start time) and that every app the probe launches has its own `--user-data-dir`; exclude only the key that process writes, and say so in the report. Search run: `rg -n '"ui-state.json"' app/runtime/real-state.ts` (2026-11-14: fingerprinted with the other userData files at line 79).

### 2026-11-09 — An agent of one provider read another provider's Doan profile

- **Area**: `app/src/main/agentAuth/fence.ts`, `app/src/main/conversation/prepare.ts` (`runFence`), `acpRunner.ts`, `runners.ts` (`openClaude`, `openCodex`), `service.ts` (`Prepared.fence`).
- **Symptom**: an Antigravity conversation used its shell tool to read a Claude transcript under `<userData>/agent-auth/profiles/claude/projects/` and answered from it. The same reach covered every profile, the plaintext working copies of vault providers (Copilot's GitHub token included), `conversations/`, the vault and other conversations' pasted images.
- **Root cause**: every Doan profile sits under one userData owned by the same uid, and the agents ran without any limit on it. Per-profile 0700 permissions and per-provider `HOME`/`CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`/`CODEX_HOME` choose where a provider stores its files; they do not stop a process of the same user from reading the others.
- **Net**: seen by chance in a connector PoC. No test or probe checked what an agent could read outside its own profile.
- **Prevention**: every Doan-connection run (`use === "run"`) starts with `Prepared.fence`, and a fence that cannot be built refuses the run (`unknown`, or `process_exited` when `sandbox-exec` cannot start), never runs it unfenced. A new spawn site for a Doan-connection run goes through `fencedCommand` or the provider-settings path, with a runner test that the fenced run starts fenced. A probe for a provider change reads and writes decoys (another profile, a staging `codex-<id>`, `conversations/`, the vault, another conversation's attachment) in Ask first and Full access. Search run: `rg -n "\bspawn\(" app/src/main/conversation app/src/main/agentAuth -g '!*.test.ts'` (2026-11-09: the conversation spawns in `acpRunner.ts` and `runners.ts` are fenced; `manager.ts` and `usageRead.ts` spawn sign-in, status, sign-out, list and usage processes, which run no model tool and are left unfenced by decision).

- **Recurrence (2026-11-16, round-3 audit)**: "left unfenced by decision" in the search note above no longer holds: Claude's list reads, usage read and MCP commands, every Copilot process and every list read but Codex's run in the outer fence; the processes still outside it are listed in ARCHITECTURE §7 'Fence'; `rg -n "options\.spawn\(|\bspawn\(|runProcess\(" app/src/main/agentAuth app/src/main/conversation -g '!*.test.ts'`.
### 2026-11-09 — The canvas panel's agent notice drew at 11px because it carried the inspector's loading-line class

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/CanvasConversationPanel.tsx` (the drawing and promoted notices), `app/src/renderer/styles.css` (`.canvas-notice`, `.shell-worktree > span`), `app/src/renderer/ProjectOverview.tsx` (worktree menu); tests `CanvasConversationPanel.test.tsx`, `ProjectOverview.test.tsx`.
- **Symptom**: the panel's "A project conversation is drawing this canvas" and "Open the current conversation" notices rendered at 11px with 7/12 padding (86px tall) instead of the shared notice's 14/20 with 12/16 padding (128px). Moving the Overview worktree menu to the toned status chip then stretched the chip to 115px; Home's same menu already stretched it.
- **Root cause**: `canvas-notice` is a single-class rule in `styles.css` (font and padding) for the inspector's "Reading styles" line. Added next to `doan-notice`, it overrode the shared notice's font and padding because `styles.css` loads after `ds/surfaces.css` at the same specificity, and its name read like a modifier for notices on the canvas page. In the menus, `.shell-worktree > span { flex: 1 }` meant the label wrapper, but the chip is also a direct `span` child.
- **Net**: missed by unit tests that checked only the notice text, and by `/design-sync`, which left the drawing tile unmeasured because the state could not be reached. Caught by a renderer-wide static survey of font sizes. The chip stretch showed only in the real-app probe.
- **Prevention**: a component class that sets type or spacing on its own must not be reused as a modifier on a design-system component. Check every extra class on a `.doan-notice` element for a standalone font or padding rule (`rg -o 'className="doan-notice[^"]*"' app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'`, then `rg '^\.<class> \{[^}]*(font|padding)' app/src/renderer/styles.css` for each; 2026-11-09: none left). A child-type selector that grows (`> span { flex: 1 }`) must not match a chip in the same row: `rg '> span \{[^}]*flex: 1' app/src/renderer` (2026-11-09: `.shell-worktree`, fixed with `.shell-worktree > .ob-chip { flex: none; }`; `.handoff-head` has one span).

### 2026-11-09 — Export inlined the wrong file after a nested self-closing `<template/>`

- **Area**: `cli/internal/export/base.go` (`templateTag`, `firstBaseHref`), `rewrite.go` (`rewriteMarkup`, also walked by `collectUses` in `customurl.go`), `inline.go` (`documentIDs`); tests `template_test.go`, `base_test.go` 'self-closing template opens'.
- **Symptom**: in `<template><template/></template><style>.inert{mask:var(--icon)}</style>…</template>`, the export took the inert `<style>` as a user of `--icon`, so an active `style="--icon:url(icon.svg)"` resolved from the canvas folder and inlined `.doan/canvases/icon.svg` instead of refusing the missing `.doan/assets/icon.svg`. The new `<base href>` check had the same hole one level up: a `<base>` inside `<template/>…</template>` hid the real, folder-moving base, and the export included files the app would not load.
- **Root cause**: the tokenizer walkers each counted `<template>` depth on their own. The HTML parser ignores the self-closing flag on `<template>` and opens one at any depth, but `rewriteMarkup` counted `<template/>` only at top level, `documentIDs` only start tags, and the first `firstBaseHref` only start tags. A walker that leaves a template too early reads inert content as document content, and `collectUses` feeds that into how active `url()`s resolve.
- **Net**: caught by the independent cross-family review of the base batch and its re-review, each with a reproduced canvas. Missed because every template test used `<template>…</template>`, never the self-closing spelling, and the walkers' depth rules were never compared with each other.
- **Prevention**: every tokenizer walker that skips `<template>` content counts depth through `templateTag` (start or self-closing tag opens at any depth, end tag closes when depth > 0). Search run: `rg -n 'NewTokenizer' cli --glob '!*_test.go'` (2026-11-09: `base.go`, `inline.go` and `rewrite.go` use `templateTag`; `assemble.go` `measureOffset` stops at the head and never enters a template; `canvas.go` uses `html.Parse`, which follows the parser). A new walker's tests include `<template/>` at top level and nested.

### 2026-11-09 — `open -g` to a running Doan brought its window over the app in use

- **Area**: `app/src/main/index.ts` (`activate` handler), `app/runtime/windows.ts` (`showMain`); tests `index.test.ts` 'an activate while another app is in front …'.
- **Symptom**: `doan onboarding start` while the app was running but not answering its socket yet sent `open -g`, and the window came forward over the person's app, against PRODUCT §5 item 0 (start keeps the current app in front).
- **Root cause**: Electron emits `activate` for every reopen, including one `open -g` sends without activating the app, and the handler treated `activate` as a Dock click and showed the window. `BrowserWindow.show()` activates the app, so the background launch became a foreground one.
- **Net**: found while building the background reveal and parked in BACKLOG; the unit tests emitted `activate` with `isActive()` mocked true, so they never separated a reopen from a Dock click. A native probe (`open -g` with Finder in front, then eight real HID Dock clicks) showed `activate` with `isActive()` false for `open -g`, and true for every Dock click even when `did-become-active` came after `activate`. The runtime helper `showMain` relied on the old behaviour (it emits `activate` right after `app.show()`), and `app.focus({ steal: true })` does not activate Doan while another app is in front.
- **Prevention**: run `rg -n 'emit\("activate"\)|on\("activate"' app/src app/runtime` (performed 2026-11-09: only the handler in `index.ts`; tests emit it with `isActive` set explicitly). A handler for an OS "bring forward" event must check `app.isActive()` before showing a window; a test or probe that emits `activate` must set the activation state it means, and a probe that has to show the window while another app is in front uses `second-instance` (`showMain`).

### 2026-11-09 — A quietly opened update popover closed when Agent usage moved focus between two places outside it

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/UpdateHeaderButton.tsx` (`onFocusOutside`); test `UpdateHeaderButton.interaction.test.tsx` "only focus leaving the popover closes it".
- **Symptom**: a stopped restart started from the Doan menu or Settings > About opens the update popover without focus, so it can sit beside Agent usage's open popover. When Agent usage's last provider left, Agent usage closed and moved focus to the page tab, and the update popover closed too, although PRODUCT keeps the other popover usable when one closes.
- **Root cause**: Radix's DismissableLayer treats every `focusin` outside the layer as leaving it (`onFocusOutside`), whether or not focus was ever inside. A popover that opens without taking focus therefore closes on any focus move anywhere, including moves that belong to another popover.
- **Net**: found by reading while recording the two-popover drag defect (BACKLOG); not reproduced in the real app, because a stopped restart needs a packaged copy with a downloaded update. Missed by the unit tests, which drove one popover at a time and never moved focus between two outside places.
- **Prevention**: a Radix popover or dialog that can open without focus (`onOpenAutoFocus` preventing the move) closes on focus only when the `focusin`'s `relatedTarget` was inside it. Search run: `rg -n 'onOpenAutoFocus=.*preventDefault' app/src/renderer -g '!*.test.*'` (2026-11-09: `UpdateHeaderButton.tsx`, now guarded, and `AgentUsageButton.tsx`, which moves focus into its list itself).

### 2026-11-09 — A Locate from a page's sidebar left the page's origin on the old root

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/App.tsx` (`onFolders` focus handling), `projectSettingsRules.ts` (`followProjectSettings`); test `App.settings.test.tsx` "a Locate of the origin's root from a page's sidebar moves the origin".
- **Symptom**: on Project Settings for worktree B entered from workspace A, a Locate of A from the page's sidebar moved A, but Back then went Home instead of to the moved workspace. Add Projects did the same.
- **Root cause**: the origin moved only inside the Project Settings page's own follow rule, which looks at the page's worktree; a reroot of any other root never reached it, and Add Projects and Settings had no follow rule at all. The shared re-root event in App, which every page sees, did not touch the origin.
- **Net**: recorded in BACKLOG from review. The App test reproduced it red; a real-app probe (`.scratch/runtime-test/focus-and-origin/probe.mjs`, folder renamed on disk, the Locate picker stubbed in main) showed Back going Home before the fix and entering the moved workspace after it.
- **Prevention**: state keyed by a root that a page or screen keeps (an origin, a remembered selection) moves where the re-root event arrives (`onFolders`' `focus`), not in one consumer's follow rule. Search run: `rg -n 'origin\.root ===' app/src/renderer -g '!*.test.*'` (2026-11-09: `App.tsx` `onFolders` and `leaveVanished`, and `projectSettingsRules.ts`, which the App move now precedes).

### 2026-11-09 — CLI path messages contradicted ARCHITECTURE §12 and §13

- **Area**: `cli/internal/cli/export.go` (`canvasArgLabel`), `cli/internal/repo/inside_relative.go`; tests `export_test.go` `TestExportCanvasNotFoundNamesNoAbsolutePath`, `config_test.go` `TestConfigPathErrorsExplainMissingAndOutside`, `inside_relative_test.go`.
- **Symptom**: `doan export /abs/repo/.doan/canvases/zz.html -o x.html` answered `Canvas not found: /abs/repo/...`, an absolute path in a message the app shows and agents relay. `doan config --add-serve ../nothere` (and `--token-css`) answered "Config path does not exist" instead of "Config path is outside the repository".
- **Root cause**: export's other messages were built from repo-relative values or stripped by `exportInternal`, but `canvas_not_found` echoed the raw argument, which may be absolute. `repo.InsideRelative` took only absolute paths and ran `filepath.EvalSymlinks` first, so a missing path failed as missing before any lexical comparison; `repo.Resolve` already did lexical first, so the two helpers disagreed with each other and with §13.
- **Net**: caught by an audit comparing messages with ARCHITECTURE. Missed because the export tests checked "no absolute path" only for `export_refused`, with relative or other-repo arguments, and the config tests' missing case was inside the root.
- **Prevention**: before resolving links, compare the cleaned path with the root lexically; keep root-alias tolerance by resolving only the existing ancestor of a lexically outside path. Test a missing `../x` alongside every "missing" path case. For any message that interpolates a CLI argument, test it with an absolute argument. Grep: `grep -rnE 'Message: *"[^"]*" *\+ *(args|arg|path)|usageError\("[^"]*" *\+' cli --include='*.go'` and `grep -rn EvalSymlinks cli --include='*.go'` (check each call has a lexical check before it when the input can be missing).

### 2026-11-09 — Two Settings > About tests failed from the day after their fixed timestamp, breaking the gate everywhere

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/SettingsAbout.test.tsx` (Version-row tests "a failed About read keeps…" and "checking shows the spinner label…"); fixed in fbecb6cd.
- **Symptom**: from 2026-11-09 both tests got "Up to date, last checked Nov 8 at 2:41 PM" instead of "…today at 2:41 PM", so the CI `gate` job failed on develop and on every open PR, whatever they changed.
- **Root cause**: the snapshot's `lastCheckedAt` was a fixed local time (2026-11-08 14:41), but `SettingsAbout` passes the real `Date.now()` to `updateView`, which writes "today" only when both fall on the same local day. The test passed on the day it was written and on no later day; nothing in the test showed that it depended on the run date.
- **Net**: caught by CI the next day, on every branch at once. Missed by the review and gate of the change that added the tests, which ran on the same day. A full app suite run with `Date` shifted 400 days and 3 years ahead (a `setupFiles` module that subclasses `Date`) found no other test that depends on the real date; `UpdateHeaderButton` also passes `Date.now()` to `updateView`, but its tests assert no dated text.
- **Prevention**: a test that asserts text derived from "now" (today, yesterday, "x ago", a countdown) either passes `now` explicitly or pins the clock with `vi.useFakeTimers({ toFake: ["Date"] })` + `vi.setSystemTime(...)`, leaving timers and promises real. When adding such a test, find the code under test reading the clock: `rg -n 'Date\.now\(\)|new Date\(\)' app/src --glob '!*.test.*'`. To check the whole suite, run it once with the clock shifted (a temporary `setupFiles` entry that subclasses `Date` with an offset); any new failure depends on the run date.

### 2026-11-08 — Closing one of two header popovers let the window drag under the other

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/headerNoDrag.ts` (new), `AgentUsageButton.tsx`, `UpdateHeaderButton.tsx`; tests `headerNoDrag.test.ts`, `AgentUsageButton.interaction.test.tsx`, `UpdateHeaderButton.interaction.test.tsx`.
- **Symptom**: with Agent usage and the update popover open together (a stopped restart opens the update popover quietly beside Agent usage), closing one removed the header's inline `-webkit-app-region: no-drag`, so a press on the other popover's area over the header dragged the window.
- **Root cause**: the fix for 2026-11-06 'The Agent usage popover stayed open on a press in the app header' put the inline style in the popover's own effect (set on open, remove on close). The update popover copied the effect, and two owners then wrote one shared element with no count, so the first close won.
- **Net**: found by reading while recording the update popover (BACKLOG), not by a test or a person. Missed by the unit tests, which rendered one popover at a time. The fix is checked with native clicks and drags in the real app (DOM events do not reach the drag region).
- **Prevention**: an element that several components change (an inline style, a class, `inert`, a body attribute) needs one owner that counts holders; components ask that owner and never set or remove the value themselves. Every popover over the header holds no-drag through `useHeaderNoDrag`. Search: `rg -n "app-region" app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'` must show only `headerNoDrag.ts` setting it from script (2026-11-08: only `headerNoDrag.ts`; the rest is `styles.css`).
- **Recurrence (2026-11-15, audit)**: the prototype window's frame name menu, a popover under that window's `.top` drag-region header, held no no-drag, so a press on the header's empty part could not close it; it now calls `useHeaderNoDrag`, pinned by `FrameMenu.interaction.test.tsx`. Native presses on the real header are not verified. Search: `rg -n 'Popover.Root|useHeaderNoDrag\(' app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'` and check each popover opened from a `.top` header holds it.

### 2026-11-08 — Look Up named a composer chip by its screen-reader text

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/styles.css` (`.anchor-chip .sr-only`), `app/src/main/editMenu.ts` (`lookUpLabel`); tests `chipField.test.ts`, `editMenu.test.ts`.
- **Symptom**: the text edit menu read `Look Up “Look atFrame Second frame…”` for a selection with a composer chip; across lines the label also carried an invisible character before a line-start chip.
- **Root cause**: Electron's `params.selectionText` is Chromium's selection text, which includes visually hidden (`sr-only`) text and the composer's U+200B filler before a line-start chip. JS `\s` does not match U+200B, so whitespace folding kept it.
- **Net**: seen in the real app (BACKLOG). The isolated PoC confirmed the label before and after `user-select: none` on the chip's prefix. The macOS dictionary panel's own query was not observed: its one-time introduction blocks it, and continuing changes the real user's setting.
- **Prevention**: text a person can select must not hold `sr-only` text that only screen readers need; mark such spans `user-select: none`, or read the label from the model, not the selection. Normalise selection strings with U+200B (and other format characters) removed before folding whitespace. Search: `rg -n 'sr-only' app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'` and check each hit against the selectable areas in ARCHITECTURE §7 'Text edit menu' 'Selection' (2026-11-08: only the composer chip and `ConversationLine.tsx`'s time label, which is outside the bubble text).

### 2026-11-08 — A release-candidate copy reported Up to date after the stable release it led to was published
