- **Area**: `app/src/main/update/service.ts` (`StableAwareMacUpdater`, `electronUpdater`); tests `service.test.ts` 'which release the feed offers'.
- **Symptom**: the signed 0.1.0-rc.2 in `/Applications` read "Up to date" after v0.1.0 was published with `latest-mac.yml`. rc.1 → rc.2 had worked, so the update path looked verified.
- **Root cause**: electron-updater's GitHub provider (6.8.11; 6.8.12 and 7.0.0-alpha.13 choose tags the same way), with `allowPrerelease`, takes the copy's channel from its version and accepts a stable tag only when that channel is `alpha` or `beta`; any other channel (`rc`) follows only its own tags. `allowPrerelease` reads as "prereleases too", but for a custom channel it means "this channel only". The service's comment claimed the opposite and nothing ran the provider's tag choice.
- **Net**: caught by a person after the first stable release, on the installed copy (the app.asar was read to find the cause). Missed by the unit tests, whose fake updater only recorded the `allowPrerelease` value, and by the one real-path check, which covered rc → rc only. Installed rc copies cannot receive the fix and need a manual reinstall.
- **Prevention**: tests of update selection drive electron-updater's real `GitHubProvider` over a fake GitHub (releases.atom, `/releases/latest`, `latest-mac.yml`) rather than asserting updater settings; each release-channel transition the app promises (rc → rc, rc → stable, stable never → prerelease) has its own case. When electron-updater is upgraded, re-read its GitHub provider's tag choice; the tests import `electron-updater/out/providers/GitHubProvider`, which 7.0 moves to `dist/`, so the upgrade breaks them on purpose. Search: `rg -n 'allowPrerelease|channel' app/src/main/update --glob '!*.test.ts'`.

### 2026-11-08 — The header's update popover opened by the menu closed at once because a hidden header opened it too

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/UpdateHeaderButton.tsx` (the `update:popover` listener and the stopped-restart opening); test `UpdateHeaderButton.interaction.test.tsx` "only the shown header opens a popover nobody pressed for".
- **Symptom**: in the real app, `update:popover` (the Doan menu's blocked Restart to Update) left the header button with `aria-expanded="false"` and no popover; a renderer log showed Radix's `onOpenChange(false)` right after the open, twice.
- **Root cause**: every screen draws its own header and the hidden ones stay mounted under `[inert]`, so a push-driven open ran in two `UpdateHeaderButton`s. Each Radix popover moved focus into its content, which was outside the other's, and each closed the other. A press never hit this: only the shown header gets the click.
- **Net**: caught by `/design-sync`'s real-app measurement before integration. Missed by the unit tests, whose hooks harness renders one instance; the Agent usage popover, the pattern copied, only ever opens from a press.
- **Prevention**: a popover, dialog or focus move that opens from an event rather than a press must act only in the instance outside `[inert]` (`button.closest("[inert]") === null`). Before wiring an IPC event to UI that lives in the header or another per-screen part, search `rg -n 'topBar\(|TopBar ' app/src/renderer/App.tsx` to count the mounted copies.

### 2026-11-08 — A fake CLI mode meant for one root re-rooted another root its watcher re-read

- **Area**: `app/src/main/shell.test.ts` (`autoCli` in 'auto-registered worktrees', 'an uninitialized worktree is not re-rooted upward').
- **Symptom**: on CI (macos-26, run 42387644873) the test expected `[main, w]` but the hand-added main had been replaced by an ancestor (`/private/var/folders/h5`): main took the upward re-root again and again while the worktree stayed as expected. Not reproduced locally until main was re-read on purpose.
- **Root cause**: the fake CLI's `up` mode applied to every root, and the test relied on re-reading only the worktree (`reopen(w)`). A stored root's watcher can re-read it at any time (a late file event from its own creation, load), and a hand-added root in `up` mode correctly re-roots upward, each new parent again in the same mode. The test's isolation assumed no read the test did not start.
- **Net**: caught by the shared gate on CI; local runs passed because main's watcher stayed quiet. A forced `shell.reread([main])` after `reopen(w)` reproduced the CI shape (main climbed to `/`) every time; with the mode scoped to `w` (`git.mode("up", w)`) the same forced read leaves main listed.
- **Prevention**: a fake CLI mode that changes what one root's read publishes applies only to that root; every other stored root must answer as before, because its watcher may read during the test. Search: `rg -n '\.mode\("' app/src/main --glob '*.test.*'` and check each mode against every root the test has stored (2026-11-08: the `error` and `uninit` modes leave a hand-added main in place; only `up` moved it).

### 2026-11-07 — Entering a problem project blanked the renderer because the overlay and the Overview shared a key

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/App.tsx` (the `<main>` children `ProjectOverview` and `ProblemOverlay`); test `App.problemOverlay.test.tsx` "the overlay and the Overview under it never share a key".
- **Symptom**: clicking a not-loaded Missing (or other problem) project's sidebar row from Home left a blank window: React #185 "Maximum update depth exceeded". Reproduced 3 of 3 times in the real app.
- **Root cause**: the new `ProblemOverlay` was keyed by the loaded root, and the `ProjectOverview` beside it in the same `<main>` is keyed by the same root (`overview.path`). Siblings with equal keys make React remount one of them on every render, and the Overview's mount effects schedule another render, so the loop never settles. Keying both by "the root" read as natural because each is "per root", and nothing warns at compile time.
- **Net**: caught by `/design-sync`'s real-app measurement before integration. Missed by the App tests: their hooks harness calls `App()` as a function and inspects the returned element tree without reconciling it, so duplicate sibling keys and remount loops never happen there; the batch's gates (unit tests, typecheck, lint, build) were all green.
- **Prevention**: give a new element that sits beside a root-keyed sibling its own key space (`key={\`problem\n${root}\`}`), and pin it with a test that collects the sibling keys of the rendered parent and expects them distinct. When adding a keyed child to `App.tsx`'s `<main>`, search `rg -n 'key=\{[A-Za-z.?]*([Pp]ath|[Rr]oot)\}' app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'` and check whether any match shares the new element's parent. Any batch that adds a renderer layer gets a short real-app entry probe before review, because the App harness cannot see reconciliation failures.

### 2026-11-07 — Reading saved roots as real paths dropped root-keyed memory and, across a stopped write, a hand-added mark

- **Area**: `app/src/main/folderStore.ts` (`openFolderStore`, `realMarks`), `canvasSeen.ts` (`copyRoots`), `uiState.ts` (`moveUiStateRoots`), `restore/uiState.ts` (`renameUiRoots`), `index.ts` (`migrate`); tests `rootMigration.test.ts`, `folderStore.interrupt.test.ts`.
- **Symptom**: the fix for a project saved through a symlink (every Doan-connection start refused) read saved roots as real paths, and each version lost state another way. (1) `canvas-seen.json` and `ui-state.json` stay keyed by the old spelling, so the first `apply` dropped the alias's seen record and baselined the real path at the current mtime (an unseen change read as seen), and the UI restore lost the workspace, expanded groups and per-root memory. Collapsing `[alias (hand-added), real]` with marks `[real]` turned the hand-added project into an auto one, which `pruneAuto` removes once its folder goes. (2) Moving that memory in memory only was lost when `folders.json` was saved with real paths before `ui-state.json` or `canvas-seen.json`: the next launch saw no alias left. A failed `folders.json` write after the marks were saved as real paths left `folders=[alias]`, `marks=[real]`, and the next launch read the auto project as hand-added, so a vanished worktree was never pruned. (3) With the ordered step in place, a failed `auto-folders.json` write after `folders.json` had collapsed the spellings left `marks=[real]` on disk while the run held `auto=[]`, so the next launch read the hand-added project as auto.
- **Root cause**: a spelling change is a rename across every store keyed by root, but it was done in the one file that lists roots. Each store was then rewritten on its own schedule, so a failure or a stop between files left a combination no single read could recover, and the failed run kept a normalised state that was not on disk.
- **Net**: three independent review rounds each reproduced a loss with temp-file fixtures and injected `rename` failures; the batch's own tests covered one alias with one mark and no failures. The final tests fail each of the five writes in turn and check that the failed run equals what reading the disk gives and that the next launch finishes hand-added.
- **Prevention**: when a stored path's spelling changes, move every store keyed by it first (find them with the search below), then rewrite the list and its marks, in one ordered startup step whose every prefix the next launch reads the same way and retries; on any failure the run takes what the files on disk give, never an unsaved normalised state. Write first the change that removes information (the marks a hand-added spelling clears), so no later stop can restore it. Test a failure at every write, not only the success path. Related: 2026-10-26 'A failed saved-folder read erased review markers' (pruning needs a cleanly read list). Search: `rg -o 'join\(userData, "[^"]+"' app/src/main/index.ts | sort -u` lists the userData stores to check for root keys (2026-11-07: `canvas-seen.json` and `ui-state.json` hold root-keyed memory; thumbnails are a regenerable cache, and conversations, trash and attachments hold none under an alias).
- **Recurrence (2026-11-15, `restore/uiState.ts`)**: the new `conversationByCanvas` map was rekeyed by root with the default whole-value precedence, so when an alias and its real path both held a map, canvases remembered only under the alias were dropped. `11cd1fabe` merges those maps per canvas, with the real path's entry winning only for the same canvas; `cd1ddea9f` changed the earlier whole-map expectation and added the overlapping-canvas case in `uiState.test.ts`. Prevention addition: when adding a nested root-keyed map, test both a key found only under the alias and a key found under both spellings, and choose collision precedence at the stored item's level. Search: `rg -n 'rekey|conversationByCanvas|shellProjects' app/src/restore/uiState.ts`.

### 2026-11-07 — The eval command reader guessed shell state and turned partial reads into evidence

- **Area**: `eval/lib/transcript.ts` (`parseCommand`, `commandWords`, `runGroups`), `eval/scenarios/D1/presets.ts` (`presetLookups`, `presetChecks`), `eval/lib/judge.ts`.
- **Symptom**: D7b failed a run that read `harden` and `clarify` through `for p in harden clarify; do doan design-skill $p …; done` (USABILITY-EVAL 'Guide trim round'). Each fix that taught the reader more shell then let it count lookups that never ran or miss ones that did: a loop variable's value lost after the loop, an unclosed `for` or one past the expansion cap read as if it ran, a body read once with the variable left as written taken for the full list, `$c design-skill` and `doan $c` skipped silently, and `if`/`while` bodies read as plain commands, so a preset inside `if true; then …; fi` or `while false; do …; done` counted as read.
- **Root cause**: the reader turned whatever it could parse into words and had no way to say it had not followed a command to the end, so every unsupported construct became a guess, and checks took the guessed words as what ran.
- **Net**: three independent review rounds each built a counterexample that made a check pass falsely; the unit tests covered only the constructs each round added.
- **Prevention**: a transcript reader reports per command whether it read it completely (`parseCommand` → `{words, incomplete}`), follows only what it supports (plain commands, `NAME=value`, closed `for … in … do … done` within a 256-iteration budget) and drops the rest as incomplete; a check never takes an incomplete read as evidence or as the full list of what ran (`preset_reads_allowed` and `no_preset_body_read` fail beside one). When extending the reader, add the counterexample that would pass falsely, not only the construct that should now pass. Search: `rg -n 'commandWords|parseCommand' eval --glob '!*.test.ts'` (2026-11-07: presets and the judge's `doanDesignSkill` use `parseCommand`; other callers take only complete words through `commandWords`).
- **Recurrence (2026-11-15, `eval/lib/handoff.ts`, `eval/lib/open.ts`, `eval/scenarios/D4b/base.ts`, `D1/presets.ts` `otherDoanCheck`, `O1/log.ts`, `O3/setup-checks.ts`, `H5/checks.ts`; found by the `/audit` contracts review)**: absence checks (`no_handoff`, `no_doan_open`, `no_install_or_registration`, `no_other_doan_binary`, `no_doan_init`, `no_config_write_call`, `no_copilot_check`, `absolute_paths_only`, `no_stop_or_paste`) took `commandWords` as the full list, so `if …; then brew install doan; fi` passed them, and the reader's doan flag table had fallen behind `run.go`, so an unknown flag such as `doan init --storage repo` made the command unreadable and invisible; every check that needs the full list now fails beside `incompleteRead(ctx.calls.commands)`, and `transcript.test.ts` compares the flag table with `run.go`; `rg -n 'commandWords' eval --glob '!*.test.ts'` and confirm each caller either looks for presence or also reads `incompleteRead`/`parseCommand`.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-16, `eval/lib/transcript.ts` `execCalls`/`rolloutCalls`, `H5/checks.ts`, `O1/log.ts`, `lib/prototype.ts`, `lib/open.ts`, `X1/checks.ts`, `D4b/base.ts`, `D1/presets.ts`; found by the `/audit` maintainability review)**: a Codex `exec_command` whose `cmd` was a template, variable or non-string was dropped instead of read as incomplete, and five callers parsed doan's flags with their own rules (two dashes only, flags before the command ignored, a flag-looking value read as a flag), while two regex checks had no incomplete guard; an unreadable exec now records a sentinel command that `parseCommand` reads as incomplete, every flag consumer reads `doanArgs` (with `refused` for attempts the CLI rejects), and the flag table and preset `CATALOG` are compared with the CLI's sources both ways; `rg -n '\^--\?|startsWith\(`--|words\.(includes|indexOf)\(' eval --glob '!*.test.ts'` should list only `transcript.ts`.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-16, `eval/lib/transcript.ts`, `D4b/base.ts`, `H5/checks.ts`, `lib/open.ts`, `R11/checks.ts`, `H2b/checks.ts`, `W1`; found by the round-3 `/audit`)**: a doan run inside `$(…)`/backticks or behind `env`/`timeout`/`nohup`/`xargs` was neither read nor marked incomplete (`body=$(doan design-skill clarify)` read as a complete `design-skill` command), an unknown flag before the command shifted the command word, the reader accepted commands the CLI refuses (a flag the command does not take, a second `--frame`, extra positionals), and checks still judged by raw text or their own copies (D4b's `tee` regex, R11's width regex, H2b's own answer reader, W1's untied finding codes); the first fix then let a trailing `# comment` turn a run into a refused command and read `$((cmd) && (cmd))` as arithmetic, both caught by an adversarial review that ran counterexamples. Substitution bodies, comments, the common wrappers, `eval` and per-command flags and argument shapes are now read (tied to `run.go` and the real CLI by `transcript.test.ts`, command names too), and an unknown flag before the command is incomplete; when a rule makes more commands refused, test it with a trailing comment and with unresolved words, since a refused read drops a run from every absence check. `rg -n 'calls\.commands\.(filter|some|find)\(' eval --glob '!*.test.ts'` should list only `D1/presets.ts` `sourceReadCheck` (any mention of the presets' source counts, failing closed) and D4b's `curl … | sh` pipe, which spans the groups the reader splits.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-16, `eval/lib/transcript.ts` `runGroups`; found by an independent review of the new `{ …; }` and function support)**: seven counterexamples read as complete with wrong commands or home paths (an argument `~/.zshrc` lost its home path in the body, `local`, `set --`, `shift`/`return` inside a loop or group, `$@` inside a substitution, `$10`, `time f`); a function call is now followed only when nothing anywhere in its body, substitutions included, changes scope, arguments or flow, and an argument carries its path below the home directory; `rg -n 'definitionHead|functions\.(get|set|has)' eval/lib/transcript.ts`.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-18, `eval/lib/transcript.ts` `expandToken`; found by the round-1 `/audit`)**: a bare variable holding several words was split as bash does, and a test pinned it, while agents run zsh (Codex's headers show `/bin/zsh -lc`), which passes it as one word, so `ids='harden clarify'; for p in $ids; …` counted two preset reads that zsh never made; such a variable is now unresolved, which makes the read incomplete. When the reader models a shell rule, check it against zsh as well as bash. `rg -n 'split\(/\\s\+/\)' eval/lib/transcript.ts`.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-19, `eval/lib/transcript.ts` `expandToken`, `interpreterMayRunDoan`, `execCalls`; found by the round-2 `/audit`)**: the zsh fix covered only several words, so `p=' harden'` still read as bash drops the space; an interpreter switch after the script (`python3 gen.py -c cfg.json`) and any `python -m` module read as an inline script; Perl/Ruby heredoc backticks and exec_command reached other than as `tools.exec_command(…)` ran unseen. Parts left in a PR body only were found again a round later, so what a fix leaves goes to `docs/BACKLOG.md` (control flow around exec_command literals is there now); the replay of 957 recorded runs was unchanged. When a fix narrows a shell rule, write its counterexample probe for the whole class (whitespace, not only several words), and test an option with a separate value before an inline switch. `rg -n 'split\(/\\s\+/\)|INLINE|VALUED' eval/lib/transcript.ts`.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-23, `eval/lib/transcript.ts` `readDoanArgs`, `recordedExecs`; listed in BACKLOG 'Known defects')**: the reader still read a doan call with a flag or `--` before the command as that command after the CLI began refusing it, and took a Codex exec literal as run inside dead code; the flag-first call is now refused, and a literal counts as run only when its script's window has a matching `CommandExecution` record, one record per literal. Replaying recorded runs showed Codex also leaves some commands that did run unrecorded (3 of 310 literals, each `doan active --json` with its result in the script output), so an unmatched literal is a candidate that also makes the read incomplete, never ruled out; when a reader starts trusting a new evidence source, replay recorded runs against it before treating its absence as proof. `rg -n 'recordedExecs|UNRECORDED_EXEC|startsWith\("-"\)' eval/lib/transcript.ts`.

### 2026-11-07 — Radix added an open tooltip's id to an unavailable control's own description

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/components/ui/tooltip.tsx` (`TooltipTrigger`), its five `aria-disabled` callers (`CanvasTools.tsx` Capture area and Export, `ConversationDeleteDialog.tsx`, `CanvasConversationList.tsx`, `SettingsMcpConnectors.tsx` `RowButton`).
- **Symptom**: while the tooltip of an unavailable control with its own sr-only reason (`aria-describedby`) was open, the control's description held both ids, so a screen reader read the reason twice.
- **Root cause**: Radix's trigger sets `aria-describedby` to the open content's id, and Radix's Slot concatenates it with the child's own value. The controls name their reason themselves so it is read while the tooltip is closed too, and the tooltip holds the same text.
- **Net**: recorded in BACKLOG; static markup with forced-open tooltips reproduced it (`tooltip.describedby.test.tsx`, red `canvas-capture-reason radix-…`). The first fix treated any truthy `aria-disabled` as unavailable, so a literal `"false"` would have lost Radix's id; review caught it.
- **Prevention**: a control that carries its own description and also sits under a Radix tooltip keeps it through `TooltipTrigger`'s rule (only `aria-disabled` `true` or `"true"` with a string `aria-describedby`); check an open tooltip's attributes, not only the closed markup. Search: `rg -n '"aria-describedby"' app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'` (2026-11-13: six callers, the five above and `CanvasTools.tsx` Play prototype, all covered by the test).

### 2026-11-07 — A toolbar tooltip took the Esc the canvas needed, and the first fix ran before the `/` list

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/CanvasTools.tsx` (`onEscapeKeyDown`), `app/src/renderer/CanvasController.ts` (`keydown`, `toolbarEscape`), `App.tsx` (`CanvasPane`).
- **Symptom**: with a toolbar tooltip open (right after pressing Capture area or the Picker), one Esc only closed the tooltip; a second ended capture mode or the pick. The first fix took the canvas step inside the tooltip's `onEscapeKeyDown`, which runs in Radix's document capture listener, so with the composer's `/` list open one Esc closed the list and also ended capture mode, where without a tooltip it closes only the list.
- **Root cause**: Radix's DismissableLayer prevents an Esc its top layer handles, and `CanvasController.keydown` (window, bubble) skips prevented events by design, so any Radix layer over the canvas swallows the step. Running the step in the capture-phase callback put it ahead of every bubble handler that might own the Esc.
- **Net**: seen in the canvas-capture runtime probe; the `/` list order was found by independent review from the event order, and its red came from driving the tooltip callback, Radix's prevention and the window dispatch with and without a tooltip (`CanvasController.test.ts`). An earlier round's red was a missing-method `TypeError`, not a behavior failure.
- **Prevention**: a Radix layer over a control whose Esc matters hands the decision on rather than taking the step: `toolbarEscape` marks the event, Radix's own `preventDefault` is the first call, and any later prevention means a later handler owned it; keydown keeps its composition and field rules. The order is Radix's internals, so `CanvasController.test.ts` reads the installed `@radix-ui/react-tooltip` and `@radix-ui/react-dismissable-layer` to pin it. Compare every Esc case with and without the layer, including the `/` list and IME composition. An Esc forwarded from the canvas iframe still does not close the tooltip (BACKLOG). Search: `rg -n 'onEscapeKeyDown' app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'` (2026-11-07: the toolbar tooltips, `ConversationDeleteDialog.tsx`, `AgentUsageButton.tsx` and `SettingsMcpConnectors.tsx`).
- **Follow-up 2026-11-09**: the forwarded Esc is fixed in `CanvasController.closeToolbarTooltip`: on an Esc message from the canvas, an open toolbar tooltip (a trigger in `.doan-canvas-tools` with `data-state` `delayed-open` or `instant-open`) gets a non-bubbling Escape on the app's document, where Radix listens, so the window keydown never takes a second step. A real-app probe reproduced the open tooltip before the fix and its close after it (`.scratch/runtime-test/focus-and-origin/probe.mjs`). Any other Radix layer open while focus is in the canvas still misses the canvas's Esc, for example the update popover opened quietly.
- **Follow-up 2026-11-12**: `closeToolbarTooltip` is replaced by `CanvasController.forwardEscape`, which dispatches a bubbling, cancelable Escape keydown on the app's document for every forwarded Esc. Any open Radix layer takes it at the capture phase as it would the key itself, and the window keydown takes the canvas's step only when no layer other than the toolbar's tooltip prevented it. A real-app probe (`.scratch/runtime-test/backlog-small-fixes/probe.ts`: Hand on, focus in the canvas, the update popover opened quietly by a pushed `restarting` → `restartAborted`) timed out waiting for the popover to close before the fix and passed after it, with Hand kept on that Esc and Select on the next. The unit harness's fake document now bubbles a dispatched event to the window, so a test of a forwarded Esc runs the same keydown path as an app Esc. Prevention: replay a key the canvas forwards on the app's document rather than calling a handler directly, so every app listener sees it on the same terms. Search: `rg -n 'dispatchEvent\(new KeyboardEvent' app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'` (2026-11-12: only `forwardEscape`).
- **Recurrence (2026-11-17, UX audit tooltips)**: name and detail tooltips added over rows of the frame drawers, the preset popover, the conversation title menus and Home's project menu took the first Esc, so the menu or drawer stayed open (seen red in a real-app probe of the frame drawer); a host now provides `TooltipEscape` with its close and the shared `TooltipContent` hands an Esc on to it when it has no handler of its own (`components/ui/tooltip.escape.test.tsx`); a host whose focused control handles Esc itself (Quick Open's input) needs none. Search: `rg -n "<(DropdownMenu|Popover)\.Portal|TooltipEscape value" app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'` and check that every menu or popover holding a tooltip trigger has a `TooltipEscape` or an own `onEscapeKeyDown`.

### 2026-11-07 — `doan export`'s cleanup did not run when the process was signalled

- **Area**: `cli/internal/cli/export.go` (`writeExport`, `writeExportWith`), `export_signal_test.go`.
- **Symptom**: an export interrupted (Ctrl-C, SIGTERM) while writing left its temporary file `.<name>.doan-<hex>` in the output folder.
- **Root cause**: the temporary file was removed only on the error path after `Write` or `Rename` returned; a termination signal ends the process before any deferred or error cleanup runs.
- **Net**: recorded in BACKLOG from the canvas-row-menu work; subprocess tests that hold the write after a partial write and send a real signal reproduced it (`TestExportSignalsRemoveOnlyOwnedTemporaryFile`, red: the owned file survived). SIGKILL, which the app's export timeout uses, still can leave the file.
- **Prevention**: subscribe to the signals before creating the owned file, remove only the path this process owns, and make publication (rename) and releasing ownership atomic to the signal cleanup, since after a rename another export may own the same name. Never sweep files by name pattern: another writer's temporary file looks the same (`TestExportTemporaryCollisionPreservesOtherWriter`; the same ownership rule as 2026-10-25 'Startup cache collection deleted a live thumbnail temporary file'). Search: `rg -n 'signal\.Notify|os\.Remove\(temp' cli --glob '!*_test.go'` (2026-11-07: export.go only).
- **Recurrence (2026-11-15, audit)**: the `.doan/config.json` writes of `init` and `config` and the exclude rewrite of the storage pair (`.config-*`, `.exclude-*`) kept a `defer os.Remove` and no signal handling, so a SIGINT/SIGTERM left the temporary file and a successful rename was followed by removing a name another writer may own. They now go through `repo.WriteStaged` (`cli/internal/repo/staged.go`), and `TestCreateTempOnlyInWriteStaged` fails any other `os.CreateTemp` in `cli/`. Prevention addition: a new staged write in the CLI uses `WriteStaged` or names its own temporary file as export does, never `os.CreateTemp` with a deferred remove. Search: `rg -n 'signal\.Notify|os\.Remove\(temp|os\.CreateTemp\(' cli --glob '!*_test.go'` (2026-11-15: `staged.go` and `export.go` only).
- **Recurrence (2026-11-16, round-3 audit)**: `writeAttributes` still rewrote the team's `.gitattributes` in place with `os.WriteFile`, so a write failing after the truncate (full disk, a kill) left it empty or cut; it now goes through `repo.WriteStaged` with an identity and content recheck, keeping the mode and a linked file. Prevention addition: a file Doan edits but does not own (the team's files, the exclude) is never truncated in place; search `rg -n 'os\.WriteFile\(' cli --glob '!*_test.go'` (2026-11-16: only the export test seed in `exporttest/seed.go`).

### 2026-11-07 — A reply one byte over the cap passed because its length was checked only on a read failure

- **Area**: `cli/internal/cli/active.go` (`readLine`, shared by `active`, `onboarding` and `work`), `active_line_test.go`.
- **Symptom**: `doan active` and `doan onboarding …` accepted a reply whose newline arrived as byte 65,537, one byte over the 64 KiB reply cap (ARCHITECTURE §4 'Connection contract'); `doan work` was safe only because `sendWork` checked the length again.
- **Root cause**: `readLine` reads through `io.LimitReader(conn, maxResponseLine+1)`, whose extra byte exists to detect an over-long line, but returned as soon as `ReadBytes('\n')` succeeded and checked the length only on the error path. When the extra byte is the newline the read succeeds, so the over-cap line took the success branch. The existing at-cap test padded its line to 65,537 bytes once the newline was added and still passed.
- **Net**: recorded in BACKLOG with the terminal agent activity follow-ups; caller-level tests at 65,536 and 65,537 bytes, newline counted, failed for both `active` and `onboarding` before the fix (`TestActiveAndOnboardingResponseLineCap`).
- **Prevention**: a cap read through a `+1` limit checks the length before trusting a successful read, and its tests send exactly the limit and the limit plus one, counting the delimiter. Search: `rg -n 'LimitReader\(' cli --glob '!*_test.go'` (2026-11-07: the `io.ReadAll` readers in `repo/git.go`, `repo/storage.go`, `cli/active.go` and `cli/handoff.go` check the length after a successful read; `readLine` now does too).

### 2026-11-07 — Setup reported success while the project row still read Not set up

- **Area**: `app/src/main/shell.ts` (`initialize`, `refresh`), `app/src/main/shell.test.ts`.
- **Symptom**: after a successful `doan init`, the setup answered success but the row stayed `uninitialized`; `shell.test.ts` 'initializes once for simultaneous requests…' and 'init result.repo cannot replace the stored identity' failed under load (earlier seen as a one-off flake of 'initializes a folder stored a moment ago…').
- **Root cause**: init's own writes wake the folder watcher, whose read can start after the post-init read and finish before it. `refresh` drops a read that a newer one overtook (it returns `false`), and `initialize` ignored that result, so it answered with nothing published from its own read. A stale-read guard that is right for watcher reads turned a caller's "read after my write" into "maybe read".
- **Net**: caught at integration, when batch G's real CLI changed the timing enough to fail two shell tests consistently; batch A's own runs passed and an earlier flake was dismissed as unrelated. A wrapper test that delays the post-init read behind the watcher's read reproduces it deterministically ('setup answers only once the row is listed…').
- **Prevention**: a caller that must publish its own post-write state checks `refresh`'s result and re-reads (bounded; `shell.initialize` tries three times and then leaves the row to its watcher). Treat a flake in a test that writes and then reads as a race to reproduce, not noise. Search: `rg -n "await refresh\(" app/src/main --glob '!*.test.*'` (2026-11-07: the watcher callback, which may be overtaken, and `initialize`, now checked).
- **Recurrence (2026-11-08, `shell.ts` `initialize`, the read before init)**: the fix above checked only the read after init. The read before init returned `false` when a newer read (the watcher, a re-read on window focus) started meanwhile, and setup answered `unavailable` ("This project is no longer available for setup.") for a folder still stored and uninitialized; 'initializes a folder stored a moment ago, while its first read is still loading' failed under load (docs/BACKLOG.md 'Known defects'). Both reads now try up to three times. Regression: 'setup is not refused when a newer read overtakes its read before init' starts setup and `rereadAll()` together and failed before the fix. The search above now lists the watcher callback and both `initialize` reads; check every `await refresh(` a caller acts on, not only the one that failed.

### 2026-11-07 — The eval fake's auth status comparison broke when the app's DTO gained a field

- **Area**: `eval/lib/socket-app.test.ts`, `app/src/main/agentAuth/state.ts` (`authStatus`).
- **Symptom**: `node .agents/skills/verify/scripts/verify.mjs --eval` failed at eval-tests (303/304) on main: the fake app's provider status no longer deep-equalled `authStatus` because main `00302c30` added `generation` to it.
- **Root cause**: the test derives the expected fake status by removing a hand-written list of app-only keys (`account`, `attempt`, `revision`) from the real DTO, so any new app-only field fails the comparison. The change that added the field did not touch `eval/` and did not run `--eval`, which `/verify` only adds for eval runner changes.
- **Net**: found by the next `--eval` gate run, in the PR that added Doan files storage; the app's own tests and the gate without `--eval` passed.
- **Prevention**: a change to an agent-auth or onboarding DTO runs `verify.mjs --eval`, since the eval fake mirrors those shapes. Search: `rg -n "const \{ [a-zA-Z]+: _[a-zA-Z]*" eval --glob '*.test.ts'` (2026-11-07: only `socket-app.test.ts`, now also stripping `generation`).

### 2026-11-07 — The streaming reply's caret sat on its own line under the Markdown

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/ConversationThread.tsx` (`AgentMessage`, `MarkdownText`), `styles.css` (`.conversation-thread .caret`, `.msg-agent`)
- **Symptom**: while a reply streamed, the blinking caret drew on a line of its own below the last paragraph, list item or code block in the Overview thread and the canvas conversation panel; the drafts draw it right after the last character.
- **Root cause**: the caret was rendered as a sibling of the Markdown output inside `.msg-agent`, which `89aa20d6` made a flex column. A flex item is blockified, so `display: inline-block` had no line to sit on. Static markup tests only checked that a caret existed, not where it was, and nothing failed. Once the caret moved into the text, its fixed 24 px height with a −6 px margin made the panel's 14/20 lines 3 px taller, so the reply would also have shrunk when streaming ended.
- **Net**: the user caught it in the running app; `/design-sync` for turn-activity recorded it as context rather than a defect. Unit tests missed it because they asserted presence only; now `ConversationThread.test.tsx` asserts the caret is inside the last text block, and the line-height effect was caught only by the runtime probe (`.scratch/runtime-test/reply-caret/probe.mjs`).
- **Prevention**: an element meant to flow with text must live inside the text's block, never as a child of a flex or grid container; give a text-line decoration `height: 1lh` with `vertical-align: top` instead of a fixed height so it cannot grow the line. Test the placement (the host element), not just presence. Grep `grep -n "inline-block" app/src/renderer/styles.css app/src/renderer/ds/*.css` and check each parent's `display`.

### 2026-11-07 — Antigravity conversations ran without the doan skill stub

- **Area**: `app/src/main/agentAuth/providers.ts` (`HOME_STUB`, the Antigravity `settings`).
- **Symptom**: a Doan-connected Antigravity canvas conversation (ACP server 1.3.0) answered that it had no `doan` skill; its ACP frames held no `skills/doan` reference. Doan had written the stub to `<GEMINI_HOME>/skills/doan/SKILL.md` for every Antigravity process. Copying the same file to `<GEMINI_HOME>/config/skills/doan/` made the agent list `doan`.
- **Root cause**: the stub path was confirmed once by a live check (2026-10-28) and recorded only as a code comment. The ACP server's skill roots (`resolve_skills_paths`: `<GEMINI_HOME>/config/skills`, `<cwd>/.gemini/skills`, `<GEMINI_HOME>/antigravity-cli/skills`, `<cwd>/.agents/skills`) do not include `<GEMINI_HOME>/skills`, and a missing skill does not fail: the agent works without Doan's guide. The unit test checked only that the path ended in `skills/doan/SKILL.md`.
- **Net**: caught by project-skill-drawer's research reading the server's skill roots and its PoC P5 asking a real turn which skills it had. Missed by `providers.test.ts` (suffix match) and by every turn since, because nothing reports a skill the provider did not load.
- **Prevention**: pin each provider's exact stub path in a test (`providers.test.ts` "puts the Antigravity stub where its ACP server reads skills") and, when a provider or its server version changes, ask one real turn whether `doan` is among its skills. `rg -n 'HOME_STUB\(|SKILL_STUB' app/src/main/agentAuth/providers.ts` (run 2026-11-07): Cursor `~/.cursor/skills` and Copilot `~/.copilot/skills` under the dedicated HOME are the other derived paths; neither was re-checked by a live turn in this feature.

### 2026-11-06 — Unfolding older models moved the line under the pointer, so a second press picked a model

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/ComposerParts.tsx` (`useOlderFold`, `AgentPicker`), `SettingsAgents.tsx` Model menu, `homeComposer.ts` (`heldScrollTop`, `olderOrder`, `lockedOlderSide`)
- **Symptom**: before merge, in the real app: unfolding "Older models" in the composer's agent and model menu (opens upward, `side="top"`) pushed the line up by the unfolded height (128px with Codex alone, 60px with Codex and an empty Copilot), and pressing the same spot again picked GPT-5.6-Luna and closed the menu. In Settings (`side="bottom"`) at 1440x1000 with five providers, the unfolded menu was flipped to the top by Radix's collision handling and the line moved 395px.
- **Root cause**: the design held the line with a pure `scrollTop` correction. A menu anchored at its bottom edge grows upward, and at `scrollTop` 0 the needed correction is negative, so the clamp silently does nothing; a collision flip moves the whole menu, which no scroll can undo. The unit tests fed the formula numbers, never the geometry that produces them.
- **Net**: caught by the `/runtime-test` probe that measured the line's viewport top before and after each toggle and clicked the original point again; the independent review had already flagged the clamp as unproven. Missed by the pure-function tests of `heldScrollTop` and by static render tests. A second review then caught that locking the side at first paint ran before Radix's asynchronous placement settled and kept menus on a cramped side when a model list arrived late.
- **Prevention**: for a disclosure that grows a floating menu in place, draw the new rows on the side away from the anchor (above the line when painted on top), lock the side from the first toggle until close (`avoidCollisions` off), and read `data-side` only after Radix has positioned (next frame), not at first paint. Probe it in the real app: line top before/after and the outcome of a second click at the original point, at `scrollTop` 0, at the 384px cap and near a window edge that makes Radix flip. Search for other in-place growth inside floating content: `rg -n 'avoidCollisions|dataset\.side|scrollTop *[-+]?=' app/src/renderer` (2026-11-06: only `useOlderFold`; `CanvasHeader.tsx` and `QuickOpenDialog.tsx` scroll lists that do not grow on toggle; `ConversationThread.tsx` is not a menu).

### 2026-11-06 — The next turn's indicator came a render after the person's message

- **Area**: `app/src/main/conversation/service.ts` (existing-conversation send), `store.ts` (`appendAll`), `app/src/renderer/turnStatus.ts` (`turnOf`); turn-activity FX commits `32a52fb3` and `dac80493`.
- **Symptom**: on an existing conversation's next turn, the person's bubble committed 10–14 ms before the turn indicator, once for a whole animation frame (spec: the indicator is there from the render that draws the message). A new conversation's first turn was fine. The first fix (open the turn at its `user.message`) then showed "working" forever after a send whose `turn.started` could not be saved.
- **Root cause**: main appended and pushed `user.message` and `turn.started` as two separate writes and two IPC events, so the renderer rendered between them, and `turnOf` started a turn only at `turn.started`. A first turn arrives whole in the read snapshot, which hid the gap in tests. The two separate writes also meant a failure after the first left a lone message that no terminal would ever close.
- **Net**: caught by the R1 real-app probe measuring DOM commits per mutation; the partial-write case by an independent cross-family review. Missed by unit tests, which fed both events in one batch.
- **Prevention**: UI that must appear "with" an accepted message reads the turn from the message (`turnOf`), not from a later event; events that open a turn are written together (`ConversationStore.appendAll`). A host test pushes `user.message` alone and expects the indicator in that same render (`ProjectOverview.context.test.tsx`). Grep for other multi-event acceptances written one by one: `rg -n 'this\.append\(' app/src/main/conversation/service.ts` (2026-11-06: none open a turn).
### 2026-11-06 — A sibling ACP tool's completion hid a command still running

- **Area**: `app/src/main/conversation/acp.ts` (`normalizeAcp`, the `running` list); FX commits `82386c22`, `cdef83c9`.
- **Symptom**: Copilot started search, glob and `sleep 6` together; the first two completed after 72 ms and the turn indicator said "working" for the 6 s the command ran. After the fix, an answered Antigravity question stayed in the running list (the runner folds it into `activities`, whose early return skipped the removal), so its title came back as a stage.
- **Root cause**: the design's stage table mapped "completed → general" per update, which assumes one tool at a time; recorded frames had only serial tools. The second variant came from two pieces of per-call state (`activities`, `running`) updated on different return paths.
- **Net**: caught by the R1 real-app probe (Copilot chose parallel tools on its own) and by the independent review that built the question sequence. Missed by fixtures, all serial.
- **Prevention**: a per-call status mapping keeps the set of calls still running and derives the stage from it; every return path that ends a call removes it from every per-call set. Test parallel starts with out-of-order completion and a call the runner already folded (`stage.test.ts`, `acpRunner.test.ts` "ends an answered Antigravity question's tool").
### 2026-11-06 — The Codex model list stayed the previous account's after an account replacement

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/dockedSupport.ts` (`modelsKey`, `generationOf`), `HomeComposerView.tsx`, `ProjectOverview.tsx`, `CanvasConversationPanel.tsx`, `SettingsAgents.tsx` (`NewConversations`), `app/src/main/agentAuth/state.ts` (`authStatus`).
- **Symptom**: after replacing the Codex account in Settings > Agents, the model picker of every conversation (and the Settings Model row) still listed the previous account's models.
- **Root cause**: main lists a new connection again (`ModelCatalog` keys by `connectionId`), but each window-side holder kept the list by folder and provider only and never asked main again once it held one. The Status DTO carried nothing that changes with the connection: a replacement keeps the provider, and the email it shows may stay the same, so no holder could tell the account had changed. Home's memory and `DockedSupport` also outlive their screens, so they miss the sign-in while Settings is shown.
- **Net**: caught by the user in the real app. Missed by unit tests, which pinned main's cache across a reconnect but never drove a window holder through a second connection.
- **Prevention**: the Status DTO carries the auth `generation`, and a window-side value read for a connection is keyed by it (`modelsKey(root, provider, generation)`), with the effect that reads it depending on it. A new window-side cache of anything account-bound must key by the generation too. Grep: `grep -rn 'listConversationModels' app/src/renderer` (every caller must key by `modelsKey` with a generation).
### 2026-11-06 — The project row chevron stayed after a click until something else took focus

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/styles.css` (`.shell-project-row` chevron and thumbnail rules); test a651898c, fixed in d4c88635.
- **Symptom**: with a canvas open, clicking a project row (the loaded one or another) and moving the pointer away left the chevron showing in place of the thumbnail. PRODUCT §5 says the chevron shows on hover or keyboard focus.
- **Root cause**: the row is a focusable treeitem (`tabIndex`), so a mouse click focuses it, and the reveal rule used `:focus-within`, which matches any focus. The draft and DESIGN wording ("focus is in it") read like `:focus-within`, while the intent was keyboard focus, which only `:focus-visible` separates from click focus.
- **Net**: caught by the user in the real app. Missed by the `/design-sync` measurement, which read "row keyboard focus" and "rest" but never the state after a click with the pointer away, and by no unit test pinning the selector.
- **Prevention**: a reveal-on-focus rule on a clickable focusable element uses `:focus-visible` / `:has(:focus-visible)`, unless PRODUCT says any focus. Run `rg -n 'focus-within' app/src/renderer/styles.css app/src/renderer/ds`; for each hit on a clickable row, probe click → pointer away and read the computed visibility. `ShellSidebar.test.tsx` pins the project row selector.
- **Same defect**: `.shell-canvas-row`'s menu button rules (canvas and design-system rows) used `:focus-within` too and kept the button after a click; fixed the same way in this run (d04767f5), still shown while its menu is open.
### 2026-11-06 — A menu's "nothing chosen" value shared its string with a real model id

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/ComposerParts.tsx` (`AgentPicker`, the pre-list Default row), its callers in `HomeComposerView.tsx`, `ProjectOverview.tsx`, `CanvasConversationPanel.tsx`, `DockedComposer.tsx`; `SettingsAgents.tsx` and `newConversationSettings.ts` (Settings Model menu); fixed in b55837f0 and a6b8e04b (picker-default-labels).
- **Symptom**: two variants, both caught before main. (1) The model menu's pre-list Default row passed `"default"`, and every composer treated a picked `"default"` as "no model picked". Once picker-default-labels removed Claude Code's paired `opus` row, Claude's own `default` row (id `"default"`) was the only way to Opus 5.5, so a conversation on Sonnet could never pick it. (2) The Settings Model menu's top unpin row and an ACP model with id `"default"` both had RadioItem value `"default"`: picking that model unpinned instead of pinning it, and a stored `"default"` checked both rows.
- **Root cause**: the "no choice" state was encoded as a string from the same space as the values it sits beside. Provider model ids are opaque strings Doan does not control (Claude Code's default row is literally `default`; an ACP agent may use any id), so any fixed string can collide. It stayed harmless only while no listed model happened to carry that id.
- **Net**: (1) caught by the batch worker's own `/code-review` after the parser change made the collision reachable; (2) caught by an independent cross-family review (Codex) that built a fixture with a `default` id. Missed by the unit tests, whose fixtures never listed a model whose id equals the sentinel.
- **Prevention**: represent "nothing chosen" as `null` or a kind-tagged value (`unset` / `model:<id>`), never as a bare string in a provider id space; when a menu mixes an unset row with provider rows, test a provider row whose id equals the unset row's former value. Search: `rg -n '"default"|DEFAULT' app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'` and check each hit compares a closed set (permission mode ids) rather than provider model or effort ids. Remaining: the Settings Effort menu still uses `DEFAULT` beside effort level ids (`SettingsAgents.tsx:261`), safe only while no provider reports a level named `default`.
- **Follow-up (d534ffd6)**: the remaining Settings Effort case is fixed. The Effort menu keeps its rows apart by kind (`unset` / `effort:<id>`, `settingsEffortMenu` and `settingsEffortPick` in `newConversationSettings.ts`), and `newConversationSettings.test.ts` covers a level named `default`.
- **Same pattern** (2026-11-06, turn-activity, caught by an independent review): the turn indicator told a Codex listing from a file read by `summary === "Reading files"`, so a file literally named `Reading files` lost its path. Fixed in `f9751e52`: a listing stage carries no `path`, and the renderer decides the form by `path`. `rg -n 'summary === ' app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'` finds none now.

### 2026-11-06 — A pick of anything else fixed the composer's resolved model and effort in Settings

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/rememberChoice.ts` (`ComposerChoice`, `preferenceAfterPick`), its callers in `HomeComposerView.tsx`, `ProjectOverview.tsx`, `CanvasConversationPanel.tsx`; fixed in 920c88d5, d2f1dae9 and 5bae249a (picker-default-labels B2).
- **Symptom**: with Settings > New conversations set to Codex and Model Default, a pick of a permission, an effort or Fast on Home or in a docked new conversation turned Settings into a pinned "GPT-6-Luna", so new conversations stopped following Codex's default. With a fixed model and effort that a project does not list, a permission pick dropped the fixed effort, and an effort picked for the resolved model was stored under the fixed one. Edit on Home's restored model was treated as picked too.
- **Root cause**: `onRemember` sent the composer's resolved choice (the provider default, a remembered or a fixed model and its effort), and `preferenceAfterPick` could not tell a value the person picked from one the composer only resolved. The 2026-11-02 entry's prevention ("record the composer's resolved provider, model and effort") is right for Last used, which records what was shown, but wrong for the fixed default, which must change only by what the person picked. Nothing visible showed the difference until Settings started printing `Default · <model>` for an unpinned default.
- **Net**: caught by the `/design-sync` probe and its Claude reviewer while measuring Settings in the real app. Missed by `rememberChoice.test.ts`, whose cases passed a choice and never said whether it was picked or resolved.
- **Prevention**: a choice passed to a memory writer carries whether each field was picked (`modelPicked`, `effortPicked`); a fixed default takes only picked fields, and an effort only for the model it was picked for. When adding a remembered field or a new composer, test "pick something else" against every Settings shape (nothing fixed, agent only, agent+model, agent+model+effort). Search: `rg -n "onRemember|preferenceAfterPick\(" app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'`.

### 2026-11-06 — Home remembered its choice on every mount: a touched check compared null with undefined

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/HomeComposerView.tsx` (`choiceTouched`), present since cff2969a; fixed in da0aeea9 (picker-default-labels B2).
- **Symptom**: opening Home, without any pick, wrote Last used and moved an unpinned Settings default onto the resolved model, so Settings showed "GPT-6-Luna" instead of "Default · GPT-6-Luna" once Home had been shown.
- **Root cause**: the composer memory's provider starts as `null` (`emptyComposer`), but the touched check tested `m.provider !== undefined`, which is always true. TypeScript accepts the comparison because the field is typed `Provider | null` and `!== undefined` is legal on any type, so the always-true condition failed silently; the remember effect then ran on mount.
- **Net**: caught by the `/design-sync` probe reading Settings after opening Home in the real app. Missed by unit tests, which mounted Home with a pick already made or never asserted that an untouched mount writes nothing (now `HomeComposerView.interaction.test.tsx` 'an untouched composer remembers nothing; a pick does').
- **Prevention**: compare against the field's actual empty value (`null` here) and pin "an untouched mount writes nothing" for every effect that persists a choice. Search: `rg -n "Touched\b|touched =" app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'` — the Overview and canvas-panel checks use `Object.keys(pick).length > 0` and are not affected.

### 2026-11-06 — Following judged a reload's unmeasured layout and never followed the new frame

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/workFollow.ts` (`followDecision`), `agentIndicator.ts` (`workFrameRect`), `CanvasController.ts` (`followWork`, `drawWork`); fixed in fe7f3e51 (work-update, follow fix round after the real-app check).
- **Symptom**: an agent sent `doan work update --frame <id>` for a frame its next write would create, the person rested, and the write reloaded the canvas; the view did not follow the new frame, or centred the board's origin instead. It happened only sometimes, in the real app.
- **Root cause**: the first layout after a canvas reload can report every frame rect as 0 × 0 at (0, 0) before the document is measured. Following and the working-frame marker read that layout like any other: a 0 × 0 rect at the origin looked already visible, so the frame was counted as followed for the turn (or the move centred (0, 0)), and when the real layout came moments later the frame was no longer a candidate.
- **Net**: caught by the isolated real-app probe of the late-write step (`/runtime-test`). Missed by the unit tests, whose layouts were always measured, and by the controller tests, which never fed a zero-rect layout followed by a real one; jsdom has no layout to produce the race.
- **Prevention**: treat a zero-size frame rect as unmeasured, not as a place: no marker, no follow decision, and nothing recorded as done for it, so the next measured layout decides. When automatic view or overlay logic judges `layout.frames`, test a zero-rect layout followed by the real one. Regression: `CanvasController.test.ts` "a reload whose first layout has zero-size frames waits for the real layout, then moves once to the frame", `workFollow.test.ts` "a zero-size rect is not a measurement: stay, uncounted, no retry". Search: `rg -n "layout\??\.frames" app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'` and check that each reader that moves the view or draws on its own copes with a 0 × 0 rect (person-started jumps go through `fitFrame`, which keeps the scale for one).
- **Recurrence (2026-11-20, explicit frame opens)**: the first zero-viewport layout consumed the requested frame and centred the origin, even when the CLI correctly found the id. Preserve the latest target through remeasurement and same-canvas reload, and test a second request during that interval. Search with `rg -n 'pendingFrame|fitFrame' app/src/renderer`.

### 2026-11-06 — The Agent usage popover stayed open on a press in the app header

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/AgentUsageButton.tsx`, the header `.top` window drag region (`styles.css`); fixed in the prompt-suggestion run (batch U, user-reported).
- **Symptom**: with the Agent usage popover open, a press on an empty part of the app header did not close it; presses anywhere else did.
- **Root cause**: `.top` is `-webkit-app-region: drag`, so macOS handles presses there as window drags and they never reach the page; Radix's outside-press dismissal never fires. The first fix lifted the drag region with a `.top:has(.usage[aria-expanded="true"])` rule: the computed style became `no-drag`, but Electron 45 kept the old draggable region, so native presses still dragged. Setting the same value inline on the header while the popover is open works.
- **Net**: caught by the person in the real app. The first fix passed unit tests (jsdom has no drag regions) and a CDP click in the real app (CDP input goes straight to Blink, past the OS hit test); only a native click (`orca computer click`) showed it did not work.
- **Prevention**: change `-webkit-app-region` from script on the element itself (inline style), not through `:has()` or other selector invalidation, and verify any drag-region change with a native click and a native drag, never with DOM or CDP events. Other popovers and menus over the header share the pitfall and are not fixed. Search: `rg -n "app-region" app/src/renderer`.

### 2026-11-05 — Canvas labels showed the page `<title>` although PRODUCT said file stems

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/canvasTree.ts` (`canvasLabel`) and every caller (header, sidebar tree, Quick Open, conversation chips, panel title); fixed in 99ce56a3 (agent-questions batch X).
- **Symptom**: the canvas header, Quick Open and reply chips showed the canvas's `<title>` ("Team scorecard") while the export name, `doan export` and PRODUCT §5 'Canvas labels are file stems' used the file stem (`team-scorecard`).
- **Root cause**: `canvasLabel` preferred `<title>` from its first commit (ad10c821), and the row type carried `title`, so every new surface that needed a name took the label function as the single source and inherited the title. PRODUCT stated the rule, but nothing in code or tests tied the label to it: tests were written with title fixtures and asserted the title, so they pinned the drift instead of the decision.
- **Net**: caught by a person looking at the real app, then confirmed by reading PRODUCT against `canvasLabel`. Missed by the unit tests (fixtures built titled rows and expected titles) and by earlier reviews, which compared diffs to tests, not to the decision text.
- **Prevention**: a field that a decision says must not be shown should not exist on the row type (`CanvasRow.title` is removed, so a reader no longer compiles); when a PRODUCT rule names what a label is built from, write the first test from that sentence, not from a fixture's shape. Search: `rg -n 'canvasLabel\(' app/src --glob '!*.test.*'` and `rg -n '\.title\b' app/src/renderer app/src/main --glob '!*.test.*'`, then check that each hit is a frame, conversation or dialog title. Run on 2026-11-05: every canvas-name caller goes through `canvasLabel`; no remaining reader of a canvas `<title>`.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-15, audit)**: five renderer names (trash and export toasts, canvas notices, the sidebar's keyboard rows, list order, the handoff notice's fallback) stripped `.html` with their own case-sensitive regex instead of `canvasLabel`'s rule, so `Checkout.HTML` and a bare `.html` read differently from the sidebar row; every path-only caller now uses `canvasStem` in `canvasTree.ts`, the one implementation `canvasLabel` calls. Search: `rg -n '\.html\$/|endsWith\("\.html"\)' app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'` should find only `canvasTree.ts`.

### 2026-11-05 — A Send now row refused back into the queue could vanish while main still held it

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/conversationStore.ts` (`markQueued`, `syncQueues`), main's queued count (`DraftStore.queued`, which leaves out `sending` items)
- **Symptom**: with one queued message in a running turn, Send now marked it `sending` in main, so the list read that followed said `queued: 0`. When the provider refused and the row came back, the next `syncQueues` (run on any HomeRun change) applied that stale read and dropped the row, with its "Couldn’t send now. It stays queued." line, while main still had the message queued and later sent it.
- **Root cause**: a queue's read mark (`after`) was set only when the message was queued. A read is trusted to drop rows once it started after that mark, but main's count is not monotonic for an item: it stops counting the item while Send now has it in flight and counts it again on a refusal. A read taken inside that window passed the mark and looked final.
- **Net**: caught by the SEND batch's own `/code-review` before integration (canvas-composer-polish), by reading what main counts. The existing `overviewRun.test.ts` case checked the row right after the refusal but never ran another sync with the old list, so it missed it. The bug was on main before that feature; moving the message into the thread only made the row's return more visible.
- **Prevention**: when main's count of something can drop and come back (an in-flight mark, a lease), move the read mark to `readsStarted(root)` at the moment it comes back, so only a read started afterwards can drop it. Regression: `conversationStore.test.ts` "a row refused back into the queue stays until a read started after the refusal says main sent it" (stale `queued: 0` read, refusal, extra sync, then a later read). Search: `rg -n 'readsStarted\(|readsApplied\(' app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'` and check each mark against what main's list leaves out. On 2026-11-05 the other marks (`canvasRun`/`overviewRun` `request.after`, the store's `taken` for returned drafts) guard states main does not stop and restart counting.

### 2026-11-05 — A server added by URL stayed Checking with Connect disabled until the page was reopened

- **Area**: `app/src/main/mcpConnectors/service.ts` (`add`, `refresh`); fixed in 69dc43fd on the mcp-connectors BF batch.
- **Symptom**: in the real app (E1), Settings > MCP Connectors > Add server by URL added the row to every agent section as `Checking` with Connect disabled. No status command ran for about 2.5 minutes; leaving the category and coming back showed `Not connected` at once.
- **Root cause**: a row with no read result and no read running derives `Checking` (design '상태 판정'), and statuses are read only at fixed points (page entry, attempt end, Retry, auth change) because they cost a network health check. Every other change that writes the connectors file runs inside an attempt (whose `end()` refreshes) or `logoutForAuth` (whose `finally` refreshes); `add` writes the file outside both, so nothing started the read the new row was waiting for.
- **Net**: caught by the person-run E1 probe. Missed by the service tests, which checked Add's reply and file contents and the reads only after explicit `refresh()` calls, and by the page tests, which fed a hand-made snapshot after Add rather than what main publishes.
- **Prevention**: any `McpService` path that adds a row, or changes what a row reads as, must start a read itself or run inside an attempt; the service test now asserts the read after Add in every signed-in provider. Search: `rg -n 'store\.(patch|record)\(' app/src/main/mcpConnectors/service.ts` and check that each hit sits in an attempt flow (refreshed by `end()`), in `logoutForAuth` (refreshed in `finally`) or is followed by `this.refresh(`. Run on 2026-11-05: the `everConnected` patches and the Remove patch are attempt flows, `record` is in `logoutForAuth`, and `add` now refreshes.

### 2026-11-03 — Export refused icons the app showed, set as url() in a custom property

- **Area**: `cli/internal/export/inline.go` (`replace`), `customurl.go`, `cli/internal/canvas/css.go` (`CSSRefs`, `CustomUses`); fixed in 62a466e1 on `fix/export-var-url`.
- **Symptom**: a design-system canvas set icons as `style="--sf-icon:url(assets/icons-mono/x.svg)"`, drawn by a shared `components.css` rule `mask: var(--sf-icon, …)`. The icons showed in the app, but the canvas row menu's Export… and `doan export` both refused them as not found (19 references in one canvas); an agent rewrote the paths to get the export through.
- **Root cause**: export resolved every `url()` from the file that holds it, which is the CSS rule for ordinary properties. Chromium resolves a relative `url()` in an unregistered custom property against the stylesheet whose `var()` substitutes it, so the base depends on a different file than the one being rewritten. Nothing in the rewrite pass knew which stylesheets use a property.
- **Net**: caught by a person exporting a real canvas. Missed by the export tests, whose seed declared a custom property `url()` only in the file that also defined its meaning and never consumed it from another folder, and by `doan check`, which does not verify local asset paths.
- **Prevention**: any code that resolves a CSS `url()` must ask whether the token sits in a custom property value (`canvas.CSSRef.Custom`) and, if so, resolve from its users (`uses.consumers`). Measure browser URL resolution in Electron with one page per layout (`.scratch/export-var-url/main.cjs` pattern): Chromium reuses the first resolution of identical `url()` text within a page, so a combined fixture page gives order-dependent results. Search: `rg -n 'resolveAsset\(|repo\.Resolve\(' cli/internal --glob '!*_test.go'` and check each caller that takes a CSS `url()`; on 2026-11-03 only export resolves `url()` tokens (check's `visit` follows stylesheets and `@import`s only).

### 2026-11-03 — Removing the loaded workspace's project left an empty workspace instead of going Home

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/App.tsx` (the effect that clears `loadedRoot`), `addProjectsNav.ts` (`workspaceRemoved`); fixed in 2ab94ada on `feat/project-remove-confirm`.
- **Symptom**: removing a project from the sidebar menu while one of its canvases (or its Overview) was open kept the window on the workspace screen with only the closed-canvas empty message; the person had to go Home by hand.
- **Root cause**: when the loaded root left the list, App cleared `loadedRoot` and left `screen` at `"workspace"`; the controller closed the open canvas. The pages (Add Projects, Settings, Project Settings) had a "the origin project is gone, go Home" rule (`addPageReturn`), but the workspace screen had no equivalent decision, and `onFolders` and `removeNow` cleaned up only memory (sidebar, history, conversations).
- **Net**: found by the user in the real app. Missed by the removal tests, which checked the destination only from the pages and from Home, never with the workspace on screen.
- **Prevention**: when adding a path that takes a root out of the list, pin in an App test where each screen lands (workspace, Overview, each page), not only the list. Search: `rg -n 'setLoadedRoot\(null\)|addPageReturn|workspaceRemoved' app/src/renderer`; run on 2026-11-03, the only `setLoadedRoot(null)` outside tests (`App.tsx:452`) is followed by the `workspaceRemoved` check.

### 2026-11-03 — A canvas row menu closed by Esc dropped focus to the page body

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/styles.css` (canvas row `.shell-more`), `ShellSidebar.tsx`, `App.tsx`; fixed in 82649b3b on `feat/canvas-row-menu` before merge.
- **Symptom**: closing a canvas row's menu with Esc, or choosing Export…, left focus on `<body>` instead of the row's menu button (canvas-row-menu spec: Esc returns focus to the button). Shift+Tab onto the idle button did the same.
- **Root cause**: the row button is revealed only on hover, `:focus-within` or while its menu is open, and the idle state used `display: none`. When the menu closes, `aria-expanded` turns false and focus is in the portal, not the row, so the button is `display: none` at the moment Radix calls `focus()` on its trigger; a box-less element cannot take focus and the call silently does nothing. App's own return after a modal (`focusCurrent`, which requires a box) failed the same way, and the batch had patched only that path with a `data-held` reveal.
- **Net**: caught by the independent review (RC2), which applied the built CSS to the row markup in Chromium. Missed by the batch's tests: they run in a DOM without the stylesheet, where every element has a box, so "focus returns to the trigger" passed.
- **Prevention**: an element that receives focus programmatically (a menu trigger, a dialog's return target) is hidden only visually (`position: absolute; width: 1px; height: 1px; overflow: hidden; clip-path: inset(50%)`), never with `display: none` or `visibility: hidden`; pin the hide rule in a CSS test. Search: `rg -n 'display:\s*none' app/src/renderer/styles.css app/src/renderer/ds/*.css` and check every hit that hides a trigger, a roving-tabindex item or a focus-return target. Run on 2026-11-03: no other hit hides a focus target.

### 2026-11-03 — Locate on the Project Settings page closed the page instead of following the moved worktree

- **Area**: `app/src/main/shell.ts` (`replace`), `app/src/renderer/App.tsx` (`onFolders`), `ProjectSettingsPage.tsx` (`followProjectSettings`); fixed in b26eb412 on `feat/project-settings` before merge.
- **Symptom**: in the real app a successful Locate on a Missing worktree's Project Settings page left the page for its origin; the page should have shown the new root (project-settings spec 완료 조건 5).
- **Root cause**: the renderer learns about a reroot only from the optional second argument of the folder-list event (`FolderFocus {from, root}`). Set Up's reconcile path (`shell.ts:124`) sent it; `replace()`, which Locate uses, sent only the list. Without the focus the old root simply vanished from the list, and the page's follow rule read that as "gone". The workspace Locate path did not need the event, because `folders:locate` returns the new root and App sets it directly, so nothing exposed the gap until a second consumer relied on the event.
- **Net**: caught by the batch C `/runtime-test` probe (43/44). Missed by the App test, which called `listeners.folders!(list, {from, root})` with a hand-made focus, and by `shell.test.ts`, which checked only the list after `replace`. A test that injects the event main is supposed to send cannot catch main not sending it.
- **Prevention**: when renderer behavior depends on an optional field of a main event, pin in main's test that every path producing that change sends it (`shell.test.ts` now asserts the focus after `replace`). Search run: `rg -n 'onChange\(entries\(\)' app/src/main/shell.ts` lists nine list-only calls; only `replace` (Locate) and the Set Up reconcile move a root, and both now send the focus. `rg -n 'listeners\.folders!\(.*from:' app/src/renderer/*.test.tsx` finds one injected focus (`App.settings.test.tsx:613`); it is now backed by the main test.

### 2026-11-02 — Evaluation unit checks inherited the real session-recording runtime

- **Area**: `eval/scenarios/{regression,audit}.test.ts`, `H1`–`H5` and `R6b` scenario tests.
- **Symptom**: a concurrent fake-agent self-test scored all 36 scenarios correctly but exited 1 when its real-state guard found the real Doan `sessions.jsonl` changed.
- **Root cause** (most likely, not confirmed): the real-run workspace isolated `DOAN_RUNTIME`, but older scenario unit fixtures passed `process.env` to `createContext`. When launched inside an agent session, their real CLI `check` calls could record associations beside the default runtime. A temporary repository alone does not isolate device-local session state. Other worker and conductor sessions writing the same real `sessions.jsonl` during the run would change the same fingerprint, and the passing rerun was also sequential, so the run does not tell the two apart.
- **Net**: the self-test real-state guard detected the change; existing scenario assertions passed because session recording is best effort and does not affect check output. A regression that observes the environment received by a context subprocess failed with the inherited runtime before the fixture environments were corrected; it pins the environment boundary, not a change to `sessions.jsonl`.
- **Prevention**: every eval fixture that runs the CLI must set an absolute temporary `DOAN_RUNTIME`, including direct init/fixture commands. Search executed: `rg -n 'env: process.env|execFileSync\(cli' eval --glob '*.test.ts'`. Remaining direct `process.env` cases run shell lifecycle tests, Python-only parser checks, a nonexistent CLI, or Git-only material generation; none invokes the real Doan CLI.

### 2026-11-02 — The sidebar list scrolled with the classic scrollbar

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/ShellSidebar.tsx` (`.shell-projects`), `ds/surfaces.css` (`.doan-scroll`).
- **Symptom**: in dogfooding, the sidebar's scrollbar looked unlike the panels'. In the built app the project list scrolled with Chromium's classic 15px bar and grey track; the panels use the thin, quiet one on a transparent track.
- **Root cause**: DESIGN 'Scrollbars' says every scroller takes `.doan-scroll`, but `.shell-projects` set `overflow: auto` on its own and never took the class; nothing checks that a scrolling container carries it.
- **Net**: no probe had a sidebar long enough to scroll. Fixed by adding `.doan-scroll` to both sidebar views; the built app now measures `scrollbar-width: thin` with the quiet thumb and an 11px bar (`.scratch/runtime-test/dogfood-repro/run-scroll2.log`).
- **Prevention**: an element that sets `overflow: auto` or `scroll` also takes `.doan-scroll` (or the same two declarations). Search: `rg -n "overflow(-y)?: auto" app/src/renderer/styles.css` and check each scroller's class.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-15, audit)**: Home's panel and both problem overlays (workspace and prototype window) set `overflow: auto` without `.doan-scroll`; each now takes the class, pinned by markup tests. The search above is still manual: check each hit's element in the TSX for `.doan-scroll`, `.doan-menu` or the two declarations, and whether a later rule turns its overflow off (`.ov` is `overflow: hidden` under `.workspace`).

### 2026-11-02 — The canvas conversation panel's head was 8px shorter than the canvas header

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/styles.css` (`.doan-conv-head`, `.right.ov-right .rp-head`), `ConversationPanel.tsx`.
- **Symptom**: in dogfooding, the canvas page's two headers did not line up: canvas header 56, conversation panel head 48 (measured in the built app).
- **Root cause**: the chrome height lived on `.rp-head`, the panel's default heading. A canvas panel with a conversation replaces that heading with the title menu, so the head fell back to the trigger's 32px plus its padding.
- **Net**: DESIGN recorded the 48 as measured, so the mismatch was documented rather than caught. Fixed by giving `.doan-conv-head` the chrome height; the built app now measures 56 for the canvas header, the panel head and both Overview heads.
- **Prevention**: put a row height on the container that always renders, not on a child a prop can replace. Search: `rg -n "min-height: var\(--size-chrome\)" app/src/renderer/styles.css`.

### 2026-11-02 — The selection-lost line never left the board

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/CanvasController.ts` (`clearSelection(true)` sets `state.notice`), `App.tsx` (where the notice was drawn), now `selection.ts` (`noticeToast`).
- **Symptom**: in dogfooding, "The selection is no longer in the canvas." stayed at the top of the board indefinitely and stacked with the agent notice there.
- **Root cause**: an event message lived in canvas state and was drawn as a state notice. The controller clears it only on the next selection, a rule that fits a value read by tests and `doan active` but not a message on screen; nothing timed it out.
- **Net**: the controller tests pinned "the notice until the next selection" as the intended state, and no probe stayed on the canvas long enough after a loss to see it remain. Now the app shows it once as the shared toast when it appears; the probe removed a selected frame from the file and saw the toast with no board notice (`.scratch/runtime-test/dogfood-repro/run-lost.log`).
- **Prevention**: a message that reports something that happened is a toast; a board notice is only for a condition that is still true (DESIGN 'Toast' and 'Notices'). Search: `rg -n "doan-notice" app/src/renderer/App.tsx` within the canvas column.

### 2026-11-02 — Tab walked into the draft, drew its focus rings and scrolled the floating tools away

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/CanvasController.ts` (board iframe creation), `app/src/renderer/styles.css` (`.doan-canvas-panel > .area`).
- **Symptom**: in dogfooding, pressing Tab on a canvas page moved the floating tools and showed focus rings on the canvas. A real-app probe reproduced both: Tab focused the draft's own buttons and links inside the board iframe, and focusing one 1,600 px down scrolled `section.area` by 778 px, so the tools row moved from y 838 to y 60.
- **Root cause**: the board iframe was in the Tab order, so the draft's controls — mock-ups, not the app's — took focus. `.area` was `overflow: hidden`, which hides scrollbars but still scrolls to reveal a focused descendant; the overlays positioned inside it scrolled with it. Pan and zoom are a transform, so nothing ever reset that scroll.
- **Net**: no test or probe had pressed Tab on a canvas page; DESIGN already noted the same mechanism for dialogs with `autofocus` in design-system canvases. Fixed with `tabindex=-1` on the iframe and `overflow: clip` on the area; the probe shows Tab skipping the canvas and the tools staying at y 838 (`.scratch/runtime-test/dogfood-repro/run-tab-fixed.log`).
- **Prevention**: a box that holds a transformed board and floating overlays clips; it never relies on `overflow: hidden`. A runtime probe of a canvas page presses Tab through it at least once with a focusable control below the fold. Search: `rg -n "overflow: hidden" app/src/renderer/styles.css` near positioned overlays.

### 2026-11-02 — The loaded project's row did nothing on a canvas, though PRODUCT said it opened the Overview

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/App.tsx` (`toggleProject`), now `shellNavigation.ts` (`loadedProjectRow`, `projectsTab`).
- **Symptom**: in dogfooding, "there is no way back to Home or the Overview". On a canvas, clicking the current project's sidebar row did nothing; the only routes were the row's "…" menu > Overview and Go > Home (⇧⌘H), neither visible.
- **Root cause**: the loaded-row branch handled two cases (another screen → enter; Overview shown → open the slot) and returned for the third (a canvas shown), so PRODUCT §5 item 3's "reachable from … the sidebar project row" held only from other screens.
- **Net**: no test exercised the loaded row while a canvas was shown; found by the user. The rule is now a pure function with each screen pinned in `shellNavigation.test.ts`; the real app was checked for all three routes (`.scratch/runtime-test/dogfood-repro/run-nav.log`).
- **Prevention**: when a navigation branch returns early, list every screen it can run on and pin each in a test. Search: `rg -n "currentScreen.current !==|shownOverviewRef.current ===" app/src/renderer/App.tsx`.

### 2026-11-02 — A picked model was forgotten: one start path skipped the memory and a fixed default overrode it

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/App.tsx` (HomeRun `host.sent`, the only Last used writer), `CanvasConversationPanel.tsx` (its own `startConversation`), `homeComposer.ts` (`defaultModel`/`defaultEffort` prefer a fixed default), now `rememberChoice.ts`.
- **Symptom**: in dogfooding, "model and effort are not remembered". A conversation started from a canvas panel never updated Last used, and with an agent saved in Settings > Agents 'New conversations' every new input started from that saved model, whatever was picked before.
- **Root cause**: Last used was written as a side effect of one start path (HomeRun's `host.sent`), not of the choice itself; the canvas panel later gained its own start call and nothing tied it to the memory. Separately, the fixed default wins over the remembered model by design, so a remembered pick was invisible for anyone with a fixed default.
- **Net**: no test covered "start from the canvas panel, then open a new input elsewhere". Found by the user, traced statically; the pure rules are pinned in `rememberChoice.test.ts`.
