Not verified in the real app: the isolated probe environment has no signed-in provider, so no model list to pick from.
- **Prevention**: record a remembered choice where the choice is made (the composer's resolved provider, model and effort), not inside one caller's success path. Any new `startConversation` or `sendConversation` caller must pass through `onRemember`. Search: `rg -n "startConversation\(|onRemember" app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'`.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-20, New conversations wording)**: Last used was already saved on selection, but the preference and its fallback explanation still described sends alone. Both now share the phrase about selections and sends, and a real-app probe picks Codex without sending and checks the saved choice after reload. Search: `rg -n "you sent with last|onRemember" app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'`.

### 2026-11-02 — Every Overview menu opened under the Overview itself

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/ds/surfaces.css` (`.doan-floating`), `app/src/renderer/styles.css` (`.ov`), every Radix `Portal` content on the Overview (`ProjectOverview.tsx` conversation and worktree menus, `ComposerParts.tsx` agent/model, effort and presets).
- **Symptom**: in dogfooding, the Overview's model and effort pickers "did not open". A real-app probe showed every portalled menu on the Overview — the conversation title menu included — open in the DOM (`data-state="open"`, opacity 1) but not painted. Home's identical pickers worked. On main since the Overview first gained portalled menus.
- **Root cause**: Radix renders a portalled menu inside a `position: fixed` wrapper at the end of `body` and copies the content's computed `z-index` onto it. No `.doan-floating` rule set one, so the wrapper sat in the root stacking context at level 0, under `.ov { position: relative; z-index: 1 }` (which keeps the Overview over the canvas panel in the same grid cell). Home has no z-index (`isolation: isolate` only), so it never showed.
- **Net**: missed by unit tests (jsdom does not paint) and by the Overview's `/design-sync`, which measured the closed screen; DESIGN 'Project Overview' even notes the composer menus were not measured. A first probe also reported the menu "visible": Radix sets `pointer-events: none` on `body` while a modal menu is open, so `elementFromPoint` skips `.ov` and returns the menu beneath it. Only the screenshot showed the truth. Fixed in `6ddc81db` (`[data-radix-popper-content-wrapper] > .doan-floating { z-index: 50 }`); `ds/controls.test.ts` checks the layer stays above `.ov`'s.
- **Prevention**: give any new stacking layer (a positioned element with a z-index) a check against portalled menus, and open at least one portalled menu on that screen in its runtime probe, judged by screenshot. In a probe, never take `elementFromPoint` under an open Radix modal as proof of visibility: restore `body`'s `pointer-events` first or compare screenshots. Search run: `rg -n 'z-index' app/src/renderer/styles.css app/src/renderer/ds` lists the layers; all except `.ov` are inside a component, below 50, or the resize cover (1000, shown only while dragging).

### 2026-11-01 — A canvas of absolutely positioned frames resized its iframe between 0 and 405 px forever

- **Area**: `app/src/main/canvasScript.ts` (`documentHeight`, `frameBottom`, `frames()`, `send`), with `nextHeight` and `CanvasController.ts` (`onLayout` remeasure) in the loop.
- **Symptom**: a canvas whose frames were all `position: absolute` never settled: the board iframe's height alternated 0 and 405 px, about 180 resizes in 3 s in the real app (pre-fix production build, BH probe). Found by review RBR5 with the real functions (`[0,405,0,405,...]`); present on main.
- **Root cause**: the height came from two numbers that both depend on the viewport we set. html's rect ignores out-of-flow content (0), and `scrollHeight` never drops below the viewport, so once the iframe was at least as tall as the frames it read as the viewport. `documentHeight` took scrollHeight only when the rect was within 1 px of the viewport: at a 405 px iframe it returned the rect (0), at 0 px the rect matched the viewport and it returned scrollHeight (405). `nextHeight` deliberately does not freeze after a sent 0 (2026-10-26 entry), and every non-confirming layout pulls a `measure` after 50 ms, so the alternation never ended. The fix bounds the height below by the frames' bottom, each frame ended at the smallest bottom among its eligible overflow-clipping ancestors (`frameBottom`: only boxes overflow applies to, following the containing-block escape of absolute and fixed elements; body clips only when html's overflow is not visible). Computed `overflow` alone does not say a box clips: an inline span or a `display: contents` wrapper still computes `overflow: hidden`, and reading it as a clip cut an absolute 405 px frame in an inline relative span to its 20 px line (review RBH2, then the BH3 probe on `40fb37bb`). A first version capped the raw bottoms at scrollHeight instead; because scrollHeight never drops below the viewport, that cap made the viewport a floor: a 100 px `overflow: auto` box holding a 3,000 px frame stayed at 150 px and a box shrunk from 600 to 100 px kept the iframe at 600 (review RBH, then the BH2 probe on `f84137be`). The cap remains only for clipping `frameBottom` does not model (`contain: paint`, `clip-path`, transformed containing blocks), where it can still hold the iframe at its current height.
- **Net**: missed by the unit tests, whose `documentHeight` cases were all in-flow content or `height: 100%` documents, and by every runtime check, whose fixtures used flow frames. Caught by a reviewer feeding the real functions an absolute-only measurement in a loop (fixed point check), not by a single-step assertion.
- **Prevention**: for any value the canvas measures against a size the renderer then sets from it, test the loop to its fixed point (measure → send → resize → measure), not one step, and include out-of-flow content; `canvasScript.test.ts` "documentHeight with only absolutely positioned frames" runs six rounds from 150 and from 0, and "documentHeight with a frame inside a box that scrolls it" runs a short box and a shrunk box. A bound taken from `scrollHeight` is never evidence of content below the viewport; do not use it as a floor. Runtime fixtures for height changes should include an absolute-only canvas, a body scroller, a short nested scroller and a box shrunk in place (`.scratch/runtime-test/buzz-canvas/BH/probe.mjs` in the BH worktree). Search run: `rg -n 'scrollHeight|getBoundingClientRect\(\)\.height|documentHeight\(' app/src/main/canvasScript.ts app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'` finds only `documentHeight` and its call in `send` as a measurement fed back into a size; the other hits (wheel `canScrollAxis`, `DockedComposer` line count, `ConversationThread` scrolling) do not resize what they measure.

### 2026-11-01 — The canvas agent indicator kept another worktree's conversation and its Stop

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/agentIndicator.ts` (`workingCanvas`, `canvasAgent`), `app/src/renderer/agentPresence.ts` (retention on a failed read), `app/src/renderer/CanvasAgent.tsx`, `app/src/renderer/App.tsx` (`CanvasPane` `agent` prop).
- **Symptom**: with project A's conversation running on canvas `billing`, opening the same canvas in another worktree or project B while B's conversation list was still loading or had failed kept showing A's notice. Its Stop sent A's conversationId to `interruptConversation`, stopping A's turn from B; a first failed read in B kept it until B's next successful read. Caught by review before integration (buzz-canvas RBR5); never on main.
- **Root cause**: the retained presence was keyed by canvas identity only (`sameCanvas`: id, else path), and the canvas the indicator received had dropped its root. Worktrees of one repository share canvas ids and paths by construction, so "same canvas" without the root matches across them. The direct navigation path publishes the new canvas and the new root in one render, so no intermediate null canvas cleared the kept value, and `CanvasAgent` is the same component instance across the switch.
- **Net**: caught by the independent review (RBR5), which ran the real pure modules through an A → B switch with B's list failed (`.scratch/review-RBR5-evidence/reproduce.log`); red on the BR5 API (`.scratch/BR5b/red-old-api.log`), fixed in `d32f4bdd` (the target carries the root; a kept presence survives only within it), with A → B tests for loading and failed lists, matching ids and id-less paths. Missed by BR5's own tests and runtime probe: every case and the probe used a single project, and agentPresence's retention tests compared canvases only.
- **Prevention**: any state kept across a failed or pending read about a canvas carries the project root in its identity, and tests switch between two roots that share a canvas id and path. Grep: `rg -n "sameCanvas\(" app/src/renderer --glob '!*.test.*'` (on 2026-11-01: `canvasConversationRows.ts`, `ProjectOverview.tsx` and `App.tsx:459` compare within one root's list; `activity.ts` and `CommentsSession.ts` compare with the root; `agentPresence.ts:22` retains by canvas only and is safe only because its one caller, `canvasAgent`, discards a previous value from another root — a new caller must do the same).

### 2026-11-01 — A ui-state save sent before the startup read could replace every remembered field

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/App.tsx` (`save`, `savedUiState`, the startup `readUiState` effect), `app/src/main/uiState.ts` (`save`, `flushSync`), `app/src/renderer/App.conversationPanel.test.tsx`.
- **Symptom**: with the startup ui-state read still pending, a successful Home send (HomeRun `host.sent` makes Home memory non-null) and any navigation (which cancels the restore and sets it `done`) let App send a full ui-state save built from the run's own state. Main replaces the whole file with it, so a quit before the read settled flushed it to disk and the next start lost what was remembered: `expanded`, `shellProjects`, `lastByRoot` (only the current canvas kept), the Home models of other providers, `conversationByRoot`, the saved `settingsSection` (the default category instead) and, with buzz-canvas BR4, `rightPanel`. Found as a renderer-callback reproduction in the review harness; not reproduced with a real slow read or a real quit.
- **Root cause**: the save gate checked proxies (`restore === "done"`, Home memory non-null) instead of "the initial read has settled", and both proxies turn true early through an unrelated path (a Home send cancels the restore and writes Home memory). Main's store has replace semantics, so the BR4 attempt to protect one field by leaving it out of early saves (`rightPanel: null`) protected nothing: an absent field is a deletion, not "unchanged". The same exposure was on main before BR4 (`origin/main` `14ec5b0b` gates `save` on `restore !== "done"` and `home === null` only); BR4 added `rightPanel` to the fields it could drop.
- **Net**: caught by the independent review of buzz-canvas BR4 (RBR4), whose harness test left `readUiState` pending, called HomeRun's `host.sent` and entered a workspace; red at `bca075a1`, green after `3cc8fb3c`, which sends no save until the read settles (a failed read counts) and then saves the merged state once. Missed by BR4's own tests, which only asserted that `savedUiState` left the field out and never held the read open while another path made a save possible. A related gap stays open: when a navigation cancels the startup restore, the read's `expanded`, `shellProjects` and `lastByRoot` are never applied, so the next save after the read still replaces them (on main too; `restoreCancelled` skips the restore effect that loads them).
- **Prevention**: a client of a replace-semantics store sends nothing until its first read has settled, and merges what it read before the first save; never protect a field by omitting it. Pin the rule with a test that holds the read open and drives every early path that can make a save possible (a send, a navigation, a pan via `viewChanged`), asserting no save and then the merged first save. Grep: `rg -n "saveUiState|savedUiState\(|readUiState\(\)" app/src --glob '!*.test.*'` (on 2026-11-01: one renderer save site, `save()` in `App.tsx`, which pans also reach through `viewChanged`, and one read; no other renderer-fed store replaces a whole file — preferences save patches).

### 2026-11-01 — A frame drawer list closed by a disabled trigger reopened by itself

- **Area**: `app/src/renderer/CanvasHeader.tsx` (`FrameDrawer`, `ZoomControl`, `useMenuFocus`), `app/src/renderer/canvasFocus.ts` (`menuCloseNow`).
- **Symptom**: with the frame drawer's list open, the canvas lost its board width (the drawer turned disabled, "Frames") and the list disappeared. When the width came back on the same canvas, the list popped open again with nobody touching it. Seen in the buzz-canvas BR1 runtime probe before merge, not on main.
- **Root cause**: the menus were shown as `open && view.enabled`, a derived value, while the person's `open` state stayed `true` underneath. Hiding a popup by masking its state with an availability flag keeps the stale intent, so the popup returns as soon as the flag does. Static markup tests render the closed trigger only and never run an open → disabled → enabled sequence.
- **Net**: caught by the BR1 `/code-review` reading the open-state logic, then pinned by a runtime probe step (width removed and restored with the list open): red on the pre-fix build (`.scratch/runtime-test/buzz-canvas/BR1/run6-red.log`, list `open: true` after recovery), green after `77b66249` (`run7.log`). Missed by the implementation's unit and markup tests.
- **Prevention**: a popup whose trigger becomes disabled closes its own state (`menuCloseNow` returns `close` for an open, disabled menu; `canvasFocus.test.ts`), and focus goes to the canvas region. Do not express "unavailable" as `open && enabled` alone. Keep the probe's restore step. Grep: `grep -rnE 'open=\{[a-zA-Z.]+ && |const shown = open && ' app/src/renderer --include='*.tsx'` (on 2026-11-01 it finds only the two canvas header menus, both now closing through `menuCloseNow`).
- **Recurrence (2026-11-15, audit)**: the prototype window's frame name tooltip was `open={cut && tip}`, so a hover kept from before reopened it with nobody pointing once the name was cut short again; the tooltip state now closes when the name fits and opens only while cut, pinned by `FrameMenu.interaction.test.tsx`. The same search still finds `CanvasHeader.tsx` (closes through `menuCloseNow`) and `UpdateHeaderButton.tsx` (`open && shown !== null`, not examined here).

### 2026-11-01 — A failed Antigravity sign-in left no evidence of why

- **Area**: `app/src/main/agentAuth/manager.ts` (`acpSignIn`, `acpStatus`, `acpExec`, `ServerError`, `serverEvent`).
- **Symptom**: in a dev app's first-run Agent step, Antigravity sign-in failed twice in a row ("Sign-in to Antigravity keeps failing") while four other providers signed in. The profile afterwards held `settings.json` (written only after a successful `authenticate`) but no `acp_token.json`, no vault image and no metadata entry; the dev log held no `doan:` line about it. Two later real sign-ins with the same Google account, in copies of that profile through the window API and through the same UI, and a retry in the original profile all succeeded, so the cause was not found.
- **Root cause**: diagnosis was impossible, not the failure itself: a failed ACP sign-in or status check kept its reason only in the in-memory `Failure` detail, which never reaches a window or the log, and `acpExec` discarded the server's stderr. The file evidence also misled: on its identity/onboarding rejection path (a `ValueError` from `get_access_token`, handled by `_sign_out_after_identity_failure`: onboarding failure, its 300 s login timeout, denied consent) the 1.3.0 server deletes `acp_token.json` itself and keeps `settings.json`, so a missing token does not show that Doan lost it. Hypothesis, not reproduced: its loopback listener answers exactly one request, so a second visit to the link (a second tab, "Open again") could end the flow.
- **Net**: reported by the user; the profile's file times were the only evidence, and they fit no single Doan code path.
- **Prevention**: a failed ACP sign-in, a status check that ends without a login, and a failed server logout now log in Doan's own categories only: how the exchange ended and the recognized auth-path server events, reworded; the RPC error text, payloads and every other server line are withheld (tests in `manager.test.ts` 'ACP sign-in diagnostics'; a first version that printed filtered server text leaked RPC `data.message` and structured stderr, review RBG). A status check stopped by its time limit says so. Never log a provider's message text, even filtered: provider errors embed whole replies. When a provider login fails, read that log before reading profile files, and read the provider's own source for what deletes its credentials before blaming Doan's scrub. Other providers' login failures still keep their output only in `Failure` detail. Grep: `grep -n 'console.error' app/src/main/agentAuth/manager.ts`.
- **Follow-up (2026-11-01, cause found)**: the user's machine was under heavy load (load average ~117 on 8 cores). Reproduced in a copy of the profile at a load of ~120-190: the server took about 30 s to answer `initialize`, the approved sign-in's `authenticate` returned `{}`, and the status check that follows it counted the server's start in its 20 s limit, was stopped before the server printed anything (`status check failed: the server was stopped after 20000 ms without an answer`), and the failed sign-in scrubbed the new token, leaving the reported file state. At ~270 a first attempt failed earlier, in `initialize`'s own 60 s limit. Fix: `acpExec` sends and checks `initialize` itself, bounded by `ACP_OPEN_MS` (60 s, as for sign-in and conversation runs), and starts the caller's limit at its answer; under a load of ~227 a status server then lived 36 s and the sign-in succeeded. Prevention: a time limit around a provider process must not include the process's start unless the start is what it bounds; measure a provider's start under load (`uptime` plus spawn-to-first-answer) before choosing a limit. Grep: `grep -n 'Timeout\|_MS = ' app/src/main/agentAuth/manager.ts app/src/main/conversation/acpRunner.ts`.
- **Recurrence (2026-11-20, MCP Connect)**: login failure diagnostics accepted free-form text. Restrict them to the failure code and the process exit code when available. Assert the complete log entry alongside the UI failure classification. Search with `rg -n 'console\.error\(.*MCP connect' app/src/main -g '!*.test.ts'`.
